# Parity gaps

## Client workspace — New Project modal built, "Campaigns" stat approximated

Built: `components/project/NewProjectModal.tsx`, entry point on the client
workspace page (`app/(app)/clients/[id]/page.tsx`, `+ New Project`,
staff-only via `useIsStaff`, matching `projects_insert`'s staff-only RLS),
against the prototype's `#newScrim`/`#newProjBtn2` (New Project — a
different scrim from `#briefScrim`/New Brief). Creates a real `projects`
row (`name`, `type`, `delivery`) via `use-create-project.ts`;
`projects_set_agency_id` derives `agency_id` from `client_id`, same
verified-empirically shape as `use-create-creative.ts`. The page itself
gained the stats row, search, filter chips (All/Needs Review/Approved) and
sort select the prototype's `#v-projects` has, none of which existed on
this page before.

**"Campaigns" stat approximated, not faithfully ported.** The prototype's
`projStats()` computes it from distinct campaign tags (`e.c`) across each
project's schedule/work items — a concept this schema has no column or
table for anywhere (no `campaign` field on `creatives`, no separate
campaigns table). Rather than invent one or drop the stat card, this ships
counting distinct non-null `projects.type` values among the client's active
projects instead — a real, schema-backed number, but not the same metric
as the prototype's. Flagging this as a guess: if "Campaigns" is meant to
track something like connected ad-platform campaigns, that's a real
feature (likely needing an ads-platform integration) this pass hasn't
scoped, not a naming difference to quietly reconcile.

## New Client modal — approver email captured, invite not sent

Built: `components/clients/NewClientModal.tsx`, entry point on the dashboard
(`+ New Client`, staff-only via `useIsStaff`, matching `clients_insert`'s
own staff-only RLS), against the prototype's `#clientScrim`/`#newClientBtn`.
Creates a real `clients` row (`name`, `industry`) via `use-create-client.ts`.

**Primary Approver Email captured in the UI but not wired to a real
invite.** Read the prototype's own `#ncSave` handler
(`frank-prototype.html` ~line 7365) before assuming otherwise: it never
sends anything either — it just appends "— invite sent to X" to a toast
with no backend behind it. A real invite means creating an auth user (or a
pending-invite record keyed on email alone, which `memberships` doesn't
support — `user_id` is `not null`), then a `memberships` row scoping them
to this client, then actually delivering an email — none of which exists
anywhere in this codebase yet. `Settings → Team` doesn't have it either:
`hooks/use-team-members.ts` is read-only, and there's no "Invite Team
Member" mutation anywhere despite the prototype having that modal too. This
isn't a New Client–specific gap, it's the same not-yet-built invite
infrastructure the whole app is missing — noted here rather than
approximated with a fake membership row (no real user to attach it to) or
skipped silently.

**"N of 10 clients used" footer text ported as display copy, not
enforced.** The prototype hardcodes a 10-client cap tied to a "Growth
plan" and blocks creation past it (`if(active>=10)`); the live `agencies`
table has `plan`/`seat_limit` columns but `seat_limit` is used elsewhere
for team seats, not a client cap, and there's no column modeling a
per-plan client limit. Showing the count without enforcing a limit that
isn't actually modeled seemed better than blocking real usage on an
invented rule — revisit if/when a real plan-based client cap is decided.

Screens or controls where the prototype does something the live app doesn't, and closing the gap needs more than a CSS/DOM fix — a schema change, a new feature, or a decision this pass isn't positioned to make on its own. See the `prototype-parity` skill for the escalation rule this file exists to serve.

## Upload/Edit modal (established prior to this file's creation)

Scoped out on the grounds that each would require a schema or feature decision, not a styling one:

- **"Which Creative" selector** — the app's modal is always invoked from one specific creative already; the prototype's selector exists because its modal can be opened generically.
- **Content Type / Format selects** — editable in the prototype; changing a creative's format after creation isn't a decision this pass can make.
- **AI vision-caption field** ("What the Artwork Looks Like") — unbuilt AI feature.
- **WIIFM Note input** — would duplicate or conflict with the Brief panel's existing `approach_notes` field; a data-modeling decision.
- **Dual "Save as Coming Soon" / "Send for Review" footer buttons** — needs a stage-transition/visibility concept that doesn't exist in the schema. **No longer theoretical** — this is the confirmed cause of a real bug report; see "Stage transitions" below.

## ShareModal's silent failure — RESOLVED

Confirmed symptom, not a theoretical gap: a real bug report ("creatives uploaded via the live site don't display through a shared-review link") turned out to have nothing to do with upload, storage, or live vs. local — every asset checked (both a throwaway repro and the actual production data) had a real, downloadable Storage object exactly matching its DB row. The cause was `shared_link_allowed_creative_ids()`'s unconditional `stage >= 5` rule (supabase/seed.sql) silently excluding creatives still in the internal band (1–4) — which is *every* creative New Brief creates, since nothing anywhere in the app ever advances a creative's stage (confirmed: no stage-updating mutation exists in any hook, component, or page). ShareModal created the link successfully regardless and handed back a working URL, with no indication anything would be missing from it.

**Fixed**: `lib/shared-link-eligibility.ts` mirrors that RPC's rule client-side (unit-tested against all four scopes) and `ShareModal.tsx` previews it live as scope/picks change — before the link is created, not blocking it. Zero eligible creatives gets a plain `.note.warn` explaining they're still in Concept, Copy, Design or Internal QC; a partial exclusion gets a calmer count ("N of M won't show"). There are legitimate reasons to create a link ahead of the work being ready, so creation is never blocked, only informed.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/share-eligibility.spec.ts`, zero-eligible and partially-eligible cases. Fixed two more fixture-teardown gaps this surfaced along the way (same class as `format_directions` below): `shared_links` created through the real UI, not `frank.createSharedLink()`, weren't covered by the token-tracking cleanup, and `creatives` created via a new `createCreativeAtStage()` fixture helper needed the same. Both now sweep by `agency_id` like the other tables.

## Stage transitions — RESOLVED (minimal), rest still deferred

**Built**: `supabase/migrations/phase9_advance_creative_stage.sql` — staff can move a creative from the internal band (stage < 5) to Client Review, and back to Internal QC (stage 4). Nothing else; exception-setting, approval attribution and an audit table all stay deferred, exactly as scoped below. `advance_creative_stage(p_creative_id, p_direction)` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path` pinned to `public` (no `extensions` needed — no `crypt()`/pgcrypto call here, unlike the guest-path functions `phase7_fix_pgcrypto_search_path.sql` had to widen). Entry point is a button on the creative review page itself (`+ New Brief`'s sibling toolbar), gated on the same `isStaff` (real membership) pattern used everywhere else in this app.

Also resolved along the way: `creatives_update`'s RLS, previously broad enough that a client-role session could write *any* column on a creative it could read — narrowed to staff-only. The scoping below already flagged this as broader than anything the UI exposed; closing it was a precondition for building the transition at all, not a separate decision. A future client-permitted transition (approving, say) should get its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function rather than widening this policy back out — `advance_creative_stage` is the template.

Verified empirically against real authenticated sessions, both roles, before any UI was written: a client's direct table write on `stage` is silently blocked (0 rows, no error — ordinary RLS semantics); a client's call to `advance_creative_stage` is explicitly rejected (`not permitted`); staff's calls succeed and the legal-move guards hold (`already at or past Client Review` / `only a Client Review creative can move back to internal` on the wrong-direction case); staff's direct table write on `stage` still succeeds too, confirming `creatives_update` isn't locked to the RPC path, only to staff. Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/stage-transition.spec.ts` — a staff advance making a creative visible through a real shared link, a client having no entry point and being rejected at the RPC layer directly (not just UI absence), and ShareModal's eligibility warning (see above) clearing once the move happens.

The rest of this entry is the original scoping this was built against — still accurate for what's still deferred (exception, audit, approval attribution), against the prototype's intent and this schema's actual constraints:

**What the prototype intends** (`frank-prototype.html`): fewer real transitions than the UI suggests, and — tellingly — the prototype's own script doesn't fully implement all of them either.

- **Artwork lands on a brief with none** (`$("#upSend").onclick`): bumps stage to at least 3 (Design) — *not* straight to Client Review. Uploading isn't "ready for the client," just "past Concept/Copy."
- **"Save as Coming Soon"** (`$("#upDraft").onclick`): explicitly touches nothing — its own toast says "not visible to the client."
- **Approve** (`doApprove`, wired to `#approve`): gated on unresolved comments (confirms first if any exist); the button's own label switches between "Approve" (client mode) and "Approve on Behalf" (agency mode) — **both roles can approve**, staff's version is explicitly framed as recording an approval given some other way (phone, email), not a separate staff privilege.
- **Request Revision** (`#reqRev`/`#revGo`): shown to both roles unconditionally, but in the prototype's own script it only posts a comment — it never sets `exception` on the underlying record. Every `changes_requested`/`rejected` value in the prototype only ever appears in its static seed data; there is no runtime path anywhere in the script that produces one. Whatever puts a creative into an exception state is not demonstrated by the prototype at all — it's outside what can be read off it, not merely unbuilt here.
- **Unpublish** (`#unpubBtn`): published → approved, staff-only (hidden from client mode), requires typing a reason — logged only as a toast message in the prototype, not persisted anywhere structurally.
- **Revoking a stale approval** (`revokeApproval`): automatic, not a button — approved/scheduled → Client Review, fired when copy changes after sign-off or a suggested edit is accepted. The prototype's own comment states the reasoning plainly: *"Signing off then changing the work silently would leave an approval nobody gave."* A real backward transition, but a side effect of an edit, not a direct user action.

**What this schema supports today:**

- *Legal transitions per constraint*: `creatives_stage_range` only checks `stage between 1 and 8` — no ordering is enforced at all; jumping from 1 straight to 8 is not blocked by any constraint. `creatives_exception_stage` requires `exception is null or stage between 1 and 5` — so approving (stage 6+) must clear any exception first, matching every `delete s.exc` alongside every `stage=6`/`stage=5` write in the prototype's own script. Sequencing beyond that (must pass through Internal QC before Client Review, say) isn't a schema rule anywhere — it would need a new trigger or be enforced only in application code, and that choice is a product decision, not something derivable from what exists.
- *Who can make them, under the existing RLS pattern*: **already broader than the UI exposes, today, with nothing built.** `creatives_update`'s `USING` clause is `is_agency_staff(agency_id) OR (the client owns this creative's project)` — a real client-role session can already write to *any* column on a creative it can see, including `stage` and `exception`, via a direct API call, not just the columns a future approve/reject UI would touch. This isn't a gap a stage-transition feature would introduce; it already exists, inherited by anything built on top of a plain `.update()` the way every other mutation in this app is written (`useUpdateBrief`, `useSaveSlideText`, `useToggleCommentResolved` — all plain table writes, no RPC, matching the app's own established convention). Matches the prototype's intent that both roles can approve — but means nothing currently stops a client from also rewriting a creative's `name` or `concept` through that same door. Whether stage/exception writes should go through a narrower RPC instead (this schema already has that pattern for the guest path — `get_shared_review`/`submit_shared_approval`/`submit_shared_comment`, all `SECURITY DEFINER`) rather than a raw table write is a real architecture decision, not free — every other authenticated mutation in this app uses the plain-table-write convention, and departing from it here would be a first.
- *Backward transitions*: the prototype allows them (`revokeApproval`, `unpubBtn`) and the schema doesn't forbid them — no constraint or trigger cares about direction, only about the exception/stage-range pairing.
- *Audit*: the schema already flags this as a known gap in its own words — the comment above `creatives.approved_at` says outright: "there's no status_events audit table yet (deferred in Phase 1), so this is the record until that exists." That table doesn't exist for *any* transition, not just approval. Whether "who moved what, when" needs recording for stage/exception changes generally is a product decision (how much history matters vs. the schema's existing minimal footprint); *that* a real audit table is already the deferred, intended answer once it does is not — it's already written down.

**Plainly, schema vs. product:**

- Schema decisions already made and not in question: exception requires stage ≤ 5; approving clears exception; nothing enforces stage ordering.
- Schema decisions still open: whether *exception* writes (as opposed to the stage transition already built above) should move to a narrower RPC too, or reuse `creatives_update` now that it's staff-only; whether `approved_by_name/email` (denormalized for the guest case) is the right shape for an authenticated approval, or whether that needs a real `user_id` reference instead; whether a `status_events` table (already deferred, not newly proposed) lands now or later.
- Product decisions, not derivable from either the prototype or the schema: what actually produces `changes_requested`/`rejected` (the prototype never shows a working path to either); whether Request Revision should set an exception at all, or stay comment-only as the prototype's own script actually does; what "Send for Review" should mean as a manual action distinct from the automatic stage-3 bump on first upload; whether an Unpublish reason needs real structured storage rather than a toast.

## Silent-success writes and unmatched role gates — RESOLVED

Found while checking whether the deployed build (predating `phase9`) had any code writing to `creatives` in a way the newly-tightened `creatives_update` would now block, before pushing. It did — and the same audit, done properly, turned up more.

**Every mutation hook doing `.update()`/`.delete()`/`.upsert()` audited** for the pattern `useUpdateAgencyBranding` already guards against: no `.select()` after the write means RLS's ordinary "zero rows matched, zero rows changed, no error" response reads as success. Five hooks, six functions had it: `useUpdateBrief` and `useUpdateCreativeCx` (both reachable by a real client session, no UI gate at all — the actual regression risk), `useToggleCommentResolved` (UI already gates the button correctly; the hook itself had no defense if reached another way), and `useUpdateKnowledgeEntry`/`useDeleteKnowledgeEntry`/`useUpsertFormatDirection` (the first two reachable by a client with zero gating — `knowledge_entries`' write policies were staff-only before this session touched anything, the UI just never matched; the third not currently reachable, Settings already redirects non-staff, fixed anyway for consistency). All six now `.select().maybeSingle()` and throw when the row comes back null, matching the existing correct pattern exactly.

**Checked intent before touching anything** rather than assuming the RLS tightening was the problem: the prototype's Brief panel computes `canEdit = MODE!=="client" && !isLive()`, and the only way into edit mode — the pencil icon — only renders when `canEdit` is true. A client gets no edit affordance at all, not a disabled one. The project table's cell editing is gated the identical way (`if(cell&&MODE!=="client"){...startEdit...}`). On the app's own creative-review page, everything else was already `isStaff`-gated (Upload/Edit, New Brief, the stage-transition buttons) — `<BriefPanel>` was the one thing rendered with no wrapper at all. Conclusion: oversight, not intent, in every case. `creatives_update` staying staff-only was correct; the fix was UI gates, not a narrower policy anywhere.

**Fixed**: `BriefPanel` (concept/reference-link/approach-notes/text-on-image), `CxCell` (project-table custom fields) and `KnowledgeSection` (create/edit/remove) all now take `isStaff` and render read-only/hidden for a client session — disabled inputs and no Save button for `BriefPanel`, disabled inputs and no "+ Add column" for the project table, no "+ Add note"/Edit/Remove for Knowledge. `ListEditor` and `CxCell` both gained a `readOnly` prop to support this without duplicating either component. Verified empirically against real staff and client sessions (client: every control disabled or absent; staff: everything exactly as before, no change to the existing experience) before pushing.

## New Brief — RESOLVED, Copy Options still scoped out of v1

**Built**: `components/project/NewBriefModal.tsx`, entry point on the project view (`+ New Brief`, staff-only via `useIsStaff`), against the prototype's `#briefScrim`/`openBriefModal`/`bwSave` (New Brief creates a creative; separately, `renderBrief`/`saveBrief` edit an existing one — the app's `BriefPanel.tsx` already corresponds to the latter). This is the first code path in the app that creates a `creatives` row — verified empirically before any UI was written (agency_id derives correctly from the `creatives_set_agency_id` trigger with none set in the payload, stage defaults to 1, a position computed from the project's current max lands where expected, and a real client-role session is rejected outright by RLS). Content Type/Format is a real cascade over `lib/formats.ts`'s catalog, storing the key. Scheduled/continuous fields are conditionally required client-side (Publish Date, Destination) with a field-level error before submit, so the schema's `check_creative_delivery_fields` trigger — confirmed by the same empirical pass to reject a missing `scheduled_at`/`destination` outright — is a backstop, not the first thing a user meets.

Below is the original audit this was built against. Ten of the thirteen fields the modal captures map onto columns or proven versioning patterns that already existed (`creatives.name/concept/reference_url/approach_notes/lead_user_id/scheduled_at/destination/due_on/format`, `copy_versions.slide_text`, `creatives.cx`). **Copy Options (`bwCopies`, "+ Add Option") did not**, and remains the one field that needs an actual decision, not a schema change derivable from what's there — New Brief ships with a single caption into `copy_versions.fields.caption` instead (via the same insert `useSaveSlideText`/`useSaveCopyFields` already use), and no "options" concept.

The prototype's own comment calls it out as distinct from Text on Image: both "create a copy version," but Copy Options is a *list of candidate captions* offered at brief time for a copywriter to draft from — not the final content of any one version. `copy_versions.fields` (jsonb: caption/headline/cta/subject_line/preview_text/alt_text) models the opposite shape: **one version's settled content**, confirmed by how `useSaveSlideText` already uses the table — every edit inserts a new row that carries the *existing* `fields` forward untouched, changing only what that edit was for. There's no column or table anywhere for "here are three options, pick one."

Open product questions this needs answered, not derived:

- **Do options persist after one is picked?** Does choosing a caption promote it into `copy_versions.fields.caption` and the rest simply vanish, or do all candidates stay on record against the creative/version?
- **Who chooses?** The brief-writer proposes them — does the copywriter pick, does a client ever see the options, or is picking itself a staff-only action regardless of who briefed it?
- **Are rejected options kept?** If they're discarded, "Copy Options" is really just brief-writer scratch space with no lasting data shape at all — closer to a text field than a first-class list. If they're kept, that's a real new column or table, and a decision about who can read it later (comment visibility's private/public split doesn't obviously apply, but it's the closest precedent in this schema).

Building Copy Options without answering these would have meant guessing at a data shape for a concept the prototype itself doesn't fully commit to — left for a later pass once the questions above have real answers.

## `format_directions` has no delete policy at all

Found while testing New Brief's format catalog (`lib/formats.ts`) against `Settings → Knowledge`: `format_directions` has `_select`/`_insert`/`_update` RLS policies (`supabase/seed.sql`) but no `_delete` policy whatsoever — not staff-only, not admin-only, just absent. Under RLS's default-deny model that means **nobody can remove a format direction through the app, staff or admin**, once one exists. Confirmed by reading the policy list, not assumed.

This doesn't look like a deliberate scoping decision — every other place in this schema that withholds a capability says so directly (e.g. `agency_settings`'s comment explicitly says "write is admin-only"; the memberships/clients tables spell out who can do what for every operation). `format_directions` has no such comment next to the missing delete policy, which reads more like an oversight than an intentional "deletion isn't a feature here" choice.

Whether it should be one: `AddFormatForm` now selects from a fixed 41-entry catalog rather than free text, which makes mis-adding an entry (wrong format, added twice by mistake) more likely to actually happen in practice, not less — the whole catalog is one click away in a dropdown. Not being able to remove one afterward, with no workaround short of a direct database edit, looks like a real gap rather than a boundary anyone decided on purpose. Recommend adding a delete policy (staff or admin-only, matching `_update`'s reach) as a small follow-up — not done here, since this pass's job was to note the gap, not decide it.

## `.review` grid — leaving the drift in place

The base `.review` rule is confirmed Drifted (see `docs/css-coverage.md`): the prototype is `grid-template-columns:0 minmax(0,1fr) 427px` with a `.22s` transition and two additional state classes, `.review.feedrailed` / `.review.withfeed`, that widen the first column to show an Instagram grid/feed-connection panel. The app's version is a plain two-column grid with no leading zero-width column, no transition, and no state classes.

Not restoring this: the leading column and its two states exist entirely to host the feed panel, which requires a platform-connections feature (OAuth to Instagram/Meta, storing and syncing feed data) that doesn't exist in this schema. Porting the grid mechanics without anything to put in that column would just be dead CSS. Left alone deliberately — see the inline comment at `.review` in `app/globals.css`.

## Shared-review preview toggle — RESOLVED

Was: the app switched phone/browser layout with a `min-width:900px` media query; the prototype uses a `.desk` class toggled by a `.pw-seg` **Phone / Desktop** segmented control (`data-sv="phone"` / `data-sv="desktop"`, prototype lines ~1922-1923, wired at ~5341-5344) — a manual override, not a responsive layout.

**Fixed on `/review/[token]` itself**, not as a separate agency-only overlay. Reading the prototype's actual markup (`#phoneWrap`, lines 1917-1938) and script (`openPhone()`/`#pwClose`/`#pwReset`, ~5075-5100 and ~5340-5346) first showed this control lives inside a `position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:200` overlay triggered from the Share modal's "Preview" button (`#shPreview`) — an agency-only QA view with "Start over" and "Back to the agency view" buttons and a link-metadata readout (`#pwState`: expiry, passcode, approve permission), all addressed to agency staff checking a link before sending it, not to the guest who opens it.

Building that exact overlay would mean a new trigger in `ShareModal.tsx`, a new full-screen modal layer, and duplicating the phone/desktop rendering already on the real public page — a bigger feature than "port the toggle mechanism." Instead, the toggle itself was added directly to the page a real guest already opens, since that page already renders both shapes and already needs something to decide which one shows. Two things deliberately left out because they don't apply to that page:

- **`#pwState`'s link-metadata readout** (expiry/passcode/approve-permission) — agency-relevant configuration, not something a guest needs told back to them about their own link, and only partially derivable from `useSharedReview`'s current response shape (`scope`, `can_approve` exist; expiry and passcode-required don't).
- **"Start over" / "Back to the agency view"** — reset a demo-only sign-in simulation and return to the authenticated app respectively. Neither concept exists for an unauthenticated guest.

**Behaviour change, disclosed, not silent**: the page now defaults to the prototype's own default (`shareView="phone"`) regardless of the visitor's actual device, replacing the previous responsive default. A real visitor on a desktop browser now sees the phone-shaped preview first and has to click "Desktop" — matching the prototype exactly, but a genuine change from what this page did before.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts`, one case per state (`shared-review-phone` at the default 1280px viewport, `shared-review-desktop` at 1600px — the browser frame plus `.pw-side` plus the gap between them overflows 1280px once both are on screen).

## Primary nav — three icons led to a dead 404 — RESOLVED

`NavRail.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS` used to link to `/calendar`, `/analytics` and `/visibility` unconditionally, for every signed-in user, all the time, none of those routes existing anywhere under `app/`. Checked empirically, not assumed from the route listing: signed in, clicked the Calendar icon from the dashboard, landed on a real `HTTP 404` — Next.js's bare, unstyled default 404 page, no `.rail`, no topbar, nothing. The app shell didn't wrap it because there was no matching route anywhere in the tree for the router to render a shell around; the user was dropped completely out of the product with no way back except the browser's back button.

**Fixed**: the three icons now render only inside a client context (`clientId` truthy, the same `/clients/[id]` path-match `Knowledge` already used), matching the prototype's own `syncRail()` — `show = client||inClient` gates Calendar/Analytics/Knowledge, `!client && inClient` gates Visibility. They're no longer reachable from the dashboard or anywhere else outside a client.

Two things deliberately not replicated, so a future pass doesn't mistake either for an oversight:

- **`syncRail()`'s `client` variable** (the agency/client *preview* toggle, `MODE==="client"` in the prototype — `previewMode` in `store/ui-store.ts` here, already real and wired to the topbar toggle) is not part of the fix's condition. In the prototype it's an OR: these icons also show in preview-as-client mode even without a selected client. This app's `previewMode` isn't tied to any specific client route, so honouring that OR would show the icons in more places (e.g. the dashboard, previewing as client, no client selected) with nothing to point them at — reintroducing dead-end surface rather than removing it. Only the `inClient` half was ported here. (Visibility's `!client` half — real *role*, not preview — is a separate fix; see "Settings and Visibility ignored client role" below.)
- **The underlying screens still don't exist.** This fix corrected *reachability* to match the prototype — the icons no longer appeared where they didn't work — but at the time didn't build Calendar, Analytics or Visibility, so clicking one from inside a client still 404'd. **Now also resolved**: each has a placeholder route (`app/(app)/calendar`, `app/(app)/analytics`, `app/(app)/visibility`) that renders inside the real app shell — rail, topbar, breadcrumb (`Topbar.tsx`'s `CRUMBS` map already had entries for all three, unused until now) — with the same `.empty` block pattern used elsewhere in the app for "nothing here yet" states, saying plainly that the screen isn't built. Still not the feature — this is a shell, not a calendar or a performance dashboard, same already-tracked gap as everything in `docs/css-coverage.md`'s deferred bucket — but a signed-in user clicking any of these three now never leaves the product.
- **Same narrower approximation of "inside a client" that `Knowledge` already has**: gated on the URL literally starting with `/clients/[id]`, not on "is this creative/project's client the one I'm in" — so the icons (like `Knowledge`) disappear again on `/projects/[id]` and `/creatives/[id]`, even though those pages belong to a client too. Extending that would need `NavRail` to know a project's or creative's `client_id`, which it doesn't fetch today; out of scope for this fix, consistent with the choice already made for `Knowledge`.

## Settings and Visibility ignored client role — RESOLVED

Found during a client-view audit, empirically: logged a real client-role session into the running app (not `previewMode` — an actual `memberships.client_id`-scoped user) and walked every built screen. `NavRail.tsx` rendered Settings unconditionally for every signed-in user, and rendered Visibility for anyone inside a `/clients/[id]` route regardless of role — both purely route-pattern gates, no permission check at all. None of the four `/settings/*` pages checked role either, so a client typing the URL directly got the real, populated agency-branding form and (until the fix below) a broken team page — full staff configuration surface, reachable and partly interactive.

The prototype's `syncRail()` treats both as real role boundaries, not reachability: `$("#navSet").style.display = client ? "none" : "grid"` hides Settings from client mode unconditionally, and `$("#navVis").style.display = (!client&&inClient)?"grid":"none"` shows Visibility only to staff — it's the screen where staff configure what a client is *allowed* to see, so showing it to the client themselves defeats the point.

**Fixed** with two layers, both keyed on real membership via a new `useIsStaff()` hook (`useMyAgency()` + `useMyMembership()`, the same pattern `CommentsPanel` already used — deliberately not `previewMode`, which doesn't restrict anything real):

- `NavRail.tsx` hides both Settings and Visibility unless `isStaff` (and hides them while that's still resolving — fails closed, never shows staff-only nav to a session not yet confirmed staff).
- `app/(app)/settings/layout.tsx` — the one layout wrapping all four `/settings/*` routes — redirects a non-staff session to `/dashboard` before rendering any tab content, so a direct URL is closed the same way the nav link already was. No dedicated "forbidden" page exists in this app yet, and a client landing on their own dashboard is a real, useful place to end up rather than a dead end.

Verified empirically both directions: a real client-role session sees neither nav item and gets redirected off every settings page (`tests/e2e/nav-rail.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/settings.spec.ts`); a real staff session still sees and reaches all of it.

**Separately**, `/settings/team` turned out to be broken for *everyone*, staff included, independent of this fix — `hooks/use-team-members.ts`'s `user:users(name, email)` embed was ambiguous (`memberships` has two FKs to `users`: `user_id` and `invited_by`), which PostgREST rejects outright (`PGRST201`) regardless of caller or RLS. The team page silently landed on its generic "Couldn't load the team" state and nothing caught it, since no spec had ever asserted the roster's actual content. Fixed by disambiguating to `users!memberships_user_id_fkey(name, email)`; verified with both a staff and a client session directly against Supabase (both now resolve correctly — RLS itself was never the problem, PostgREST's embed selection was). Not a prototype-parity issue, but found by the same audit.

**Also found in the same audit and now fixed**: `CommentCard`'s Resolve/Reopen button was gated on `isStaff` alone, stricter than both the database and the prototype. Verified empirically, seeded through real authenticated sessions per `frank-conventions`: `comments_update_own`'s RLS policy (`author_id = auth.uid() or is_agency_staff(agency_id)`) lets a client resolve or reopen a thread *they authored*, and correctly blocks them from resolving anyone else's — authorship, not role. The prototype agrees; it only gates "Make Public/Private" behind `MODE==="client"`, never Resolve/Reopen. Changed the condition to `isStaff || thread.author_id === currentUserId`; the internal-toggle and Make Public/Private gates stayed on `isStaff`, since those really are staff-only at both layers. Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/comment-resolve.spec.ts`.

## Dashboard — three of four stat cards are permanently empty — RESOLVED

Was: `app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx` rendered a single `.stat` child (Active Clients) inside `.stats`, which is CSS'd as a 4-column grid (`.stats{grid-template-columns:repeat(4,minmax(0,1fr))}`, matched against the prototype, untouched by the recent CSS pass). With one real child and three that didn't exist, the grid showed one populated cell and three blank grey ones — not zeros, not placeholders, just empty grid tracks. The prototype's other three (Live Projects, Waiting on Approval, Feedback to Action) all needed cross-table aggregation queries that were never built.

**Fixed**: `hooks/use-agency-creative-stats.ts` runs the prototype's `clientStats`-equivalent aggregation — one `projects` count plus one `creatives(stage, exception)` select, banded client-side with the same `bandOf()` step 1 built — and the three remaining cards are wired to it, `.n.flag` applied to Waiting on Approval / Feedback to Action exactly as the prototype does. No `agency_id` filter added client-side; both queries rely entirely on RLS, verified empirically against a two-agency fixture before wiring (`scripts/check-agency-creative-stats-scoping.mjs`, kept as a permanent check, not a one-off).

**Loading state, not in the prototype to match**: the prototype has no async data, so it has no equivalent of a stats query still in flight. Rather than inventing a real skeleton/shimmer treatment not established anywhere else in this codebase, an unresolved card shows `…` in place of the number — same shape as this page's own `"Loading your clients…"` text, and deliberately not `0`, since a zero here reads as "confirmed empty" rather than "not loaded yet" (the same failure shape as the `isStaff` loading-flash bug in `app/(app)/creatives/[id]/page.tsx`). Uses `isPending`, not `isLoading`, since the query stays disabled (and therefore not "loading") until `useMyAgency()` resolves an `agencyId` — `isLoading` alone would flash past that wait straight to the empty-but-"done" look.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`, both the re-baselined screenshot and a dedicated case asserting the four numbers against the fixture's known seed data (1 project, 1 stage-5/review creative → `1`/`1`/`1`/`0`).

## Client workspace and project table — both much thinner than the prototype

Two related, previously-verbal-only findings, formalized here:

- **`/clients/[id]`** (`ClientWorkspacePage`) renders a client's name and a flat list of its projects — no stats strip, no progress bars, no per-project deadline column. The prototype's equivalent client workspace has all of that. — **Project row half RESOLVED, see below. Stats strip/search/filters RESOLVED — see "New Project modal built" below.**
- **`/projects/[id]`** (`ProjectPage`) renders a simplified flat `.ptable` — see the note moved from `app/globals.css` below — where the prototype's is the full resizable, reorderable, custom-column calendar/table grid (`.tbl`, drag-and-drop columns, sticky headers, the whole system audited separately under "calendar" in `docs/css-coverage.md`'s deferred bucket). — **Partially RESOLVED for scheduled-delivery projects, see "Project calendar table" below. Continuous-delivery projects are unchanged (still the flat `.ptable`) and still open.**

Neither is a CSS problem — the classes that exist are correctly styled and matched against the prototype. Both are feature-completeness gaps: the richer prototype views need real queries, real aggregation, and in the project table's case a substantial column-management feature, not a stylesheet change.

### Client workspace project rows — RESOLVED

Was: creative count, approval progress and status columns all rendered `—`, and the row's only real content in that half was a `<span class="tag blue">Scheduled/Continuous</span>` standing in for delivery mode — a placeholder pattern, not the prototype's `renderProjects()`.

**Fixed**: `hooks/use-project-creative-stats.ts` (per-project `bandOf()` aggregation, same shape as the dashboard's hook) now drives the creative count (`${total} total`), the approval bar (`.bar`/`.bar i`/`.barlbl`, ported from the prototype, width `Math.round(done/total*100)%`), and the status tag — `grey` "Not started" / `amber` "`N` with the client" (or "need your review" in client-preview mode, reading `store/ui-store.ts`'s existing `previewMode` the same way the prototype reads `MODE==="client"`) / `green` "All approved" / `blue` "In production", with `pending = waitingOnApproval + feedbackToAction` combined into one count exactly as the prototype's `projStats().pend` is — deliberately not split into two tag states the way the dashboard's cards are.

**Delivery mode relocated, not deleted**: the prototype puts `KBADGE(p.kind)` in the row's `.sub` line under the project name, alongside the project's `type` text — ported as `components/project/KBadge.tsx` plus `.kbadge`/`.cname .t .sub` CSS, both copied verbatim from the prototype. This also resolved a head-row mismatch: the app had grown an extra "Type" column (`Project`/`Type`/`Creatives`/`Mode`/`Due`) not present in the prototype's actual head row (`Project`/`Creatives`/`Approval Progress`/`Status`/`Deadline`) — `.crow`'s grid-template-columns (`1fr 96px 150px 128px 92px 70px`) already matched the prototype's 5-data-column-plus-actions shape, so the extra column was always being squeezed into tracks sized for something else. Removed; project type now reads from the `.sub` line instead of its own column.

Loading state follows the same `isPending`-driven `…` placeholder as the dashboard's cards, for the same reason (`useProjectCreativeStats` returning no data yet reads as legitimately unresolved, not as a confirmed zero).

**"Creatives" column header relabelled to "Posts"** — a deliberate product-copy decision (explicitly requested), not a prototype-parity fix; the prototype's own head row says "Creatives" here. Scoped to this one count label (`{total} total` under a "Posts" header) — the `creative`/`Creative` entity name itself, the `creatives` table, hooks, and the creative review pages are all untouched.

## Project calendar table — built for scheduled-delivery projects, several pieces still deferred

Was: clicking a project from the client workspace always landed on `/projects/[id]`'s flat `.ptable` — Creative/Format/Platforms/Scheduled/Stage, no date grouping — for both delivery modes. Explicitly called out as wrong: a scheduled-delivery project should go straight into a calendar view, matching the prototype's own click-through (`openProject()` → `go("calendar")`, never a separate table screen).

**Built**: `components/project/ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`, replacing `.ptable` on `/projects/[id]` for **scheduled-delivery projects only** — continuous-delivery projects are unchanged (still `.ptable`, ordered by due date), matching the prototype's own split (`isCont()` routes to `renderWork()`, a flat list, never the calendar). Ports `frank-prototype.html`'s `v-calendar`/`renderList()`/`tableRows()` **Month view specifically** — Mon-Sun week grouping with real ISO 8601 week numbers (`lib/calendar-weeks.ts`, unit-tested against the prototype's own worked example: week 36 = 31 Aug-6 Sep 2026), the `.stg` status pill with the prototype's exact stage/exception colours (`stageColor`/`exceptionLabel`, added to `lib/stage-labels.ts` alongside the existing `stageLabel`/`bandOf` so all four read the same table and can't drift apart — unit-tested), platform chips (`lib/platform-colors.ts`, ported `PLATCOL` verbatim), and the bottom legend. Columns shown: Week/Date/Day/Time/Status/Post type/Lead/Platform/Creative/Concept/Approach notes, plus this project's real custom columns (reusing the existing generic `CxCell`/`AddColumnForm` mechanism as-is — "Shoot"/"Boost" in the reference screenshot are themselves just custom columns in the prototype's own data model, not built-in fields, so nothing new was needed there). Month navigation (prev/next/Today) and an "All Statuses" filter (reusing `bandOf`) are both real and functional.

**A new brief jumps the calendar to its own month**: `NewBriefModal` gained an `onCreated(scheduledAt)` callback, fired with the same value already computed for the insert; `ProjectPage` uses it to remount `ProjectCalendarTable` (via `key`) focused on that month. Without this, a brief written for a month the view isn't currently showing would land in the database correctly but never appear anywhere the person could see it without guessing which direction to click "Next" — caught by `tests/e2e/new-brief.spec.ts`'s existing "creates a scheduled creative end to end" case failing once the calendar (defaulting to the current real month) replaced the old ptable (which showed every creative regardless of date).

**Week and Calendar-grid view modes, the Columns picker, and frozen Week/Date/Day columns — RESOLVED, built in a follow-up pass.** All three were explicitly requested after the first pass shipped without them:

- The `#calMode` segmented control is now real, not just Month: **Week** reuses the same table renderer over `getWeekDays()` (one Mon-Sun chunk instead of every week in the month, no week-band row since there's only one week); **Calendar** is a genuine month grid (`components/project/ProjectCalendarGrid.tsx`, ported from `renderCal()`'s calendar branch — `.calhead`/`.calgrid`/`.cell`/`.ev` day cells, max 3 events shown before a "+N more" that expands the cell in place rather than the prototype's floating `#daypop`, a deliberate simplification). `lib/calendar-weeks.ts` gained `getWeekDays()` and `getMonthGridDays()` (the 35/42-cell trim rule) alongside the existing `getMonthWeeks()`, all unit-tested.
- **Display Columns** (`components/project/ColumnsPopover.tsx`, ports `#colpop`/`renderColPicker`): show/hide only, not drag-to-reorder or resize (still deferred, see below). Week/Date/Day aren't in the list at all — see next point.
- **Frozen columns**: Week/Date/Day are hard-coded as always-visible and sticky (`FROZEN_COLUMNS` in `ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`, `position:sticky` with per-column `left` offsets matching the prototype's `sk1`/`sk2`/`sk3`) — a product decision, not a partial port: the prototype's own picker *does* list them as toggleable, this build doesn't, on the reasoning that a frozen column a person can also hide is a confusing combination. Caught one real bug while building this: the header cell's inline `left` style was gated on `c.left` being truthy, which silently dropped the style for the Week column specifically (`left: 0` is falsy) — found by an actual horizontal-scroll screenshot, not by reading the JSX, exactly the kind of thing prototype-parity's "screenshot the real, authenticated app" step exists to catch.
- **Creative hover preview — RESOLVED, real data only.** `components/project/CreativePreviewPopover.tsx` ports `#evpop`/`showPreview`/`postHTML`, attached to both the table's Creative-name cell and the calendar grid's event chips. Deliberately not a literal port: the prototype's own preview fabricates like/view counts (`nfmt(400+hash(name)%2400)`, no schema backing at all) and renders per-format layouts (YouTube/LinkedIn/Email/SMS cards) this pass collapses into one generic layout. What's shown is all real — name, stage/exception + colour, a real open-comment count (`useComments`, counted client-side), the real latest caption (`useCopyVersions`, fetched only for the hovered creative, not per row), and a real "Published N days ago" once `creatives.published_at` is set (added to `useCreatives`'s select). The media area is a decorative gradient placeholder (creative name + time overlaid, no real asset image fetched) — closer to the prototype's own `art()` (itself a generated placeholder, not a real render) than a literal image preview.
- **Toolbar visual pass**: added the `viewbar`/`.vtab` "Main Table" tab (cosmetic — no "+", no multi-view management, see below) and the `.kbadge sch` "Scheduled" pill, both ported CSS classes already used elsewhere in the app.

**Still deliberately not built, same escalation rule as everything else in this file:**

- **Cross-project view** — the prototype's `#calProj` select can show "All" of a client's scheduled projects on one calendar. This build is always scoped to the one project it was opened from; the `Project` column (redundant at that point) was dropped from the ported column set for the same reason.
- **Column drag/reorder and per-column resize, saved/named views (the "+" next to "Main Table", Save/Save as New View), Export** — the prototype's `VIEWS`/`COLSET`/`bindColDrag`/`baseline`/`markDirty` machinery. Column *visibility* is real now (see above); reordering, resizing and persisting a view are not. This is most of what's left of `docs/css-coverage.md`'s "calendar" bucket and is a substantial feature in its own right.
- **Inline cell editing** (click a cell, edit in place) — matches the existing `.ptable`'s own scope (it never had this either, only `CxCell`'s custom-column editing did).
- **Text on image / Post copy columns** — need each creative's *latest* `copy_versions` row (slide_text/fields.caption) fetched per row across the whole visible set; deferred rather than adding an extra per-project batch query in this pass (the hover preview fetches this per-creative on demand instead, which is a much cheaper way to surface the same data for one creative at a time). Same for the Creative-name cell's inline version/comment subtext line ("V2 · copy V1 · no comments") — the hover preview shows a real open-comment count instead, but not inline in the row.
- **Reference link under Concept** — `creatives.reference_url` is fetched by `useCreatives` already (added alongside `concept`/`approach_notes`) but not yet rendered in this table; a one-line addition, just not done here.
- **Day-cell comment-count badges** on the calendar grid's event chips (the prototype's `.cchip` on `.ev`) — the hover preview has this (see above); the always-visible inline badge on every chip would need a per-project comment-count aggregate query this pass didn't add.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/project-calendar.spec.ts` (renamed from `project-table.spec.ts`, whose subject this replaces) — the calendar renders with New Brief present, a new brief jumps the visible month to match its own scheduled date, the status filter narrows rows against a real second creative at a different stage, Calendar mode renders the day grid and hides the Columns button, the Columns picker hides and reshows a column, Week/Date/Day stay frozen (with real text, not the empty-sticky-cell shape the `c.left` truthiness bug produced) across a real horizontal scroll, and hovering a creative shows the real preview popover with real name/status data.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/client-workspace.spec.ts`, re-baselined screenshot plus a dedicated case asserting the badge, count, bar label and status tag text against the fixture's known seed data.

### Column set corrected against the real content-planner template, hover-hold fixed, popover font fixed

The first pass's column set (`TOGGLABLE_COLUMNS`) was built against `frank-prototype.html`'s fictional demo `COLS` array. A real client's actual content planner (Hapi Dental, Sep '26 — not the prototype) showed a different, more specific set of 18 columns in a fixed order, which is what a real spreadsheet-replacement product should actually match. Corrected:

- **Labels and sub-labels** now read verbatim off that sheet: "Post Type / Suggested Type" (was "Post type / Suggested"), "Status / Status of Post" (was "Stage of post"), "Asset Name/Link / Latest Post Visual" (was "Creative / Latest visual"), "Concept / Describe the Post or Add Ref Link", "Approach Notes / Explain the WIIFM Approach".
- **Client Feedback** (red header, matching the sheet's own red column) maps to the most recent *public, unresolved* comment on the creative — `hooks/use-latest-feedback-by-creative.ts`, another whole-project batch query. Public only, deliberately: an internal-only note isn't "client feedback" by definition, and this is a judgment call on what the column means, not something read directly off any single field.

**Text on Image 1/2/3 and Post Copy 1/2/3 — RESOLVED, consolidated to one column each per explicit follow-up direction.** Initially ported as six separate columns matching the sheet literally. Corrected to **one "Image on Text" and one "Post Copy" column**: each cell shows the *latest* `copy_versions` row's value, and hovering it (when there's more than one version) opens `components/project/CopyVersionHistoryPopover.tsx` — a real version-history list, newest first, the current one marked "Latest" — rather than spreading versions across separate columns. `hooks/use-copy-versions-by-creative.ts` batches *every* version per creative for the whole project in one query (not just the latest, and not one query per row), same shape as `useProjectCreativeStats`. This also resolves the "Copy Options" tension the six-column version ran into: what's shown here is real chronological revision history (`copy_versions.version_no`), not the prototype's unrelated "multiple candidate captions on one version" concept — that's still genuinely undecided (see "New Brief" above) and nothing here fakes it. Cell affordance ports the prototype's own `copyCell()` pattern (`.copyc`/`.cc-t`/`.cc-n`, clamped latest value + "+N earlier"), CSS for the popover ports `.copypop`/`.cp-row` verbatim.
- Column count: **14 total** (3 frozen + 11 toggleable) after this consolidation.

**Hover preview didn't hold — fixed.** The popover hid instantly on the trigger's `mouseleave`, and the prototype's own `.evpop` CSS this was ported from sets `pointer-events:none` (the prototype's card is purely decorative — the pointer passes through it, it never receives hover at all). Combined, moving the pointer from the trigger toward the card raced an instant close, so the card could never actually be reached. Fixed with the standard hover-card pattern: a short hide delay (`scheduleHide`) instead of an instant clear, `pointer-events:none` removed from `.evpop` so it can receive events, and the popover's own `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` cancel/reschedule that same delay. Applies to both `ProjectCalendarTable`'s table rows and `ProjectCalendarGrid`'s day-grid chips, which share the one `CreativePreviewPopover` component. Verified with a real `page.mouse.move` from the trigger onto the popover, not just a hover-and-read check.

**Popover title text rendered in the wrong font/size — fixed.** The placeholder art's title was an SVG `<text>` element; SVG text doesn't inherit the page's CSS font stack the way an ordinary DOM node does, so it rendered in a generic fallback instead of the app's actual Inter setup. Moved to a plain HTML overlay (`.pp-art-overlay`/`.pp-art-title`/`.pp-art-time`) positioned over the (now text-free) gradient SVG — inherits the correct font automatically, no font-name matching to get right by hand.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/project-calendar.spec.ts` — all 14 columns present with the sheet's exact labels, the hover preview holds open across a real pointer move onto the card, and a dedicated case seeding two real `copy_versions` rows (`frank.createCopyVersion`, a new fixture helper) confirms the Post Copy cell shows the latest and the hover popover lists both versions newest-first with the latest marked — all verified against a real authenticated session.

## Saved Views — column drag-to-reorder, resize, and agency-wide saved layouts

Was: column order and width were fixed, and the "Column drag/reorder and per-column resize, saved/named views... Save/Save as New View" bullet under "Project calendar table" above was explicitly deferred as a substantial feature of its own. Built in this follow-up per direct request, referencing the prototype's own `VIEWS`/`COLSET`/`bindColDrag`/`markDirty` behavior and two reference screenshots of it in use.

**Schema**: `supabase/migrations/phase10_calendar_views.sql` — a new `calendar_views` table, deliberately **not** project- or client-scoped (same shape as `format_directions`: agency-internal reference data). This is a real product requirement, not a default: "saved globally, so the user can select for other clients too" was explicit. A view's `column_order`/`hidden_columns` naming a key that doesn't exist on whatever project it's applied to (a custom column from a different project, say) simply has no effect there — the same graceful-miss behavior column visibility already had for an unrecognised key. RLS mirrors `format_directions` (staff-only select/insert/update) plus a delete policy `format_directions` itself is missing (flagged below that entry as a likely oversight, not repeated here).

**This migration has not been applied to the live Supabase project as of this pass** — applying it needs `supabase db push` (or the SQL run directly) against a real Postgres connection this session doesn't have credentials for (no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` for CLI login, no DB password for a direct `psql`/pooler connection — only the anon and service-role API keys are in `.env.local`, which don't grant DDL). The user opted to have this applied rather than deferred or left local-only; until someone with the right credentials runs it, `useCalendarViews`/`useCreateCalendarView`/etc. all fail with a real, surfaced PostgREST error (`PGRST205`, "Could not find the table 'public.calendar_views' in the schema cache") — verified empirically, not just assumed, and confirmed to degrade gracefully (the picker/tabs UI still renders with zero saved views, the Save flow shows the real error in the modal rather than failing silently or crashing).

**Built, working independent of the migration** (client-side state, verified against a real session):
- **Column drag-to-reorder** — plain mouse tracking (`mousedown`/`mousemove`/`elementFromPoint` hit-testing/`mouseup`) rather than native HTML5 drag-and-drop, which needs `dataTransfer` wiring to work in Firefox at all and leaves a default ghost image that's more to fight than to use. Frozen columns (Week/Date/Day) aren't draggable — consistent with them not being in the Columns picker either.
- **Column resize** — a `.grip` handle per column, `mousedown` + `mousemove` tracking a width delta, 60px floor. Frozen columns have no grip: their widths are load-bearing constants for the sticky-column `left` offsets (`FROZEN_COLUMNS`), so a resizable frozen column would either need to leave those offsets wrong or add reflow logic for no real product ask behind it.
- **Dirty tracking and the Save button/dropdown** — `ProjectCalendarTable` compares the live `columnOrder`/hidden-columns/`columnWidths` against a baseline (either the built-in default, when on "Main Table", or the active saved view's own stored values) and shows nothing when they match. Reordering, resizing, or hiding/showing a column all mark it dirty; switching tabs silently discards whatever wasn't saved first, matching the prototype's own `switchView()`/`restoreBaseline()` (no confirmation prompt — this is a deliberate match to source behavior, not an oversight). "Save as New View" / "Save" and the dropdown's three options (`Save to "{name}"` / `Save as New View` / `Discard Changes`) render exactly per the two reference screenshots.
- **View tabs** (`.viewbar`) — "Main Table" plus one tab per saved view, a "•" dirty dot (`.dot2`) on the active tab when dirty, a "..." menu (`.vdots`) on the active tab for Rename/Delete, and a "+" (`.vadd`) that opens "Save as New View" directly.
- `components/project/SaveViewModal.tsx` — one small modal for both "Save as New View" and "Rename" (same shape: a name field, a duplicate-name check against the other loaded views, Cancel/Save).

**Found and fixed along the way — a real, pre-existing, app-wide bug, not scoped to this feature**: every error-message fallback in this codebase (14 call sites, 12 of them predating this session) checked `error instanceof Error` before falling back to a generic string. Verified empirically against this project's own Supabase instance (not assumed from postgrest-js's TypeScript types, which do declare `class PostgrestError extends Error`): the actual runtime error object the browser client (`@supabase/supabase-js`) hands back for a failed request — a `42501` RLS denial and a `PGRST205` schema-cache miss both checked directly — has `constructor.name === "Object"` and fails `instanceof Error`. Every one of those 14 checks was therefore silently discarding the real backend message (the one field most likely to say *why* a save failed) and substituting a generic fallback, or nothing, across the entire app: dashboard/project-page load errors, every New*Modal's submit error, `ShareModal`, `UploadOrEditModal`, `BriefPanel`, `FormatDirectionCard`, brand settings, the creative-review stage-advance error. `lib/errors.ts` (`errorMessage(error, fallback)`, unit-tested) checks by shape instead of by class and is now used at all 14 sites. Caught only because this pass's own new mutations hit a real failure (the pending migration above) and the modal showed nothing instead of the actual `PGRST205` message — the same silent-failure shape frank-conventions already tracks once before (`use-update-agency-branding`'s missing-`.select()` bug), just in the error-display half of the same pattern rather than the write-succeeded-silently half.

Spec coverage: `tests/e2e/project-calendar.spec.ts` — resizing a column shows the Save button and Discard Changes reverts the width exactly; dragging a column header reorders the table and marks it dirty; hiding a column via the existing Columns picker also marks it dirty. Not covered (needs the migration applied first): an actual create/rename/delete round-trip against `calendar_views`, and a view's layout surviving a reload or being visible from a different project. `lib/errors.test.ts` covers `errorMessage` directly, including the exact Supabase-shaped plain object verified above.

### `.ptable` — moved here from `app/globals.css`

The following comment sat directly above `.ptable` in `app/globals.css` since Phase 2; moved here because a rationale for *not* matching the prototype belongs in the parity-gaps record, not scattered across the stylesheet as a standalone comment. A one-line pointer is left in its place.

> Project view — dual-mode creative table. No prototype equivalent exists for a plain flat list (the prototype's table is the full resizable, reorderable, custom-column grid — a later phase); this is a simplified stand-in built from the same tokens.

## Comment card — missing the `.intag` "Private" badge

The prototype marks an internal comment two ways: `.cmt.internal`'s border colour (which the app has, restored this session) and a distinct `.intag` badge — an uppercase, pink, icon-plus-label pill (`<div class="intag"><svg>…lock…</svg>Private</div>`, prototype line ~3797) rendered inside the card itself. The app has no `.intag` CSS at all and never renders that badge; instead `CommentCard` shows a generic `<span className="tag grey">Private</span>`, a different, older, more generic pattern used elsewhere in the app for other pills.

Not caused by the recent CSS pass — `.intag` wasn't touched by any of the nine stylesheet commits, only `.cmt.internal` and `.intog` were. Worth noting the app's `.tag.grey` and the prototype's `.intag` are two different visual treatments for the same concept; picking one is a design decision, not a mechanical port, since the app's version already ships and changing it changes what every existing internal comment looks like.

## Knowledge — file uploads and agency-level Reference Material built

Client-level `knowledge_entries` already existed and worked for text notes (8 fixed sections, matching the user's own description of what the client folder should hold — this wasn't new ground, just missing an upload path). Agency-level "Reference Material" had never been ported past the prototype at all — `/settings/knowledge` was actually Format Directions under a stale tab label, confirmed by grepping the whole live app for "Reference Material"/"Add Reference" and finding zero hits outside `frank-prototype.html`.

**Built**:
- `supabase/migrations/phase11_agency_knowledge.sql` — a new `agency_knowledge_entries` table (agency-wide, staff-only all four RLS verbs, no client dimension — same shape as `format_directions`, this table's closest precedent, but *with* a delete policy this time, unlike `format_directions`' documented gap). Adds a nullable `asset_id` to `knowledge_entries` for client-level file entries. Widens `assets_select`'s RLS with one more `exists` branch so a client session can view a file attached to their own client's knowledge folder — the policy has to be fully re-declared (Postgres has no `ALTER POLICY ... ADD CONDITION`); the first two branches are copied verbatim from `phase0_baseline.sql`, only the third is new.
- `lib/upload-knowledge-asset.ts` — shared upload helper (validate → safe filename → `storage.upload` → insert `assets` row with rollback-on-failure), same shape as `use-upload-creative-version.ts`'s upload step, just not tied to a `creative_id`. Path is `{agency_id}/knowledge/{uuid}-{filename}` — no Storage-policy migration needed, since `storage.objects`' RLS (`phase8_storage.sql`) only ever inspected the first path segment (`agency_id`).
- `components/knowledge/KnowledgeFilePreviewModal.tsx` — the "view on click" popup. A PDF renders in an `<iframe>` (the browser's own native PDF viewer — real scroll/zoom/search, no PDF.js dependency added); an image renders directly; anything else (DOCX/XLSX/CSV) gets a plain download link rather than a fake inline preview, since no dependency-free renderer exists for those.
- `KnowledgeSection.tsx` gained "+ Add file" alongside the existing "+ Add note", and file/image entries with a real `asset` now render as a clickable trigger for the preview modal instead of the old "Preview isn't wired up yet" placeholder. Remove now works for every kind, not just text (Edit stays text-only — there's no "body" to edit on a file entry).
- `components/settings/AgencyKnowledgeSection.tsx` + wired into `/settings/knowledge` below the untouched Format Directions section (per direct instruction to leave formats alone for now). Settings tab relabelled "Format directions" → "Knowledge" (`app/(app)/settings/layout.tsx`), matching the client-side nav item's own name — both the agency and client sections are now called Knowledge, per direct instruction.

**Migration not yet applied to the live database** — same situation as `phase10_calendar_views.sql`: this session has no DB password or CLI access token, only the app's own API keys, which can't run DDL. Two migrations are now pending (`phase10_calendar_views.sql`, `phase11_agency_knowledge.sql`) and both need to be run from the Supabase SQL Editor (or `supabase db push` with real credentials) before Saved Views or Knowledge file uploads work end-to-end.

**Found and fixed as a direct consequence — `useKnowledgeEntries` made resilient to the pending migration**: unlike `calendar_views` (a brand-new table with nothing to break), `knowledge_entries` is already live and working for text notes today. Selecting the new `asset_id` column before the migration lands would have 400'd on every request — breaking an already-shipped feature, not just leaving a new one inert. Fixed by catching Postgres's `42703` (undefined_column) and falling back to the pre-migration column list, verified empirically against the real, not-yet-migrated database (a real text note saved and rendered correctly; a file upload attempt surfaced the real, honest error instead of taking the whole page down). The same class of gap existed in the test fixture teardown: `knowledge_entries` (and `assets`) were never swept by `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s agency-wide cleanup at all — invisible until this session's own new spec became the first to actually write a row into either table, orphaning one fixture agency mid-run. Both now swept, in FK-safe order (before `clients`/`agencies`, since `knowledge_entries.client_id` and `assets` are both referenced from elsewhere in the chain).

**Found and fixed, a real (not schema) blocker**: the `assets` Storage bucket itself had a hard allowlist — `image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp,video/mp4,video/mov` only, `file_size_limit: 1 MiB` — rejecting every PDF/DOCX/XLSX upload regardless of what the application code allowed. Confirmed empirically (a real PDF upload attempt failed with Storage's own "mime type ... is not supported", not an RLS or schema error). Widened via `supabase.storage.updateBucket()` to also accept PDF/DOC/DOCX/XLS/XLSX/CSV and raised the size limit to 25MB, matching `lib/knowledge-file-validation.ts`'s own cap exactly (the bucket-level list should be a superset of, not a separate source of truth from, the client-side validator — the two now agree by construction, not by coincidence). The `public: true` flag on the bucket was already set before this change and was left as-is — only the mime-type allowlist and size limit were widened, both purely additive (more allowed, nothing newly restricted). Flagged for the user's explicit sign-off (a shared-infrastructure config change, applied directly via the Storage Admin API since it isn't reachable through a SQL migration) rather than assumed acceptable — see the session's own account of this exchange for the full reasoning.

### Reference Material rebuilt to match the reference screenshot exactly

The first pass reused the generic `.kbcard`/`.kbentry` grid client-level Knowledge already had — functional, but not what the reference screenshot showed. Rebuilt `components/settings/AgencyKnowledgeSection.tsx` against the prototype's actual `renderMethod()` markup (`frank-prototype.html`) instead: a `.panel` with a `.panel-h` title+count, one `.kb-item` row per entry (a colour-coded `.kb-ic` square — blue "Aa" for a text note, red file-extension glyph for a file, matching the prototype's `KBCOL`/`KBICO`), a `title · ext · size · author · date` metadata line, and a single `.kb-foot` "Add Reference" button (replacing the old two separate "+ Add note"/"+ Add file" buttons — clicking it now opens an inline two-way choice instead). `.panel`/`.panel-h`/`.kb-item`/`.kb-ic`/`.kb-b`/`.kb-acts`/`.kb-foot`/`.fbtn` ported into `app/globals.css`, verbatim from the prototype's own rules. `.kbcard`/`.kbentry` are left in place — client-level `KnowledgeSection.tsx` still uses them; whether to give client-level Knowledge the same visual treatment is an open question, not assumed.

`use-agency-knowledge.ts` now also fetches `created_by`/`bytes` and joins a third `users` query for the author's name (the metadata line needs "Raj Kapadia", not just a date) — same two-query-instead-of-embed reasoning as the existing `asset_id` join, extended by one more table.

Also wrapped the page's intro copy in the prototype's `.note` info box (it was plain `.sub` text before) — the reference screenshot shows one blue note above both the Format Directions and Reference Material panels, not a plain paragraph. `settings-knowledge.png`'s screenshot baseline was regenerated for this.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, and the unit suite are clean. `settings.spec.ts`'s knowledge-tab e2e test passes against the real, still-un-migrated database — its `.empty` "Couldn't load reference material" state now renders correctly once `useAgencyKnowledge`'s default 3 retries exhaust (this needed a longer `waitForSelector`, not a code fix — a fresh query's retry backoff genuinely takes several seconds before settling to an error, same TanStack Query default this whole app already relies on elsewhere). The actual `.panel`/`.kb-item` visual layout — unreachable end-to-end until `phase11_agency_knowledge.sql` is applied — was instead confirmed by rendering the same markup against the real `app/globals.css` in an isolated static page with representative fake data (blue/red icon squares, `PDF · 412 KB · Raj Kapadia · 18 Feb 26` metadata line, single "Add Reference" button); screenshot matched the reference.

### Format Directions rebuilt as the full categorized catalog, replacing the opt-in "+ Add format" list

Re-shown the reference screenshot, the user confirmed this still didn't match: the reference's Format Directions panel lists all 41 catalog formats up front, grouped into collapsible categories with per-category counts (`Social — organic · 14 formats`, etc.) — not a growing list an admin opts individual formats into one at a time. This was a real behaviour change, not a reskin, confirmed worth doing before starting since `lib/formats.ts` already carries `FORMAT_CATEGORIES` and a `category` field per format (ported from the prototype's own `FORMATS` object) — no new data needed to group by it.

Bigger context driving this, from the same message: agency-level Reference Material (universal) and client-level Knowledge (client-specific) are both meant to become context an eventual AI-assisted "draft caption from reference and concept" feature will read, alongside Format Directions as the third, per-format layer. That feature (and its Claude/Gemini/ChatGPT provider hookup) is out of scope for this pass — flagged and deferred, not built — but it's why Format Directions needed revisiting now rather than staying deferred: the three layers need to actually hold real, well-organized content before any drafting feature can consume them, and a hard-to-browse opt-in list works against that.

**Built**:
- `components/settings/FormatCategorySection.tsx` (new) — one collapsible `.fd-cat`/`.fd-body` section per category (`frank-prototype.html`'s own collapse behaviour: only the first category defaults open, including "General" starting closed — ported from its `FD_OPEN` seed, `FD_OPEN[c] = i===0`, rather than guessed).
- `components/settings/FormatDirectionRow.tsx` (new, replacing the deleted `FormatDirectionCard.tsx`) — one `.fd-row` per format. Every format in the catalog renders, whether or not an agency has saved anything against it yet (a format with no row is just a stub — `record` is `undefined`, and the meta line falls back to the prototype's own "no caption" copy). Clicking "Edit" expands the same field set the old `FormatDirectionCard` had (direction text, caption length, sentence range, artwork lines, words per line, case rule) inline below the row — the prototype's own `data-fdedit` opens a modal instead, but this app already has an established inline-edit convention (the component this replaced used it too) and building a new modal system wasn't part of what was asked for.
- `app/(app)/settings/knowledge/page.tsx` — Format Directions is now one `.panel` (title + total count in the header, `.fd-note` subtitle, then one `FormatCategorySection` per `FORMAT_CATEGORIES` entry), matching Reference Material's own panel shape. `useFormatDirections`'s query is unchanged (still only fetches saved rows) — the page merges it against the full catalog by `format_id` into a `Map` and passes that down, rather than changing what the hook fetches.
- `app/globals.css` — `.fd-note`, `.fd-cat` (+ `:hover`, `svg`, `.open svg`, `b`, `span`), `.fd-row` (+ `:last-child`), `.fd-h` (+ `b`), `.fd-m`, `.fd-d` ported verbatim from the prototype. One class has no prototype precedent: `.fd-edit`, a plain unscoped border/padding wrapper for the inline edit form — the old `.bf-b` this replaced only toggles via a `.brief.open` parent selector, which doesn't apply once the outer wrapper is `.fd-row` instead of `.brief`; simpler to add one small class than take on the prototype's modal for this.
- Deleted `components/settings/FormatDirectionCard.tsx` and `components/settings/AddFormatForm.tsx` (the "+ Add format" catalog picker) — both fully superseded, nothing else referenced either.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. `settings.spec.ts`'s "add a format from the catalog" test was rewritten as "edit a format direction inline" (the flow it tested no longer exists) and passes; the knowledge-tab screenshot baseline was regenerated and visually matches the reference exactly (category rows, chevron, per-category counts, "no caption" on every unedited format). `settings-team.png` fails independently in this same spec file — unrelated pre-existing drift in the Team tab's screenshot (a stale label-width mismatch), not touched by or caused by this work.

**Still open, deferred by direct instruction**: the AI-assisted drafting feature itself (concatenating all three knowledge layers into a prompt, choosing between Claude/Gemini/ChatGPT) is future work — not started, not designed in detail here beyond confirming today's data shape already supports it.

## AI provider capability — Claude/ChatGPT/Gemini, platform-pays with a per-agency cap

The three knowledge layers above exist to feed an eventual "draft caption from reference and concept" feature. This entry is the capability that feature will call, not the feature itself — no UI trigger was built, because no caller was specified (what exact combination of agency/client knowledge + format direction + concept goes into a prompt is a separate, later design question).

**Decisions made explicitly, not assumed**: platform pays for provider usage (the agency never supplies its own key) — the tradeoff being cost exposure to the platform rather than per-agency setup friction — capped per-agency (not one global cap, so one agency's volume can't starve the others) and measured in request count (not tokens or estimated $, for simplicity — a very long draft costs the same as a short one against this cap, a known, accepted imprecision).

**Built**:
- `supabase/migrations/phase12_ai_drafting.sql` — `ai_usage_events` (agency_id, user_id, provider, created_at — staff-only RLS, same shape as `agency_knowledge_entries`) backs the cap by counting real rows since the start of the current month, rather than a separate counter column that could drift out of sync; also serves as an audit trail of who drafted with what. `agencies.ai_monthly_request_cap` (default 300) is the per-agency ceiling — configurable per agency, not hardcoded.
- `lib/ai/providers/{anthropic,openai,google}.ts` — one `generate(prompt)` per provider, each reading its own env var (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_API_KEY`) and throwing a clear error if unset, rather than a confusing SDK-internal failure. `lib/ai/generate-draft.ts` dispatches by provider via dynamic import, so a broken env var for one provider can't take the other two down at module-load time.
- `app/api/ai/draft/route.ts` — the only place any of this gets called from. Confirms a real staff membership (same shape `use-my-membership.ts` already reads client-side — RLS would block the usage-log insert either way, this just returns a real 403 instead of a confusing 500), checks the agency's usage-this-month against its cap *before* calling any provider, calls the provider, and only logs the usage event on success (a failed provider call shouldn't count against the cap).

**Not yet applied to the live database** — same situation as the last two migrations. `phase12_ai_drafting.sql` needs the Supabase SQL Editor (or `supabase db push` with real credentials) before this route does anything but 500 at the cap-check step.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint and `next build` (with the three new SDK dependencies — `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `openai`, `@google/generative-ai`) all clean. Functionally verified everything reachable without the pending migration or a real provider key, against a real logged-in session on the local dev server: missing/invalid fields → 400, a staff session with no membership on the requested agency → 403, and — critically — a *valid* staff session on its own real agency reaches all the way to the cap-check step and fails there with "Couldn't load this agency" (`agencies.ai_monthly_request_cap` doesn't exist yet), confirming the auth and membership logic is correct and the only remaining blocker is the pending migration. The unauthenticated case never reaches the route at all — `middleware.ts`'s existing session gate redirects it to `/login` first, the same as every other non-public route in this app.

**Not yet possible to verify**: an actual successful generation (no real provider API keys are configured anywhere, and none were supplied) and the cap itself actually blocking at the limit (needs the migration applied first). Both need real keys in `.env.local` and Vercel's Production environment variables (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_API_KEY`) before either can be exercised for real.

## "Draft from Brief" — wired to the real AI capability, model picker, and the WIIFM note stops being hand-typed

Three decisions made explicitly before writing any of this (not assumed): the model an agency drafts with is one agency-wide setting (Settings > Knowledge — an admin changes it rarely, it's not a per-draft picker, since it's a cost-affecting choice under the platform-pays model above), the cost context shown next to each model is relative tiers ("Most capable" / "Balanced" / "Fastest, cheapest") rather than hardcoded $ figures (provider pricing drifts, a stale number would mislead), and the WIIFM note on a brief stops being something a human types — it's re-derived by AI from whatever copy was last saved, every time.

**Found while placing the "Draft" trigger — a real course-correction, not a new decision**: the reference screenshot that prompted this (Upload/Edit modal, a "WIIFM Note" open text box) suggested drafting belonged there.
