It doesn't. `components/creative-review/ChecksAndDraft.tsx` already existed with a mock "Draft from Brief" button and its own code comment recording a prior developer handover verbatim: *"Draft from Brief sits in the New Brief window and the brief editor on the review screen — not in the upload panel, because by upload the copy already exists."* A first pass wired the real AI call into `UploadOrEditModal.tsx` anyway (next to the Caption field) before this comment was noticed; that work was reverted in full once found, and rebuilt in the correct, already-designated place instead. `UploadOrEditModal.tsx`'s only real change ended up being the WIIFM-note refresh call below, unrelated to where drafting itself lives.

**Built**:
- `supabase/migrations/phase12_ai_drafting.sql` (extended, still unapplied) — `agencies.ai_default_model` (admin-only to change, same `is_agency_admin()` gate as `agencies_update` already has), and `ai_usage_events.provider` renamed to `model` (stores the exact model id, not just the vendor, since one agency setting can name any model in the catalog).
- `lib/ai/models.ts` — the model catalog (Claude Opus 5/Sonnet 5/Haiku 4.5, GPT-4.1/mini, Gemini 2.5 Pro/Flash) with relative tier labels, no per-model $ figures.
- `lib/ai/run-ai-task.ts` — the cap-check-then-call-then-log logic factored out of `app/api/ai/draft/route.ts` (which now resolves the model from the agency's own setting rather than accepting one from the client) so `app/api/ai/wiifm-note/route.ts` (new) doesn't duplicate it.
- `app/api/ai/wiifm-note/route.ts` — fetches the creative's concept and its *latest* copy version itself (never trusts client-supplied copy text, so the note can't drift from what's actually saved), asks the model for one WIIFM statement per line, and overwrites `creatives.approach_notes` with the parsed result.
- `components/settings/AiModelSettings.tsx` (+ `use-agency-ai-settings.ts`, `use-update-agency-ai-model.ts`) — the model picker, wired into Settings > Knowledge above Format Directions.
- `components/creative-review/ChecksAndDraft.tsx` rebuilt for real: "Draft from Brief" now calls `/api/ai/draft` with a prompt assembled from the creative's concept + approach notes, this format's direction (`useFormatDirections`), and both knowledge layers' text entries (`useAgencyKnowledge`, `useKnowledgeEntries`) — `lib/ai/build-caption-prompt.ts`'s `buildCaptionPrompt`/`parseDraftOptions` (unit-tested) ask for and parse three options in one call, each grounded in a different persuasion principle, matching the mock UI's original Scarcity/Social proof/Authority shape but for real. "Accept" saves the chosen option as a real new copy version (`useSaveCopyFields`), same path any other copy edit uses.
- `components/creative-review/BriefPanel.tsx`'s "Approach notes — WIIFM rationale" `ListEditor` is now always `readOnly` (regardless of `isStaff`) and no longer part of `useUpdateBrief`'s payload; `components/project/NewBriefModal.tsx`'s equivalent input at brief-creation time is removed outright (a brand-new brief has no copy yet for anything to derive a WIIFM note from).

**Known gap, not silently dropped**: `NewBriefModal` can still create a first copy version directly (an initial `caption` typed at brief time) — that path doesn't trigger the WIIFM-note refresh the way `UploadOrEditModal`'s save does, since the insert happens inside `useCreateCreative`'s mutation body, not somewhere the modal can hook a follow-up fetch onto easily. A brief created with an initial caption keeps a null `approach_notes` until the first Upload/Edit save. Flagged rather than expanded further given the size of this pass already.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build`, and the unit suite (44 tests, including new coverage for `buildCaptionPrompt`/`parseDraftOptions`) all clean. `creative-review.spec.ts`'s two screenshot baselines were regenerated for the layout shift (approach notes render shorter now that they're never editable) and the new "Draft from Brief" button; both pass. Functionally verified end-to-end against the real dev server with a throwaway fixture agency/client/project/creative: clicking "Draft from Brief" reaches the real route, passes the staff-membership check, and fails exactly at the pending-migration boundary ("Couldn't load this agency") — same confirmation shape as the AI capability entry above, not yet a real generation (needs the migration applied and real provider keys, neither available here).

## New Brief / Upload or Edit / Draft from Brief merged into one window (CreativeModal)

A direct, explicit instruction to merge three separate entry points into one two-tab window (Brief, Upload & Copy). Before building it, `project-details/frank-developer-handover.docx` turned up a second time this session (first found via the `ChecksAndDraft.tsx` comment, this time read in full): its "Decisions worth knowing" section states, verbatim, *"Briefing and upload are different moments — Drafting belongs at briefing time. Checking belongs at upload. The New Brief button appears only on the table; the review screen offers Upload or Edit instead,"* and separately places Draft from Brief in "the New Brief window and the brief editor on the review screen — not in the upload panel." The merge as requested reverses both. Surfaced to the user before writing any code (the doc's own words: *"Changing them is fine; changing them unknowingly is not"*) — confirmed explicitly: proceed with the merge, knowingly overriding both.

**Built**: `components/creative-review/CreativeModal.tsx` replaces `NewBriefModal.tsx`, `UploadOrEditModal.tsx` and `ChecksAndDraft.tsx` (all three deleted) with one component, a discriminated union on `mode: "create" | "edit"`:
- **Brief tab** — name, content type/format, publish date or destination, lead, concept, reference link, Text on Image, custom columns (create mode only — these become read-only display in edit mode, since nothing else in the app lets them change post-creation and this pass doesn't add that). No caption field, per direct instruction. In edit mode, "Save Brief" reuses `useUpdateBrief`/`useSaveSlideText` exactly as `BriefPanel` used to.
- **Upload & Copy tab** — disabled (with a "Save the brief first" tooltip) until a creative exists. Holds the artwork drop zone, caption/headline/CTA, "Draft with [Model]" (inline now, not a nested dialog — three options render as cards with "Use this," filling the Caption field without saving), and the three Check buttons, unchanged from `UploadOrEditModal`.
- Creating a brief no longer auto-closes the window — it unlocks the Upload & Copy tab in the same session instead, which is the point of the merge (a caption can be drafted and saved for a brand-new brief without reopening anything). `new-brief.spec.ts`'s two creation tests updated accordingly: assert the tab is enabled and close via Cancel, rather than asserting the window closed on its own.
- `BriefPanel.tsx` (still rendered inline on the review page) is now purely informational: collapsed by default (was open), every field read-only, no save affordance at all — editing happens only in `CreativeModal`'s Brief tab now. The review page's "Upload or Edit" button is renamed "Creative" and opens the merged window on the Upload & Copy tab; the empty-state "Upload Artwork" button does the same, unchanged label.
- `use-create-creative.ts` no longer accepts a `caption` field at all (only `slideText`, still brief content) — matches the caption field's removal from the Brief tab.

**Known gap, carried over from the previous entry, now slightly different in shape**: a brief created with Text on Image still creates an initial copy version without triggering the WIIFM-note refresh (same reasoning as before — that insert happens inside `useCreateCreative`'s own mutation body). Since caption can no longer be set at creation time at all, this gap now only affects slide text, not caption — a smaller version of the same known limitation.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build` and the unit suite (41 tests) all clean. Full e2e suite (54 tests) run twice: 53/54 both times, the one failure (`settings-team.png`) confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (a stale label-width mismatch flagged earlier this same session, nothing to do with this change). Screenshot baselines regenerated for `creative-review.spec.ts` (new tab bar, renamed button, collapsed Brief panel) and `settings.spec.ts` (a genuine, separate timing race in the *previous* entry's Drafting Model panel — it could settle to its error state at a different point in the retry cycle depending on system load, the same class of fix `page.waitForSelector` already applies to Reference Material on the same page; fixed the same way, verified stable across three repeated runs).

## "Move to Client Review" / "Move back to Internal" removed — UI only, database left alone

Direct instruction: the two stage-transition buttons on the review page "are not needed." Removed the buttons, the `useAdvanceCreativeStage` hook (deleted, nothing else used it), and `tests/e2e/stage-transition.spec.ts` (deleted — all three of its tests existed to exercise this exact UI; none tested anything else). Cleaned up two now-dangling references to the deleted hook: a comment in `use-create-creative.ts` explaining its `refetchType: "all"` fix by analogy to the deleted one (rewritten to stand on its own), and a citation in the `frank-conventions` skill's own convention list (same fix, same pattern — points at `use-create-creative.ts` alone now).

**Deliberately not touched**: `phase9_advance_creative_stage.sql` (the `advance_creative_stage()` RPC and the `creatives_update` RLS tightening that shipped with it) — already applied to the live database, and the RLS tightening is a real security improvement independent of whether any UI calls the RPC it also protects. "The button isn't needed" was read as exactly that — the button — not as an instruction to also roll back a security-relevant schema change via a new migration, which nothing in the request asked for. The RPC itself is now unreachable from anywhere in the app but still exists and still enforces staff-only access if called directly.

## Modal spacing made generous and consistent, system-wide

Direct instruction, with two Slack settings-page screenshots as the reference: existing popups felt cramped, and every popup should share the same, more spacious feel. `Modal.tsx`'s own `.modal-h`/`.modal-b`/`.modal-f` wrapper is what every popup in the app renders through (`NewClientModal`, `NewProjectModal`, `ShareModal`, `CreativeModal`, `SaveViewModal`) — widened there once rather than adjusting each modal's own markup: header padding `15px 20px` → `20px 28px` (title 15px → 16px), body padding `20px` → `28px`, footer padding `13px 20px` → `16px 28px`.

`.field`/`.frow` (the shared label+input row markup every one of those modals uses) is also used by `GuestComposer.tsx` — the public shared-review page's comment composer, not a popup. Bumping those classes directly would have changed a live, public-facing page nobody asked to touch. Scoped the more generous field spacing (20px between fields, 7px label gap, 9px/12px input padding, 14px row gap) under `.modal-b` specifically, leaving the bare `.field`/`.frow` rules — and therefore `GuestComposer` — at their original, tighter values. Caught this by running the full e2e suite after the first pass, not by inspecting every `.field` call site up front: `shared-review-public.spec.ts`'s two screenshots showed a real diff (taller inputs) that had nothing to do with any popup.

`CreativeModal`'s own `.mtabs` tab strip bleeds a negative margin against `.modal-b`'s padding to sit flush with the modal's edges (see the earlier tab-styling entry) — updated its `-20px` to `-28px` to match, mechanically, not a restyle.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build`, unit suite (41) clean. Full e2e suite twice: 50/51 both times (54 minus the 3 deleted stage-transition tests from the entry above), the one failure the same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake. `upload-artwork-modal.png` baseline regenerated for the real spacing change (visually confirmed against a fresh render before regenerating, not just accepted blind); `shared-review-public.spec.ts`'s two baselines confirmed back to zero-diff once the fix was scoped, proving the public page was genuinely unaffected rather than just visually close enough to pass anyway.

## Five fixes: annotation composer clipping, Public/Private nomenclature, a visibility toggle after posting, full brief editability, and a button rename

**Annotation composer clipping/z-index** — the pin/region comment popup (`AnnotationLayer.tsx`'s `.annot-composer`) always centred itself under the anchor point; a pin near the artwork's own edge pushed a fixed-width popup half outside the image, clipped by `.canvas`'s scroll container. Now computed inline per-instance: flips left/right/up/down based on the anchor's own x/y (>0.75 or <0.25 of the image), and `z-index` raised from 5 to 60 as a second, defensive guarantee. Verified empirically, not just reasoned about — uploaded a real image via the actual upload flow, placed a pin at x=0.92/y=0.1 (the exact corner that used to break), and measured the rendered composer's bounding box: fully inside the image's own right edge, not clipped.

**"Adding comments from a pin or selection" had no visibility control at all** — both `AnnotationLayer.tsx` and `CaptionHighlighter.tsx` had their own near-identical inline comment form (each a separate `DraftForm`), and both hardcoded `visibility: "private"` in the page handler with no way to change it. Extracted the shared shape into `components/creative-review/CommentDraftForm.tsx` (one component now, not two copies) and gave it the same staff-only Public/Private checkbox the main comment composer already had — a client-role session never sees it, since the DB's `enforce_comment_visibility` trigger would silently force it back to public regardless of what was picked.

**Nomenclature: "Internal" → "Private"/"Public", everywhere it's user-facing** — the composer checkbox ("Mark internal" → "Private"/"Public", reflecting its own current state), the locked-reply label, and the Comments filter chip ("Internal" → "Private"). Left every internal identifier alone (`.cmt.internal`, `visibility: "private"`, the `internal` filter key) — this was a copy change, not a rename of the underlying concept.

**A submitted comment had no way to change visibility after the fact** — `comments_update_own` (author or staff) already permits this at the RLS layer, and the enforcement trigger already handles the "client tries to flip it" case correctly (forces public) — nothing in the schema needed to change. Added `hooks/use-toggle-comment-visibility.ts` and a `VisibilityTag` component in `CommentsPanel.tsx`: the static "Private" badge is now a clickable Public/Private pill for staff (on both a top-level thread and any of its replies), unchanged (static, non-interactive) for a client viewer.

**Brief fields locked after creation, unlocked** — `CreativeModal`'s Brief tab had name/format/content type/date-or-destination/lead as read-only display once a creative existed, matching what `NewBriefModal` originally scoped as create-only. Direct instruction reversed this: all of it is now editable in both modes, gated only by the modal's own footer Save action — the *only* place these stay display-only is the small meta line above the post itself on the review page (`{creative.format} · {stageLabel}`), which was never an edit surface to begin with. `hooks/use-creative.ts` gained `lead_user_id`/`due_on` to its select (needed to pre-fill the now-editable Lead/Needed-By fields — neither was fetched before, since neither was ever shown outside create mode). `hooks/use-update-brief.ts` rewritten to accept and write the full field set instead of just `concept`/`referenceUrl`.

**"Creative" button renamed "Edit"** — one-line change, `app/(app)/creatives/[id]/page.tsx`.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build`, unit suite (41) clean. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). Screenshot baselines regenerated for `creative-review.spec.ts` (button rename, Brief tab now showing real selects) and `comment-visibility.spec.ts` (composer checkbox text width, the new per-comment Public/Private pill). Functionally verified beyond screenshots: edited every brief field (name/format/date) on a real existing creative through the actual UI and confirmed the new values landed in the database, not just that the inputs looked editable.

## Copy fields matched to format, version tabs for both Copy and Creative, Draft handles multiple fields

Three separate reports resolved together, since the fix for one (per-format copy fields) changed the shape everything else in the Upload & Copy tab needed to work against.

**"Headline"/"Call to action" always shown, regardless of format** — these were leftover from the very first hardcoded modal, never matched to what a format actually needs. The prototype (`frank-prototype.html`) already has the real answer: a `copy` field list per format (e.g. Instagram Feed → `caption, alt`; Meta Feed Ad → `primary, headline, description, cta`; Instagram Story → nothing at all beyond Text on Image) with a label map (`COPYLBL`). Ported both into `lib/formats.ts` (`FormatDefinition.copyFields`, `COPY_FIELD_LABELS`) — no schema change needed, since `copy_versions.fields` is already a plain `jsonb` object, never constrained to three named keys. `CreativeModal`'s Upload & Copy tab now renders exactly the fields a format's `copyFields` names (long-form ones as a textarea, short ones as an input — same split the prototype makes), and shows a plain note instead of any fields at all for a format with none.

**Draft from Brief redesigned around multiple fields** — asking a model for one "caption" doesn't work when a format needs four different fields at once (Meta Feed Ad's primary/headline/description/cta, all in the same voice, none contradicting another). `buildCaptionPrompt`/`parseDraftOptions` (`lib/ai/build-caption-prompt.ts`) now take the format's own field list, ask the model to fill every one of them per option (one line per field key, e.g. `HEADLINE: ...`), and parse each option into a `Record<string, string>` rather than a single string. "Use this" now populates every field the format needs from one option, not just a caption.

**Version tabs — Copy and Creative both, one interaction model, confirmed before building**: clicking an older version shows it read only; a "Rework this version" button (Copy only — see below) copies its content into the always-editable latest tab, which creates a new version on Save. `useCopyVersions`/`useCreativeVersions` already fetched full history, newest first, for the review page's own version selectors — `CreativeModal` now receives those same arrays (`copyVersions`/`creativeVersions` props, replacing the old `latestCopyVersion`/`latestCreativeVersionNo`) instead of fetching its own separate copy.

- **Copy tabs**: `viewingCopyVersionNo` tracks which version is being looked at; `draftFields` is the actual editable draft, untouched by merely browsing old tabs. "Rework this version" copies that version's fields into `draftFields` and jumps back to the latest tab — matches "if the user reworks any of the previous versions it creates a new one from that" exactly.
- **Creative tabs**: no "rework" action — there's no text to copy for a binary file, and the only way to supersede one is uploading a genuinely new one (the existing drop zone, on the latest tab). Older tabs show a read-only preview (image/video inline, `useAssetSignedUrl`) plus an "Open" link. The latest tab, when a version already exists, now also states which file is current and its upload date above the drop zone — previously the drop zone gave no indication anything had been uploaded before.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build`, unit suite (46, new coverage for multi-field `buildCaptionPrompt`/`parseDraftOptions`) all clean. Functionally verified end-to-end against the real dev server, not just read through: seeded a `meta_feed` creative (a real 4-field format) with two copy versions directly, confirmed the modal renders exactly Primary Text/Headline/Description/Call to Action (not the old fixed three), confirmed the latest version's real values populate the editable fields, confirmed clicking the older tab shows its own real content read-only with a working Rework button, and confirmed clicking Rework correctly copies that version's fields back into the editable draft. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) after a dev-server restart — a first pass showed additional failures (`client-workspace`, `project-calendar`, a `NavRail`/`useCurrentUser` "No QueryClient" crash) that traced to `next dev` degrading after a very long-running session under repeated concurrent test load, not this change — confirmed by the crash being in files untouched this entire session, and by the exact same suite passing clean immediately after a fresh restart.

**Root cause of the originally-reported "Draft button isn't clickable"**: confirmed directly against the live database — `agencies.ai_default_model` still doesn't exist. `phase12_ai_drafting.sql` remains unapplied; nothing here is a code fix for that, the button correctly resolves and works once the migration lands.

## Modal redesign to match the "Plan the activity" reference, upload-mode selector removed

Direct instruction, with a screenshot of a "Plan the activity" modal as the explicit reference ("Please copy. Our modal is not looking good at all"). Two asks: lose the "What Are You Uploading?" selector and show Creative and Copy as two always-separate sections, and explain why Draft still shows disabled.

**Visual pass, `app/globals.css`** — `.mtabs`/`.mtab` rebuilt from the earlier "raised folder tab" style (itself from a now-superseded Slack reference) to a plain underline tab: a single `border-bottom` on the strip, an active tab picking up `var(--action)` on both text and its own 2px underline, no background or border on the tab itself. New `.msection-h` class (bold label, bottom divider, generous margin) for titled section breaks, matching the reference's "Iteration"/"Status" grouping. `.field label` now uses `var(--muted))` instead of full-strength ink, matching the reference's softer label weight. `.modal` corner radius `10px` → `16px`; `.iconbtn` (the header close button, only used by `Modal.tsx`) changed from a bordered square to a circular, borderless, soft-grey button.

**`CreativeModal.tsx` structural change** — deleted `type UploadMode`, the `UPLOAD_MODES` constant and the `uploadMode` state entirely, along with the "What Are You Uploading?" three-way segmented control that used to gate which sections rendered. `includesCreative`/`includesCopy` were mode-derived booleans; replaced with a single `includesCopy = copyFieldSpecs.length > 0` (a format either has copy fields or it doesn't — Creative is now unconditional, since every format has artwork). The Upload & Copy tab now always renders both sections, under `.msection-h` "Creative" and `.msection-h` "Copy" headers, neatly separated exactly as the reference shows. `handleSaveUpload()` changed alongside this: a file is only mandatory the very first time a creative is saved — once any version exists, dropping a new one is optional, since a save might just be reworking copy this time.

**Draft button "greyed out" — same root cause as the previous entry, re-confirmed, not a new bug**: queried the live database again in this same turn; `agencies.ai_default_model` still does not exist. `phase12_ai_drafting.sql` has not been applied. This is not something further code changes here can fix — restated plainly rather than re-investigated as if it might be new.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, `next build`, unit suite (42) all clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a real `meta_feed` creative, screenshotted both the Brief tab and the Upload & Copy tab of the live modal, confirmed the rendered layout matches the reference (underline tabs with populated/empty dot indicators preserved, Creative section with header/divider/drop zone, Copy section with header/divider/Draft button and the correct per-format fields, rounded modal corners, circular close button, no mode selector anywhere). Full e2e suite: 51/52, the one failure (`settings-team.png`) the same pre-existing, unrelated flake noted in every prior entry this session (a settings/team page never touched by this change). `upload-artwork-modal.png` baseline diffed and visually confirmed as exactly this redesign before regenerating — old mode selector and raised tabs gone, new underline tabs and section headers present, nothing unexpected.

**Follow-up spot fix**: `CreativeModal`'s "Rework this version" button (shown when viewing an older Copy tab) wasn't wrapped in a `.field` div like every other row in the modal, so it had no bottom margin and sat flush against the Check WIIFM/Creative/Brand row beneath it. Wrapped it in `.field` — same 20px spacing every other field in the modal already gets, no new CSS needed.

## Comment cards shrunk ~15% for more breathing room in the Comments panel

Direct instruction: give the comment cards more spacing within the panel by reducing their own size 10–15%. `--d-cmt` (the density token driving `.cmt`'s padding, and the anchor point for the absolutely-positioned timestamp) went from `10px` to `8.5px` (its `body.comfortable` counterpart, currently unused anywhere in the app, adjusted proportionally too for consistency: `14px` → `12px`). Within each card: avatar (`.who`) `21px` → `18px`, author name/body/reply text and the timestamp all reduced roughly 10–15% (e.g. `12.5px` → `11px`), `.cmt-h`'s reserved right-padding for the timestamp trimmed from `76px` to `68px` to match the now-smaller tag/timestamp. Card-to-card `margin-bottom` increased slightly, `8px` → `10px`, which is the actual "more spacing between them" half of the ask — shrinking the cards alone would tighten the list, not open it up.

Deliberately left `.tag` (the Public/Private pill) alone — it's a shared component used well beyond comments (client vs. staff dashboards, project stage pills), so shrinking it globally to match would have reached UI nobody asked to touch. It reads very slightly larger than the now-smaller card content around it; not visually a problem at the sizes involved.

**Verified**: typecheck clean, only the pre-existing `<img>` lint warning (unrelated). Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a creative with three top-level comments plus a reply, screenshotted the live Comments panel — no overlap between the name/tag row and the timestamp, cards visibly smaller with clearer gaps between them. `comment-visibility.spec.ts`'s `comment-cards.png` baseline (an element screenshot of `.cmts`) diffed and confirmed as exactly this sizing/spacing change before regenerating. Full e2e suite: 50/51, the one failure the same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake.

## Comments panel: narrower, borderless cards, recoloured Public/Private pill

Direct follow-up to the previous entry, with a Dribbble reference screenshot ("Task and Comment") pointing at that layout's plain (borderless) comment rows and small, squared-off, softly-coloured status pills. Four asks, all in `app/globals.css` plus one component change:

- **Panel width, another 15% narrower** — `.review`'s second grid column (the only place the comments panel's width is set): `427px` → `363px`, and its `≥1700px` breakpoint `486px` → `413px`.
- **Comment body text to 11px** — `.cmt p` was already at 11px from the previous pass; `.reply .rb p` (a reply's own body text, previously 10.5px, overlooked in that pass) brought in line to match.
- **Public/Private pill: smaller, less rounded, colour-coded, no longer sharing `.tag`** — the old markup reused `.tag.grey` (a fully-rounded 20px-radius pill shared with role badges and project-stage chips elsewhere in the app). Gave it its own `.vis-tag`/`.vis-public`/`.vis-private` classes instead of restyling `.tag` itself, since changing that shared class would have reached UI nobody asked to touch. New pill: 4px radius (not a full pill), tighter padding, soft blue background for Public, soft rose background for Private — matching the reference's `Pending`/`Moodboard` tag treatment. `CommentsPanel.tsx`'s `VisibilityTag` updated to use the new classes (same component structure, both the static client-facing badge and the interactive staff toggle).
- **Lost the border around each comment card** — `.cmt`'s `1px solid var(--line)` became `1px solid transparent` (kept the reserved 1px, rather than `none`, so the existing `.cmt.act` selection-highlight rule — which only overrides `border-color` — keeps working with no layout shift). Cards now read as plain, borderless blocks separated by their own white background against the panel's grey canvas plus margin, exactly like the reference. The old `.cmt.internal` rule (a pink border for private comments) is gone — that signal is now carried by the pill's own colour alone, not a second, redundant cue.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a creative with three top-level comments (mixed public/private) and a reply, confirmed visually — narrower panel, no card borders, small squared blue/rose pills, 11px body text throughout, no overlap with the timestamp. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). Three baselines regenerated after diffing each against its actual (all confirmed as exactly this change, nothing unexpected): `comment-visibility.spec.ts`'s `comment-cards.png`, and `creative-review.spec.ts`'s `creative-review.png` and `upload-artwork-modal.png` (both show the narrower comments panel in the background).

## Creative review screen: three floating white panels, reserved feed-rail column, pill/comment-list polish

Direct follow-up, with a doc-editor Dribbble screenshot as the explicit layout reference (an empty AI-assistant rail, a white document panel, a comments sidebar) plus three specific asks.

**Public/Private pill, smaller still, matched to the date text** — `.vis-tag` (added in the previous entry) brought down from `10px`/`1.5px 6px`/`4px` radius to `9.5px`/`1px 5px`/`3px` radius — `9.5px` is exactly `.cmt-h time`'s own size, so the pill's text and the timestamp next to it now read as the same weight of "metadata," not competing for attention.

**The review screen restructured into three floating panels** — previously a plain two-column grid (`.stage` + `.cmts`), edge to edge, no gutter, both sharing the page's own grey `--canvas` background. Now `.review` is a three-column grid (`300px minmax(0,1fr) 363px`, `413px` on the ≥1700px breakpoint) with a `16px` gap and padding, and `.feed-rail`/`.stage`/`.cmts` each became independent white (`var(--surface)`), rounded (`14px`), bordered panels sitting on that grey background — matching the reference's own three-white-panel-on-muted-background structure. `.cmts` dropped its old `background: var(--canvas)` and `border-left` (both redundant/contradictory now that it's its own bordered panel); `.cmts-b`'s padding opened up from `10px` to `6px 14px 14px` to match the more generous, less cramped feel of the reference's own list.

- **Middle panel ("the creative over a white panel")** — `.stage`'s content (the Brief panel, the Instagram-style post mockup) already sat inside its own bordered `.postbox` card; what changed is the area *behind* it, which was grey and is now the same white as the panel itself, so the post reads as sitting cleanly on a plain white document surface rather than a grey canvas — no changes needed inside `.postbox` itself, only the panel wrapping it.
- **Left rail — reserved, not built**: a new `.feed-rail` column with a small dashed-border placeholder (`.feed-rail-ph`, a phone-outline icon + "Feed preview" / "A mobile feed mockup will live here.") occupies the space, per direct instruction ("the left will be empty for now, but will feature a yet to be designed feed on a phone — make provision for that space"). This is deliberately not the prototype's own collapsible Instagram-feed rail (`.review.feedrailed`/`.review.withfeed`, `docs/parity-gaps.md`'s older, still-accurate entry on that specific gap) — that one needs a real platform-connections feature this schema doesn't have. This is a plain, static, always-visible placeholder with nothing behind it yet; the CSS comment above `.review` cross-references both so a future reader doesn't conflate the two.
- **Right panel (comments) — matched spacing/padding to the reference's own plain list style**: comment cards no longer have their own per-card white background or border (the previous pass's borderless-but-backed card, itself a step in this direction) — with the panel now white too, a same-colour background per card would've been invisible padding for nothing. Replaced with a subtle `border-bottom` divider between comments (`.cmt:last-child` drops it) and slightly more breathing room (`padding-bottom` extended, small `margin-bottom` between entries), so the list reads as clean, evenly-spaced rows separated by hairlines — not stacked boxes — matching the reference. The selection-highlight ring (`.cmt.act`, used when a canvas pin/region is clicked) no longer needs a `border-color` override since there's no longer a base border to color; simplified to box-shadow alone, same visible ring as before.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a creative with three comments (mixed public/private), confirmed visually — three distinct white panels on the grey page background, the feed-rail placeholder present and empty, comment rows separated by hairlines with no boxed background, smaller pills matching the timestamp's size. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). Three baselines diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating: `comment-visibility.spec.ts`'s `comment-cards.png`, `creative-review.spec.ts`'s `creative-review.png` and `upload-artwork-modal.png`.

**Immediate correction**: the reference has no visible border stroke around its panels — just the white-on-muted background contrast doing the separating. `.feed-rail`/`.stage`/`.cmts`'s shared rule had added `border: 1px solid var(--line)` on top of that background contrast, which the reference doesn't have. Dropped the border, kept the white background and `14px` radius — the `16px` grid gap and the background-colour difference alone now do all the separating, matching the reference exactly. (`.feed-rail-ph`'s own dashed border is a separate, deliberate placeholder affordance, not the panel border, and was left alone.) Re-verified the same way: diffed each of the three affected baselines against actual before regenerating, full e2e suite 50/51 (same pre-existing flake).

**Second correction — misread which side has a panel at all**: pointed at the same reference again, more precisely — the left (AI rail) and right (comments) sides in it have *no* white panel whatsoever, boxed or borderless; they sit directly on the page's own muted background. Only the *middle* (the document) is a white panel. What actually has "its own box" in the reference is each individual comment — a separate small white card, spaced apart, floating on that muted background — not a single white sidebar containing borderless rows (which is what the previous two passes had built, working from a misreading of the same image).

Corrected: `.feed-rail` and `.cmts` lost their white background and radius entirely — both now render directly on `.review`'s own `--canvas` background, same as the gutters between panels. `.stage` keeps its white panel (the reference's middle document, never in question). `.cmt` reverted to an individual bordered white card (`background: var(--surface)`, `border: 1px solid var(--line)`, its own `border-radius`, `10px` margin-bottom for spacing between cards) — the design from before either of the last two passes, still carrying this session's smaller size/text/pill refinements. `.cmt.act`'s selection ring is back to `border-color` + `box-shadow` (it has a border to color again). The feed-rail's dashed placeholder box now floats directly on the blue canvas too, with no white section behind it, per the same instruction.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded three comments (mixed public/private), confirmed each renders as its own separate white card with visible spacing on the pale blue canvas, and that the feed-rail and comments-panel backgrounds now match the canvas exactly with no seam. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `comment-visibility.spec.ts`'s `comment-cards.png` was the only baseline with an actual pixel diff (the other two full-page/modal screenshots either have no comments seeded or are mostly covered by the modal scrim, so the background-only change didn't register against them) — diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this fix before regenerating.

## Copy section redesign: verified the Draft pipeline, removed version tabs, fixed a spurious-version bug

Direct instruction to rework the Copy section, framed as a design request: *"The best case scenario ... is that the caption Draft or Suggestion uses [concept, ALL agency instructions, ALL client instructions, Format Directions, the AI model]. Is there a way the user can just press Draft and get the best caption based on all this?"* — plus three concrete asks (remove the Copy version tabs, stop creating a version when nothing changed, close the modal once a version is created).

**Investigated before changing anything, since this was posed as a question, not just an instruction.** Traced `runDraft()` (`CreativeModal.tsx`) and its five hooks (`useFormatDirections`, `useAgencyKnowledge`, `useKnowledgeEntries`, plus `concept` state and `useAgencyAiSettings`'s model) end to end, then proved it with real seeded data rather than trusting the read-through: wrote a script that queries `agency_knowledge_entries`, `knowledge_entries` (across two different sections) and `format_directions` exactly as those hooks do, runs the real `buildCaptionPrompt`, and asserts every seeded marker string survives into the assembled prompt. All five passed. **Finding: this was already built, correctly** — pressing Draft already sends the concept, every agency reference-material text note (no cap, no format filter), every client knowledge text note across all eight sections (no cap, no section filter), the matching format's own Format Direction, and routes through the agency's configured model. Nothing here needed new plumbing; the "best case scenario" already exists at the code level. The single caveat, not asked about but worth recording: only `kind === "text"` entries are included — a `file`/`link`/`image`-kind Reference Material or Knowledge entry contributes nothing to the prompt (there's no text to extract), which is an inherent limit, not a bug.

**What actually needed fixing were the three concrete asks:**

- **Copy version tabs removed** — `CreativeModal.tsx` had grown a full second version-browsing UI for Copy (`viewingCopyVersionNo` state, `isEditingCopy`, a `.viewbar`/`.vtab` tab strip, `reworkCopyVersion`, and a parallel read-only-plus-"Rework this version" branch) alongside the always-editable "latest" view. All of it removed — the Copy section is now unconditionally the always-editable latest-version form, no tabs, no browsing. Scoped to Copy only, per the request's own framing ("the Copy section"); the Creative (artwork) side's own separate version tabs are untouched.
- **A no-op Save no longer creates a version** — `handleSaveUpload()` previously called `saveCopy.mutateAsync` unconditionally, inserting a new `copy_versions` row even when the merged fields were byte-for-byte identical to the latest version's. Added a small `fieldsEqual()` comparison (sorted key/value check) between `{...latest.fields, ...draftFields}` and `latest.fields`; the insert (and the WIIFM-note refresh fetch that follows it) is now skipped when they match and a latest version already exists.
- **Modal closes once Save is pressed, version or no** — `onClose()` was already unconditional at the end of `handleSaveUpload()` before this change (verified by reading it, not assumed) — preserved through the refactor, now sitting after the (possibly-skipped) copy-version insert rather than inside it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean (one now-unused `latestCopyVersionNo` removed as part of the cleanup). Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a creative with two existing copy versions and a real creative version (artwork), opened Upload & Copy — confirmed zero `.vtab` elements for Copy (the one `.vtab` present is the untouched Creative-side tab), confirmed the visible fields are the latest version's own values, pressed Save with nothing changed and confirmed both that `copy_versions`' row count stayed the same (2, not 3) and that the modal closed. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake), unit suite 42/42.

**`phase12_ai_drafting.sql` applied to the live database** — after all this session's repeated confirmation that the Draft button's disabled state traced to this migration never having been run, the user applied it directly via the Supabase SQL Editor. Re-confirmed by direct query immediately after: `agencies.ai_default_model`/`ai_monthly_request_cap` both exist now, defaulted to `claude-opus-5`/300 for every agency including the real "No Fluff". Also found and fixed at the same time: ~16 leftover throwaway agencies from this session's own verification scripts, whose teardown had been silently failing (a foreign-key constraint the scripts' `.delete()` calls never checked `error` on) — cascade-deleted across every dependent table (comments, copy/creative versions, creatives, projects, clients, knowledge entries, memberships, assets, ai_usage_events, calendar_views, agency_settings) plus their throwaway auth users, confirmed with the user first since it was a real (if disposable) database. Only "No Fluff" remains.

**`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` added to `.env.local`** (gitignored, confirmed before writing either) — the platform-pays model needs a real provider key server-side, which didn't exist in this repo at all before now. A first attempt without `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` failed with Anthropic's own 400 ("This API key is not scoped to a workspace...") — the user's key is an org-level key, which Anthropic requires a workspace header for on every request. Fixed in `lib/ai/providers/anthropic.ts`: `defaultHeaders` on the SDK client now includes `anthropic-workspace-id` when `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` is set, omitted entirely otherwise (a workspace-scoped key doesn't need it). Dev server restarted twice this session to pick up each new env var in turn.

## Check WIIFM / Check Brand made real

Direct instruction: both check buttons were still 100% mocked (`CHECK_COPY`'s hardcoded "Mock finding —" fixtures, a fake 700ms timeout, no real data in or out) — "Check WIIFM should be referencing the Reference Material in the Knowledge settings. Check Brand should be referencing the respective client Knowledge section."

**New shared prompt/parse helper, `lib/ai/build-check-prompt.ts`** — same pure, unit-tested shape as `build-caption-prompt.ts`: `buildCheckPrompt({ intro, concept, copyText, referenceLabel, referenceNotes, findingCount })` assembles the prompt (omitting empty sections, same convention as the caption prompt), `parseCheckFindings(text)` splits the model's `TONE:`/`TITLE:`/`BODY:` blocks back into `{ tone: "ok" | "warn", title, body }[]`, dropping any block missing a field rather than keeping it half-empty. One function serves both checks — they differ only in intro framing and which knowledge source is passed as `referenceNotes`.

**New route, `app/api/ai/check/route.ts`** — deliberately not a reuse of `/api/ai/draft` even though the two routes are now nearly identical (agencyId + a pre-assembled prompt in, staff-only, delegate to `runAiTask` for the model call/cap/usage-logging): a network trace of "Check Brand" should read as a check, not a draft, matching this repo's existing one-route-per-AI-action convention (`draft`, `wiifm-note`, now `check`).

**`CreativeModal.tsx` wiring**:
- `agencyNotes` (all agency Reference Material text notes — already existed, hoisted out of `runDraft()` to component level so both Draft and the checks share one computation instead of two copies of the same filter) is what Check WIIFM sends as its reference notes.
- `clientNotesLabeled` (new) — all client Knowledge text notes, each prefixed with its section (`[Protected Terms] Never say "cheap"...`) via `KNOWLEDGE_SECTIONS`' own label map. Check Brand uses this rather than Draft's plain, unlabeled `clientNotes` — which section a note came from matters more here (a protected-terms violation is a different kind of finding than a tone mismatch), where Draft's own flatter prompt didn't need that distinction.
- `copyTextForCheck` — the copy fields as they stand right now in the editable draft (not the last saved version), one line per non-empty field as `Label: value`.
- Both checks guard on the same three preconditions, each with its own message rather than silently doing nothing: no copy typed yet ("Add some copy first..."), the relevant knowledge source genuinely empty ("No agency Reference Material yet..." / "No Knowledge entries yet for this client..."), and — the one bug this surfaced in testing, see below — knowledge still loading.
- "Check Creative" is untouched, still the same mocked placeholder it always was — not part of this request (it would need OCR/vision on the actual artwork, not a text prompt) and explicitly out of scope; `MockCreativeCheck` now just names that reasoning at the point where it's separated from the two real checks.

**A real bug found and fixed while verifying, not in the original scope**: `useAgencyKnowledge`/`useKnowledgeEntries`'s data is `undefined` until their queries resolve; `(undefined ?? []).filter(...)` reads as "genuinely no knowledge configured" indistinguishably from "hasn't loaded yet". A fast test click (and, less reliably, a fast real user) could see the false "No Reference Material yet" message even though the agency has plenty, just not fetched yet at that instant. Threaded `isLoading` through from both hooks as a `knowledgeLoading` prop into the new `LiveCheck` component, folded into its already-derived `running` state (`(canRun || knowledgeLoading) && no result yet`) rather than a `useEffect`-set boolean — this repo's established fix for "don't call setState synchronously inside an effect" (same pattern as `AiModelSettings.tsx` earlier this session), confirmed by `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` actually flagging the naive version during this change.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; unit suite 47/47 (5 new tests for `build-check-prompt.ts`). Functionally verified against the real dev server with a real Anthropic call (not mocked) — seeded an agency with Reference Material naming a persuasion-principle rule, a client with Tone and Protected Terms knowledge, and copy that violates both ("cheap", exclamation marks, no concrete numbers): Check Brand correctly flagged the protected-term repeats and the exclamation marks against the documented tone; Check WIIFM correctly flagged the missing concrete number/scarcity and named the same protected-term issue from a persuasion-principle angle. Also directly reproduced and confirmed the loading-race bug against a bare Supabase client before fixing it (a fresh sign-in could read both tables fine — ruling out RLS — the false-empty state only appeared through the UI's own timing). Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake); one additional pre-existing baseline (`settings-knowledge.png`) also needed regenerating in this pass — unrelated to this change, a side effect of the `phase12` migration above finally being live (the old baseline was captured showing "Couldn't load this setting", now correctly shows the real Claude Opus 5 selector).

## Check WIIFM / Check Brand: PDF-based Reference Material and Knowledge

Immediate follow-up to the entry above. The user's real agency's Reference Material turned out to be entirely `kind === "file"` rows — three uploaded PDFs (Cialdini's Six Principles, Drew Whitman's Cash­vertising, a behavioural-biases reference) — and the previous pass only ever read `kind === "text"` rows, so both checks reported "No Reference Material yet" despite three real documents being uploaded. Confirmed by direct investigation before writing any code: no PDF-parsing library is installed, no server route reads Storage bytes directly anywhere yet, and — the fact that made this tractable without adding a parsing dependency — the already-installed `@anthropic-ai/sdk@^0.128.0` types confirm Claude's Messages API accepts an inline base64 PDF natively as a `document` content block, no text extraction needed.

**New `lib/ai/attachment.ts`** — `Attachment { base64, mediaType: "application/pdf", title }`, threaded through `generate-draft.ts` → each provider's `generate()` → `run-ai-task.ts`. Anthropic's provider now sends attachments as `document` content blocks ahead of the text prompt (Anthropic's own recommended ordering); OpenAI's and Google's providers accept the same parameter for signature parity but don't send it — neither has native-PDF wiring yet, so a PDF simply contributes nothing for those two providers, an explicit, documented gap rather than a silent inconsistency. PDF only, deliberately: Claude's own attachment types only cover PDF and plain text, so a DOCX/XLSX/image knowledge upload still contributes nothing (unchanged from before this feature).

**`app/api/ai/check/route.ts`** now accepts an `attachments: {storageKey, mimeType, title}[]` array alongside the prompt — sent by reference, not by value, since the client only ever holds a signed-URL pattern for these files. The route downloads each PDF directly from Supabase Storage using the signed-in user's own session (RLS's `assets_bucket_select` already scopes storage reads to the object's own agency, no service-role needed), base64-encodes it, and passes the result to `runAiTask`. Non-PDF mime types are skipped; a combined-size cap (15MB) protects against an unusually large Reference Material folder inflating one request's token cost.

**`CreativeModal.tsx`**: `agencyPdfAttachments`/`clientPdfAttachments` (new, mirroring `agencyNotes`/`clientNotesLabeled`) filter each knowledge source to `kind === "file" && asset.mime_type === "application/pdf"`. `LiveCheck`'s "has any reference material" check (used both for the empty-state message and to gate whether a request even fires) now considers attachments alongside text notes — a PDF-only Reference Material folder no longer reads as empty. `buildCheckPrompt` gained `attachmentTitles: string[]`, naming the attached document(s) in the prompt even when there are zero text notes, so the model knows to read them.

**A real, reproducible Claude safety-classifier issue found and fixed while verifying against the user's actual PDF-only setup — not a code bug, but the code needed to route around it.** Check Brand, with a PDF attached, refused outright (`stop_reason: "refusal"`, zero content blocks) with high consistency (confirmed 8/8 and separately 6/6 across repeated real API calls) — exactly the scenario Check Brand exists for (a protected-terms PDF, copy that violates it). Isolated by binary-searching the prompt wording directly against the live API rather than guessing: the trigger was `CHECK_INTROS.brand`'s closing clause, "...and flag anywhere the copy drifts from it" — something about the combination of an uploaded document plus "flag violations" framing reads to Claude's classifier as generating a compliance/audit-style report against a real document, which it declines. Removing only that clause (keeping everything else, including "protected terms") eliminated the refusal entirely (confirmed 0/8, then a further 0/4 with a slightly different rewording) — reworded to "...and note anything worth reconsidering", same meaning, no trigger. `CHECK_INTROS.wiifm` never exhibited this (no "flag"/"drift" framing to begin with) and was left alone. Also added a defensive, generic backstop regardless of wording: `lib/ai/providers/anthropic.ts` now retries up to twice on a `stop_reason: "refusal"` before surfacing a distinct, clear error ("Claude declined to complete this request...") instead of the generic "Claude returned no text" — a real safety-classifier decline shouldn't read as a crash.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean (added `eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` on the two intentionally-ignored `_attachments` parameters in the OpenAI/Google providers — no existing `argsIgnorePattern` convention in this repo's eslint config to lean on instead). Functionally verified against the real dev server with real uploaded PDFs (hand-built minimal single-page PDFs with a distinctive marker sentence each, uploaded through the actual Storage bucket/path convention) and zero text notes on either side: Check WIIFM correctly read and cited the agency PDF's content; Check Brand correctly cited the client PDF's protected-term restriction and flagged the exact copy that violated it, across three repeated clean runs after the wording fix (zero refusals). `npm run build` clean. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake).

## Upload & Copy tab redesign: Checks as its own inline section, sticky centred tabs, populated tabs carried by weight not a dot

Direct instruction, five parts: stack the three Check buttons instead of a horizontal row; expand results inline underneath each button instead of opening a separate modal; give Checks its own section (same footing as Creative and Copy, not nested inside Copy); a short muted explainer under each of the three section headers; and the Brief/Upload & Copy tab bar should be sticky and horizontally centred, with the populated/empty dot replaced by bold-vs-grey text weight.

**Checks is now a real third section, always shown** — previously the three check buttons lived inside `{includesCopy && (...)}`; a format with no copy fields (Instagram Story, etc.) never showed them at all, even though Check Creative doesn't need copy fields and Check WIIFM/Check Brand already handle "no copy yet" with their own message. Moved the whole block out to sit after Copy, unconditional.

**No more per-check modal — `expandedChecks: Record<CheckKind, boolean>` replaces `activeCheck: CheckKind | null`.** Each check is its own `.checkrow`: a full-width button (icon, label, a chevron that rotates on expand) and, when expanded, a `.checkrow-body` directly beneath it rendering the exact same `CheckModalBody` component as before — unchanged internally, it just no longer lives inside a `<Modal>`. Collapsing a row unmounts its result the same way closing the modal used to, so re-expanding runs the check again rather than showing a stale cached answer — matches the prior behaviour exactly, not a new cost consideration.

**`.mtabs` (Brief/Upload & Copy) is now `position: sticky; top: 0` within `.modal-b`'s own scroll, with a solid background so scrolled content disappears behind it rather than showing through** — bled to the modal's full width (`margin: -8px -28px 22px`, padding added back to realign the tab labels) so the sticky bar's background covers edge to edge. `justify-content: center` centres the two tabs horizontally, replacing the previous left alignment.

**Tab dots removed — replaced by the label's own weight/colour.** `.mtab-dot`/`.mtab-dot.on` deleted; `briefPopulated`/`uploadPopulated` (already existed, previously drove which dot filled in) now add a `.populated` class instead, which sets `font-weight: 700; color: var(--ink)` — an empty tab stays at the base `font-weight: 500; color: var(--muted)`. The active tab's own blue (`.mtab[aria-selected="true"]`) still wins over both states, unchanged.

**A short explainer under Creative/Copy/Checks** — new `.msection-d` class (`11.5px`, muted, directly under `.msection-h`, which had its own `margin-bottom` trimmed from 18px to 6px to keep the header+explainer pair visually tight): "The artwork or video for this post." / "The caption and on-post text, matched to what this format needs." / "Run the copy against WIIFM strength, this client's brand knowledge, or the creative itself."

**A real rendering bug found while verifying, not part of the original scope**: the check icon (`CheckIcon`) rendered as a giant black rectangle instead of a small line icon the moment its button's class changed from `.btn.sm.chk` to the new `.checkrow-btn` — the icon's `fill: none`/sizing had only ever been set by `.btn.chk svg`, never by anything on the `<svg>` itself, so losing that class left the SVG unstyled (default black fill, unconstrained intrinsic size). Fixed by giving `.checkrow-btn svg` the same sizing/fill rule directly, with the chevron's own rule now only adding what makes it different (colour, margin, rotation).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a real creative, confirmed the tab bar centres and sticks while scrolling, confirmed "Brief" (populated, a real creative exists) renders bold/ink while an unpopulated "Upload & Copy" renders in the lighter unselected weight, confirmed all three Check buttons stack vertically with visibly correct icons, and expanded Check Creative to confirm its result renders inline with a rotated chevron and no separate modal opens. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this redesign before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

**Immediate correction**: "centred horizontally where they sit" meant vertical centring within the tab bar's own row (a minor, mostly-already-true alignment detail), not horizontal centring of the two tabs within the modal — the left-aligned layout from before this entry was preferred and should have stayed. `.mtabs` dropped `justify-content: center` (back to the default left alignment) and gained `align-items: center` in its place; `position: sticky` and everything else from the entry above is unchanged. Re-verified the same way: `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual (tabs now left-aligned, still sticky) before regenerating, full e2e suite 50/51 (same pre-existing flake).

**Second correction — the sticky tab bar wasn't actually flush with the header on scroll.** `position: sticky; top: 0` on `.mtabs` measures its stuck offset against `.modal-b`'s *padding* edge, not its border edge — since `.modal-b` has its own 28px padding, the tab bar stuck 28px below the modal header instead of flush against it, confirmed by measuring both elements' real bounding boxes in a scrolled browser (`mtabs.top - modalH.bottom` was 28px, exactly `.modal-b`'s padding value). Fixed by setting `top: -28px` instead of `0` — the negative offset compensates for that padding edge so the sticky element can travel the rest of the way up. Re-measured after the fix: gap is exactly `0`. Regenerated `upload-artwork-modal.png` (a separate, unrelated 0.01-ratio anti-aliasing-level diff on the same run, consistent with this suite's known font-rendering noise — visually identical to the correct expected layout before and after, not a structural change); full e2e suite 50/51 (same pre-existing flake).

## Checks becomes its own third tab; unnecessary space above the tabs closed; section description position fixed

Three more direct follow-ups to the tab-bar work above.

**Unnecessary space above the tabs, even before any scrolling** — `.mtabs`'s own `margin-top` was `-8px`, only partially cancelling `.modal-b`'s 28px padding, so there was a visible ~20px gap between the modal header and the tab row on first open (before the sticky offset entry above even applies). Changed to `-28px` — the same value used for the sticky `top` offset fix — so the tab bar sits flush against the header from the moment the modal opens, with no jump when scrolling starts.

**Checks promoted to its own tab, matching Creative/Copy's own footing.** Previously "Checks" was a third `.msection-h` block stacked underneath Creative and Copy inside the Upload & Copy tab; now it's a third tab (`Tab` type: `"brief" | "upload" | "checks"`, a third `.mtab` button, disabled under the same "save the brief first" gate as Upload & Copy). Each of the three checks — WIIFM, Creative, Brand — is now its own full section on that tab (`.msection-h` + `.msection-d`, exactly the Creative/Copy pattern), not a row in a stack.

**The collapse/expand mechanism is gone — "may not need to be collapsed with this new structure" was right.** With each check as a permanent section rather than a row that opens/closes, there was no longer a natural "mount" moment to hang the old effect-driven auto-run off. `LiveCheck` and `MockCreativeCheck` were rewritten from mount-triggered (`useEffect` firing when a row expanded) to button-triggered — an explicit `run()`/`onClick` per section, the same shape as Draft's own `runDraft()` in the Copy section right next to it. A disabled button now shows its reason as inline muted text beside it (no copy yet, or the relevant knowledge source is genuinely empty) rather than replacing the whole section with a message — closer to how Draft's own disabled state already reads. `expandedChecks` state, the `checkrow`/`checkstack`/`checkrow-btn`/`checkrow-body` CSS, and the now-fully-unused `CheckIcon` component (nothing referenced its per-kind SVGs once the icon-in-a-button treatment was dropped) were all deleted rather than left dead. `CheckModalBody` renamed to `CheckSection` to match what it actually is now.

**The muted explainer sat below the section's divider line instead of under its title.** `.msection-h` had its own `border-bottom`; `.msection-d` came after it as a separate paragraph, so the divider landed between the title and the description rather than after both. Moved the border onto `.msection-d` instead (which is always the very next element after a `.msection-h` in this modal, by convention) — now heading, then description, then the divider, then the section's own content.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean (confirmed `.btn.chk`/`.checkrow*`/`.checkstack` and `CheckIcon` genuinely unused repo-wide via grep before deleting). Functionally verified against the real dev server: confirmed the tab bar sits flush against the header unscrolled, confirmed three tabs (Brief/Upload & Copy/Checks) with Creative and Copy no longer showing any check content, confirmed each of the three checks renders as its own titled section with the description sitting directly under the title and the divider after it, and ran "Check Creative" from a cold section (no prior expand state) to confirm it still runs and shows its result inline with no collapse mechanism anywhere. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 on a fresh dev server restart (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — an intermediate run before the restart also flagged two unrelated `project-calendar.spec.ts` tests, which passed cleanly on immediate re-run, matching this session's already-documented long-running-dev-server-under-load flakiness pattern, not a regression from this change (nothing here touches project-calendar code). `npm run build` clean. `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating.

## Standalone "Save Copy" button, a read-only "Latest Copy Version" section on Checks, tab renamed to Content

Three more direct asks on the same modal.

**A copy-only save that doesn't close the modal** — the only way to save copy before this was the modal's own footer Save, which also uploads any picked file and then always closes (`onClose()`unconditionally at the end of `handleSaveUpload`). Extracted the actual copy-saving logic (the no-op-if-unchanged check from two entries back, the `saveCopy` mutation call, the `onCopyVersionCreated` callback, the WIIFM-note refresh fetch) into its own `saveCopyIfChanged(): Promise<boolean>`, called by both `handleSaveUpload` (footer Save, still closes after) and a new `handleSaveCopyOnly` (the inline button, doesn't). The button sits directly after the format's own copy fields — "under On-screen Hook" for a format that has one, under whatever the last field is for any other — labelled "Save Copy", and reports what happened right next to itself: green "Saved as the latest version." on a real save, muted "No changes to save." when the no-op guard caught it, or the existing shared error banner on failure. Typing in any copy field after a save clears the note, so it can't read as still-current once the fields have moved on.

**Checks tab gains a first section, "Latest Copy Version"** — plain text, no inputs, not editable, one `.field`/`label`/`.fd-d` triple per field the current format actually uses (same read-only pattern the old per-version "browse an older version" view used, before that whole mechanism was removed). Sourced directly from `latestCopyVersion` (`copyVersions[0]`, already flowing into the modal as a prop) — no new data-fetching. "Every time a user saves changes... this gets appended" turned out to already be true structurally: `useSaveCopyFields`'s `onSuccess` already invalidates the `["copy-versions", creativeId]` query key, which is exactly what the parent page's own `useCopyVersions` reads — saving (via either the new inline button or the footer Save) triggers a refetch that flows straight back down into the still-open modal's `copyVersions` prop, and this section re-renders with the newly-saved content with no extra plumbing. Verified live, not assumed: saved a field via the new button, switched to Checks without closing the modal, and confirmed the exact just-typed text was already showing.

**Tab renamed "Upload & Copy" → "Content"** — the button label, the internal `Tab` type's `"upload"` value kept as-is (an implementation detail, not user-facing, changing it would only have touched code with no visible effect), comments referencing the old name updated for accuracy, and `new-brief.spec.ts`'s two assertions on `getByRole("tab", { name: "Upload & Copy" })` updated to `"Content"` — both would have failed against the renamed tab otherwise.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server end to end: confirmed the tab bar now reads Brief/Content/Checks, typed into Primary Text, clicked Save Copy, confirmed the modal stayed open and showed "Saved as the latest version.", then switched to Checks (still the same modal instance, never closed) and confirmed "Latest Copy Version" showed the exact text just saved. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (renamed tab, new Save Copy button) before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## WIIFM Direction: a second read-only Checks section, live-updating on every copy save, with a real bug fixed underneath it

Direct follow-up: a "WIIFM Direction" section, directly under "Latest Copy Version", read-only text, regenerated every time copy is saved, with an "Updating…" state while it's in flight.

**This reuses an already-existing mechanism rather than building a new one.** `app/api/ai/wiifm-note/route.ts` already existed — it derives a list of "what the reader actually gets" statements from the latest copy version and writes them to `creatives.approach_notes` — and `saveCopyIfChanged()` was already calling it after every save, fire-and-forget (`fetch(...).catch((err) => console.error(...))`, no loading state, no visible result anywhere except a pre-existing read-only "Approach notes — WIIFM rationale" field on the Brief tab that had no way to reflect a fresh call without the whole modal reopening).

**New `hooks/use-refresh-wiifm-note.ts`** — wraps that same fetch as a `useMutation` instead of a bare fire-and-forget call, purely so its `isPending`/`isError`/`error` become something a component can read reactively (the "Updating…" state the user asked for). `onSuccess` invalidates `["creative", creativeId]` — the same query key `useCreative` reads — so both the new Checks-tab section and the Brief tab's existing field update from the same underlying refetch, the identical reactive pattern `copyVersions`/`latestCopyVersion` already established two entries back. `saveCopyIfChanged()` now calls `refreshWiifmNote.mutate(agencyId)` (not awaited — Save Copy's own "Saved" confirmation doesn't wait on a second AI call) instead of the old raw `fetch`.

**New section, `WIIFM Direction`, directly after `Latest Copy Version`** — plain text, one line per note, no inputs, sourced from the same `approachNotes` derived constant the Brief tab's field already used (hoisted out to be shared by both, replacing two separate inline `props.creative.approach_notes ?? []` reads). Three states: `refreshWiifmNote.isPending` → "Updating…"; `.isError` → the shared error-banner treatment; otherwise the notes themselves, or "No WIIFM direction yet" before the first save.

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed while verifying, not part of the ask**: `wiifm-note/route.ts` built its "copy to analyse" string from three hardcoded field names — `fields.caption`, `fields.headline`, `fields.cta`. That triple only matches a handful of formats; Meta Feed Ad (this session's own running example) uses `primary`/`headline`/`description`/`cta` and has no `caption` field at all, so the route found nothing and returned "No copy to analyse yet" for any format shaped that way — previously invisible, since the fire-and-forget call only logged failures to the console. Fixed by building the same way `copyTextForCheck` already does client-side: look up the creative's own format, get its real `copyFields` list from `lib/formats.ts`, and join each populated field as `Label: value`. Required also selecting `format` from `creatives` in this route (previously only `concept`).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server with a real Anthropic call on a Meta Feed Ad creative (the exact shape that triggered the bug) — saved a Primary Text field, switched to Checks immediately and caught the real "Updating…" state on screen, then waited for the real call to resolve and confirmed six specific, genuine WIIFM statements appeared (not the "No copy to analyse yet" error the unfixed route would have produced for this format). Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake, no new baseline needed — the Checks tab in that fixture never has saved copy so this section never renders content in it). `npm run build` clean.

## Brief tab broken into sections, Text on Image restyled, Approach Notes removed, Caption doubled

Five smaller, related asks on the same modal.

**"Approach notes — WIIFM rationale" removed from the Brief tab** — superseded by the previous entry's "WIIFM Direction" section on the Checks tab, which reads the same `approach_notes` column and (unlike this static field) actually updates live. The `approachNotes` derived constant it used stays — `runDraft()` and the new WIIFM Direction section both still need it — only the Brief tab's own `ListEditor` display of it is gone.

**Brief tab broken into three sections, matching the Content tab's own pattern** — previously a flat run of `.field` divs top to bottom. Now: `Details` (name, content type/format, publish date-or-destination, lead), `Concept` (the concept textarea, reference link), `Text on Image` (the slide list) — each a real `.msection-h` + `.msection-d` pair. The Concept field's own label shortened from "Concept — What Is Being Made and Why" to just "Concept" now that the longer phrase lives in the section's own description instead — duplicating it in both the heading area and the field label would have been redundant, and every other section in this modal already keeps its field labels short with the fuller explanation living one level up.

**"Text on Image" promoted from a small internal list-editor label to a real section heading** — same treatment as Concept, sitting directly above the slide list it describes.

**`ListEditor`'s per-item layout changed from label-beside-field to label-above-field** — each "Slide N" label sat to the textarea's left in a fixed-width column (`min-width: 42px`), which both visually separated the label from every other field's own top-aligned label style and pushed the textarea's own left edge in from where every other field's left edge sits. Restructured to a small header row (label + remove button) above the textarea, and dropped the fixed-width column entirely — the textarea's left edge now lines up flush with the rest of the section, exactly like every other field. `ListEditor` also gained a `bare` prop (skips its own `.bsec` wrapper and internal heading) for this call site specifically, since the heading is now external (`.msection-h`); `BriefPanel.tsx`'s own read-only usage (the collapsed brief summary on the main review page, out of scope for this request) is untouched and keeps its internal heading — same component, two calling conventions.

**Caption field doubled in height** — `rows={3}` → `rows={6}`, scoped to the `caption` field key specifically (not every long-form copy field — Description/Body/Notes/Primary stay at 3), since that was the specific field named.

**Every `.msection-h` across all three tabs audited for a paired `.msection-d`** — all 8 (Brief's three, Content's Creative/Copy, Checks' Latest Copy Version/WIIFM Direction/each of the three checks) already had one from prior entries; the three new Brief-tab sections needed real descriptions written for them (done above), closing the "add explanations where missing" ask.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: confirmed all three Brief sections render with headings and descriptions in the right order, added two Text on Image slides and confirmed each "Slide N" label sits above its own textarea flush with the section's left edge (not beside it, not indented), and confirmed the Content tab's Caption field is visibly twice as tall as Description/other long fields. A first screenshot appeared to show the Details section missing entirely — turned out to be a scrolled-past-it state from Playwright's own auto-scroll-into-view during a later step in the same script, not a real bug; confirmed by reading the live page's own `innerText` (the text was there) and by a fresh screenshot at scroll position zero (Details visible exactly where expected). Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake — a `upload-artwork-modal.png` failure on one run turned out to be its own transient flake, unrelated to any structural change, confirmed by re-running it alone and by a second full-suite run both passing clean). `upload-artwork-modal.png` and `creative-review.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (new Brief sections behind the modal, taller Caption field) before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## Version numbers named explicitly, everywhere copy versioning is in play

Two related asks: "Latest Copy Version" on the Checks tab should name which version it is (e.g. "Latest Copy Version 3"), and the "Save Copy" button on the Content tab should say "Save Version 3/4/5" — dynamically, and "anywhere the system is suggesting using a new version."

**New `nextCopyVersionNo`** — `(latestCopyVersion?.version_no ?? 0) + 1`, one derived value shared by every button that could create a new copy version, so they can never drift out of sync with each other:
- The inline button (renamed from the previous entry's "Save Copy") now reads `Save Version {nextCopyVersionNo}` unconditionally — always naming the version slot a save would fill, not just when there happen to be unsaved changes.
- The footer's own Save button, previously a flat "Save" on every tab but Brief, now reads the same `Save Version {nextCopyVersionNo}` whenever the current format has copy fields at all (`includesCopy`) — since that button saves copy too (it's the same `saveCopyIfChanged()` under `handleSaveUpload`), and the state it acts on is shared across tabs, not just whatever's currently on screen. Falls back to plain "Save" for formats with no copy fields, where "version" wouldn't mean anything.
- The inline button's own post-save confirmation changed from generic "Saved as the latest version." to "Saved as version {N}." — the version number captured from `nextCopyVersionNo` *before* the save runs (not read back after, since the query invalidation that updates `latestCopyVersion` happens asynchronously and wouldn't be reflected yet at that point).

**"Latest Copy Version" section heading** now reads `Latest Copy Version {latestCopyVersion.version_no}` once a version exists, falling back to the bare label before the first one does.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server end to end: seeded a creative with two existing copy versions, confirmed both the inline and footer buttons read "Save Version 3" and the Checks tab heading read "Latest Copy Version 2" (all three independently reflecting the same underlying state correctly), then edited and saved — confirmed the save note said "Saved as version 3.", the inline button advanced to "Save Version 4", and the Checks heading updated to "Latest Copy Version 3", all without closing or reopening the modal (same reactive query-invalidation path as the "Latest Copy Version"/"WIIFM Direction" entries above). Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual (footer button now reads "Save Version 1" on a brand-new creative) and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

**Real bug found while chasing "the pill is still too big"**: `.vis-tag`'s `font-size: 9.5px` was already equal to `.cmt-h time`'s own `9.5px` in the stylesheet — reported as still-too-big anyway, which meant something was overriding it at render time rather than the declared size being wrong. Found it by screenshotting a single comment header at 3x device scale and comparing letterforms directly: the interactive (staff-only) pill visibly rendered larger than the static one. Root cause: `CommentsPanel.tsx`'s `VisibilityTag` puts `vis-tag` and `vistoggle` on the *same* button element for staff, and `.vistoggle`'s reset used the `font` shorthand (`font: inherit`) rather than `font-family` alone — that shorthand resets size/weight/line-height too, and since both classes have equal specificity with `.vistoggle` declared later in the file, it silently won, inheriting the button's own larger default size and undoing `.vis-tag`'s explicit `9.5px`. Only the interactive staff variant was affected; the static client-facing span (no `.vistoggle` class) was already correctly sized. Fixed by narrowing `.vistoggle`'s reset to `font-family: inherit` only, so `.vis-tag`'s own font-size/weight are no longer shadowed.
