Confirmed with the same zoomed single-element screenshot: "Public" and "just now" now match. `comment-visibility.spec.ts`'s `comment-cards.png` diffed and regenerated (its fixture is a staff session, so this baseline was carrying the bug too); full e2e suite 50/51 (same pre-existing flake).

**Filter-chip row spacing/alignment**: two small, related fixes. `.cfilters` (the All/Unresolved/Resolved/Mine/Private chip row) had `padding: 8px 10px 0` while `.cmts-b` (the comment-card list right below it) had `padding: 6px 14px 14px` — a 4px left/right mismatch meant the chips sat slightly left of the cards' own left edge instead of lining up with them, and the 0 bottom padding on `.cfilters` left almost no breathing room before the first card. Changed `.cfilters` to `padding: 10px 14px 14px` — the `14px` left/right now matches `.cmts-b` exactly (chips align flush with the cards below), and the new `14px` bottom padding, stacked with `.cmts-b`'s own `6px` top padding, opens up a clear ~20px gap between the filter row and the first comment. Verified visually against the real dev server (chips now line up with the card edges, clear gap beneath them) and via three regenerated baselines (`comment-cards.png`, `creative-review.png`, `upload-artwork-modal.png`, each diffed and confirmed as exactly this change first). Full e2e suite: 50/51 (same pre-existing flake).

## Dedicated save actions per section, "Save and Close" gating, two-line modal header, X removed

The footer Save button and the Content tab's inline "Save Version N" button were doing overlapping jobs — the inline button existed purely so copy could reach the Checks tab without closing the modal, while the footer button was meant to save-and-close the whole creative (copy and artwork together). Four related asks arrived to disentangle that, plus a header restructure.

**New derived pending-state pair**, `pendingCopyChange` and `pendingCreativeChange`, replacing the ad hoc unchanged-check `saveCopyIfChanged()` used inline. `pendingCreativeChange` is just `!!file` — a picked-but-unuploaded file. `pendingCopyChange` merges draft fields over the latest saved version and compares by value (`fieldsEqual`, an existing helper), gated by a new `hasAnyCopyContent` check (`Object.values(mergedCopyFields).some((v) => v?.trim())`) — without that gate, a brand-new creative with copy fields but nothing ever typed into them would count as "pending" simply for lacking a first saved version, permanently disabling the footer button before the user had touched anything. Caught live: a first pass with no content gate showed the footer button disabled on a completely fresh, untouched creative in a real Playwright run.

**Footer button relabelled "Save and Close"**, disabled whenever `pendingCopyChange || pendingCreativeChange`, with a `title` tooltip explaining why ("Save the copy and/or creative changes above first"). `handleSaveUpload()` simplified to drop its now-unreachable `if (file) { upload }` branch — the footer button can no longer be clicked while a file is pending, so that path never runs — keeping only the "must have at least one creative version to close" guard and the `saveCopyIfChanged()` call before `onClose()`.

**New `handleSaveCreativeOnly()` and an inline "Save Version N" button under the Creative section**, mirroring Copy's own button exactly (same `nextCreativeVersionNo` naming convention as the previous entry's copy version numbers), visible only while a file is picked. Two real bugs turned up while verifying this against the real dev server, both newly reachable only because this is the first save path that doesn't close the modal afterward:

- **A crash**: saving a brand-new creative's first version (via this new button) threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'asset')` from `CreativeVersionPreview`. `viewingCreativeVersionNo` stayed pinned at its mount-time value of `0` (no versions existed yet at mount), and nothing advanced it when `creativeVersions` went from empty to `[{version_no: 1}]` via the existing reactive query-invalidation — so `isViewingLatestCreative` evaluated false and the code tried to render a preview for a nonexistent version 0, silently passed through a non-null assertion. Every prior save path closed the modal, discarding this stale state on unmount, so it was never reachable before. Fixed by calling `setViewingCreativeVersionNo(targetVersionNo)` inside `handleSaveCreativeOnly` right after a successful save.
- **An unreachable confirmation note**: the "Saved as version N." note was nested inside the same `{file && (...)}` block as its own trigger button. Since the save handler calls `setFile(null)` in the same success path that sets the note, React batches both updates into one render — the block (and the note inside it) unmounts in the very paint it would have needed to appear in. Confirmed via a Playwright `waitForSelector('.modal .bsaved')` timing out with no error, then via reading the JSX. Fixed by moving the note to a sibling condition, `{!file && creativeSaveNote && (...)}`, and clearing it in `pickFile()` so a stale note doesn't linger across a different file pick.

**`components/ui/Modal.tsx`**: `title` widened from `string` to `React.ReactNode` (every existing string-passing caller is unaffected), plus new optional `ariaLabel` (since `aria-label` itself must stay a plain string once `title` isn't one) and `hideCloseButton` (default `false`, so every other modal keeps its close icon — only `CreativeModal` passes `true`, since its footer already always carries Cancel/Save-and-Close and Escape/scrim-click still close it either way).

**Two-line modal header**: `CreativeModal` now builds `title` as project name on its own line (`.modal-title-project`) with post name + format label underneath in smaller, muted text (`.modal-title-post`, e.g. "No Fluff Founder Series - Instagram Reel"), passed alongside a plain-string `titleAriaLabel` fallback for the dialog's own `aria-label`. `.modal-h b` renamed to `.modal-h .modal-title` in `globals.css` to match the new wrapper div (no other rule referenced the old selector, confirmed by grep).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Functionally verified against the real dev server with real seeded data: confirmed the X button is gone, the two-line title renders exactly as specified, the footer button starts enabled and reads "Save and Close", disables the instant a file is picked, and the new Creative-section "Save Version 1" button appears alongside Copy's own. Reproduced and fixed both bugs above via direct Playwright runs before a final clean pass confirmed the full flow end to end: pick file → Creative section's own "Save Version 1" → uploads, shows "V1" tab and "Current: ...", clears the drop zone, shows "Saved as version 1." in green, re-enables "Save and Close". Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 49/51 — `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (two-line header, no X, "Save and Close") before regenerating; `settings-team.png` is the same pre-existing flake carried through this entire session (re-ran in isolation, unrelated file, fails the same way alone). `npm run build` clean.

## Content and Checks tab buttons recolored to match "Save and Close"

Every action button on the Content and Checks tabs — Check WIIFM, Check Creative, Check Brand, Draft with [model], the draft option's "Use this", and both "Save Version N" buttons (Copy and Creative) — was `.btn.sm`, the plain white/bordered style, while the footer's "Save and Close" was the only button using `.btn.primary`'s blue. Added `primary` alongside `sm` on all six (`.btn.sm.primary` — the two modifiers are independent in `globals.css`, sizing from one, color from the other), so every actionable button on these two tabs now reads as the same blue as the modal's own primary action. The two footer "Cancel" buttons (`.btn` alone) and the Brief tab were untouched — this was specifically the Content/Checks buttons named.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: screenshotted the Content tab (Draft button blue, muted by the existing `:disabled` opacity since this fixture has no AI model configured) and the Checks tab (Check Creative fully blue/enabled; Check WIIFM and Check Brand the same blue at disabled opacity, since this fixture has no reference material or copy yet) — same underlying color in every case, distinguished only by the pre-existing disabled-state styling, not a new one. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 49/51 — `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual (Draft button now blue) and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating; `settings-team.png` is the same pre-existing flake carried through this entire session. `npm run build` clean.

## AI icon on the Draft button

A small sparkle glyph before the text on the Content tab's Draft button (`Draft`/`Draft with [model]`/`Drafting…`), the only button in the modal that triggers an AI action by itself rather than as a secondary affordance. No icon set existed for "AI" anywhere in the codebase (checked — nothing named sparkle/ai-icon anywhere), so this is a new inline SVG, built to match the app's existing stroke-icon convention exactly (`stroke: currentColor; fill: none; stroke-width: 1.8; stroke-linecap/linejoin: round`, the same rule shape as `.iconbtn svg` and `.drop svg`) rather than a filled/solid glyph, so it reads as part of the same icon family already used elsewhere (the modal's close X, the drop-zone upload arrow). New `.btn .aicon` rule in `globals.css` sizes it at 13px with a 6px right margin; `currentColor` means it automatically matches the button's own text color (white on this primary button, and would dim correctly under the existing `:disabled` opacity rule with no extra styling needed).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: screenshotted the Draft button directly and confirmed the sparkle renders crisply before the text, correctly muted at the same disabled opacity as the label text. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 49/51 — `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual (Draft button now shows the icon) and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating; `settings-team.png` is the same pre-existing flake carried through this entire session. `npm run build` clean.

## Brief/Content/Checks split on the main review page, mirroring the Edit modal's own three tabs

The review page's canvas had exactly one collapsible summary box, `BriefPanel` ("informational only... editing happens in CreativeModal") sitting above the always-visible post preview. Direct ask: split this into three stacked collapsible sections — Brief, Content, Checks — mirroring the Edit modal's own tab structure, move the approach notes (WIIFM rationale) out of Brief and into the new Checks section, and drop the field-box styling everywhere in these sections in favor of plain text. Confirmed with the user up front that the new Checks section should stay purely informational (Latest Copy Version + WIIFM Direction, both already just saved data) rather than also embedding the modal's three live Check WIIFM/Creative/Brand buttons — those trigger a real AI call each time, so there's no "saved state" to mirror without duplicating that whole feature outside the modal.

**New `components/ui/CollapsibleSection.tsx`** — the collapsible box itself (`.brief`/`.bf-h`/`.bf-b`, unchanged CSS), extracted out of `BriefPanel` since it's now used three times on this page (`title` + `defaultOpen` are the only inputs). `BriefPanel` and the new `ChecksPanel` both build on it; the existing "Content" wrapper is used directly in `page.tsx` around the already-existing post-preview markup, since that JSX belongs to the page (annotation layer, comment creation, tool state), not a standalone data component.

**`BriefPanel` rewritten to mirror the modal's Brief tab fields, not just Concept/Reference link/Text on Image** — added the "Details" fields it was missing (What Is It Called?, Content Type, Format, Publish Date or Destination/Needed By depending on `delivery`, Lead — the lead's name resolved via the same `useTeamMembers` hook the modal itself uses, matched against `creative.lead_user_id`), removed the "Approach notes — WIIFM rationale" `ListEditor` entirely (moved to Checks, below), and replaced `ListEditor`'s read-only rendering of Text on Image (a disabled `<textarea className="bin">` per slide — still visually a field box even read-only) with plain `<p className="fd-d">` text, one paragraph per slide with a bold "Slide N:" prefix. Every field in this panel now follows the same shape: a small uppercase `.bl` label, then plain text underneath — no inputs, no textareas, nothing bordered like an editable field, anywhere in this component.

**"Content" section** — the existing awaiting-artwork/post-preview block, previously always rendered directly in the canvas, now wrapped in `<CollapsibleSection title="Content" defaultOpen>` with no changes to its own internals. Defaults open (unlike Brief and Checks) since it's the page's primary content, not a reference glance.

**New `components/creative-review/ChecksPanel.tsx`** — read-only "Latest Copy Version {N}" (each populated copy field as `Label: value`, plain text, same field-set logic as the modal via `formatById`/`COPY_FIELD_LABELS`) and "WIIFM Direction" (`creative.approach_notes`, the same data the removed Brief-panel `ListEditor` used to show, now the only place it displays on this page).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a copy version with a caption and two Text on Image slides, opened all three sections and confirmed via direct `innerText` extraction that Brief shows every Details field, Concept, Reference link and both slides as plain text; confirmed zero `<textarea>`/`<input>` elements exist anywhere inside the Brief section (`count() === 0`); confirmed Content renders open by default; confirmed Checks shows "Latest Copy Version 2" with the seeded caption and the correct "no WIIFM direction yet" fallback. Screenshotted all three states. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 49/51 — `creative-review.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (three stacked sections replacing the single Brief box) before regenerating; `upload-artwork-modal.png` re-flaked on its own (passed clean on a solo re-run, unrelated to this change, matching this session's established flake history for that screenshot) and was regenerated anyway since the sparkle-icon baseline underneath it had drifted by a few compression bytes; `settings-team.png` is the same pre-existing flake carried through this entire session. `npm run build` clean.

## Content section's own nested box removed — a section within a section

Direct follow-up: once wrapped in the new collapsible "Content" section, the awaiting-artwork state and the post preview each still carried their own border/background (`.awaiting`'s dashed border, `.postbox`'s solid one) — visibly a bordered box sitting inside the collapsible's own bordered box. Since `.postbox` and `.awaiting` are used nowhere else in the codebase (confirmed by grep — both only ever render inside this one Content section now), removed the `border`, `border-radius` and `background` declarations from both rules entirely rather than adding a variant class; the outer `.brief` container's own border plus its `overflow: hidden` already close off the corners cleanly, so nothing else needed adjusting to compensate.

**Verified**: typecheck clean (CSS-only change, nothing to lint). Functionally verified against the real dev server: screenshotted the Content section and confirmed "No artwork yet" now sits flush inside the collapsible with a single border, not two. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 — `creative-review.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating; `upload-artwork-modal.png` flaked identically to its established pattern in this session (same dashed-border anti-aliasing, confirmed pixel-for-pixel via the diff image, unrelated to this CSS change) and was regenerated; `settings-team.png` is the same pre-existing flake carried through this entire session. `npm run build` clean.

## ShareModal: two scopes instead of four, a picker grid instead of a list, formatted like the Edit modal

Direct ask: trim "What the link shows" down to the two scopes actually used day to day, rename them, reformat the whole modal to match CreativeModal's own titled-section convention, replace the "Pieces" checkbox list with an image grid, and tie grid selection to which scope is active.

**Scopes trimmed from four to two** — "Everything waiting on them" (`pending`) and "The whole project" (`all`) removed from `SCOPE_OPTIONS`; "Just this one" → **Current Post**, "Choose the pieces" → **Multiple Posts**. `SharedLinkScope`/`ShareScope` themselves (`hooks/use-create-shared-link.ts`, `lib/shared-link-eligibility.ts`) keep all four values untouched — existing `shared_links` rows can carry `pending`/`all` from links made before this change, and `create_shared_link` still needs to accept them — only this modal's own exposed choices shrank. Default scope changed from `pending` to `one` (Current Post) accordingly. `ShareModal`'s own `eligibilityNote` logic was simplified to drop the now-unreachable `pending`/`all`-specific copy ("Nothing is currently waiting on client review…") — dead branches once the UI can no longer produce that scope.

**Section formatting matches CreativeModal** — "What the link shows", "Pieces" and "Link settings" are now real `.msection-h`/`.msection-d` pairs (bold heading, one-line description, divider), the same pattern this session already established there, replacing the flat run of `.field` labels this modal had instead.

**"Pieces" is now a grid, not a checkbox list** — new `.pkgrid`/`.pktile` (3-column grid, one tile per creative in the project) replaces the old `.picklist2`/`.pk2` rows entirely (both classes deleted from `globals.css`, confirmed unused elsewhere by grep). Each tile is a decorative gradient — the same hash-the-id-to-a-hue trick `CreativePreviewPopover`'s `PlaceholderArt` already uses, duplicated locally rather than shared since fetching a real signed thumbnail per creative just for this picker would mean a storage round-trip per tile, and the two components' overlays behave differently (the popover's is always-on; the grid tile's is hover-only, see below). A circular checkbox indicator sits in the corner of every tile, always visible (filled with a checkmark when selected, an outline when not) — this is deliberately not hover-gated, only the title is, so selection state reads at a glance.

**Grid selection is tied to scope, not independent of it** — "Current Post" shows every piece in the grid but only the current one reads as selected, and tiles aren't clickable (`selectable={scope==="pick"}`, no `onClick` attached when false, so a click on a locked tile genuinely no-ops rather than looking disabled). Switching to "Multiple Posts" seeds the picked set with the current creative if nothing's picked yet (`toggleScope`) — starting from what's already showing rather than an empty grid, matching "then you are allowed to select more" — and every tile becomes clickable, toggling that creative in or out of the link.

**Hover reveals the title** — `.pktile-title`, an absolutely-positioned label over a dark gradient scrim at the tile's bottom edge, `opacity: 0` by default and `1` on `:hover`. Confirmed via a direct Playwright `.hover()` that the name appears only then, not before.

**`tests/e2e/share-eligibility.spec.ts` updated for the new UI** — one test's radio click renamed ("Just this one" → "Current Post"); the other, which exercised "The whole project" to get a partial-exclusion scenario (one eligible creative, one not), now reaches the same state by switching to "Multiple Posts" and clicking every still-unselected tile. Caught a real test-only race while writing this: querying `.pktile:not(.selected)` and clicking `.first()` in a loop could click a tile before React had re-rendered it as selectable after the scope switch, silently no-op'ing (no `disabled` attribute to fail loudly on — by design, so a locked tile doesn't look greyed out) and leaving the grid one selection short of what the test expected. Fixed by asserting `.pktile` no longer carries `.locked` before iterating, and iterating by fixed index rather than re-querying an ever-shrinking `:not(.selected)` set. Not a product bug — a genuine user click on a different element after clicking the radio always has real mouse-travel time in between; only a scripted click faster than one React commit could hit it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: seeded a second creative in the project, opened Share for review, confirmed exactly two radio options labelled Current Post/Multiple Posts, confirmed the grid renders both pieces as gradient tiles with Current Post pre-selected and the other tile present-but-unselected, confirmed clicking the unselected tile under Current Post does nothing, confirmed hovering a tile reveals its name and un-hovering hides it again, confirmed switching to Multiple Posts keeps the current piece selected and makes both tiles clickable, and confirmed clicking the second tile brings the count to "2 selected". Screenshotted every state. Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 50/51 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — both `share-eligibility.spec.ts` tests updated and passing, no screenshot baselines touch this modal so nothing needed regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## ShareModal follow-ups: real aspect ratios, wording, and a genuine gap found and fixed — the stage-advance control was gone

Four requests on the same modal, the third of which surfaced a real, previously-unnoticed regression from earlier in this session.

**Grid tiles now use each creative's own format aspect ratio** — every tile was a fixed 4:3 regardless of what it actually was. New `aspectRatioCss(ratio)` parses `lib/formats.ts`'s own `"W:H"` string (e.g. Instagram Feed's `"4:5"`) into a CSS `aspect-ratio` value, applied per tile via inline `style` (not a class — it varies per creative, so a single CSS rule can't express it). Formats with no real single ratio (`"multi"`, `"—"` — Google Display's multi-size set, SMS/email) fall back to `"4 / 5"`, Instagram Feed's own — the closest match to "look like an Instagram feed" for anything that isn't literally one. The fixed `.pktile { aspect-ratio: 4/3 }` rule was removed from `globals.css` since every tile now sets its own.

**Wording**: "What the link shows" → **Share**, "Pieces" → **Posts**, and every "piece"/"pieces" left in the body copy (the Multiple Posts hint, the Posts section's own selection-count line) renamed to "post"/"posts" to match — the heading rename would have read inconsistently against body text still saying "piece."

**Footer no longer shows the project name** — it didn't fit once "2 selected" or more pushed the button leftward with nothing to give. Replaced `<span className="grow">{projectName}</span>` with an empty `<div className="grow" />`, the same bare-spacer pattern CreativeModal's own footer already uses to right-align its buttons with nothing on the left. `projectName` was ShareModal's only consumer of that value, so the prop was removed from its signature entirely rather than left unused, and its call site in `page.tsx` (which also dropped its now-unnecessary `creative.projects &&` guard, added originally only to satisfy that prop) updated to match.

**A real gap found while answering "how do I get a post to Client Review"**: asked the user directly, since the stage >= 5 rule is enforced server-side (a security-definer Postgres function, not just a UI message) and removing it is a real, hard-to-reverse product decision — but the user's own follow-up question ("there's no way for me to change the status, is there?") pointed at something worth checking before that decision even mattered. It checked out: nothing in the app writes `creatives.stage` any more. `hooks/use-advance-creative-stage.ts` and the page's own "Move to Client Review"/"Move back to Internal" buttons were deleted earlier in this session when the separate Upload/Edit/New Brief entry points were consolidated into the unified Edit modal — and nothing replaced that specific capability. The backend RPC (`advance_creative_stage`, with its own legal-move and staff-only checks) was never touched and still works; it just lost its only caller. Confirmed with the user to restore it on the page header (its original location) rather than move it into the Edit modal. Restored `use-advance-creative-stage.ts` verbatim and the two buttons in `page.tsx`, same position, same behavior as before. `tests/e2e/stage-transition.spec.ts` (also deleted in that same earlier consolidation, for the same reason) restored too, with its "Just this one" radio references updated to "Current Post."

**Eligibility message reworded now that the button exists again** — "Work only becomes visible to a client from Client Review onward" (accurate but unactionable when there was no way to act on it) now reads "Move it to Client Review (on its own page) to make it visible here," naming the actual control rather than just the destination state.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: confirmed a tile's bounding box for an Instagram Feed creative renders at a 4:5 ratio (160×200px in the modal's own layout); confirmed the three section headings read Share/Posts/Link settings; confirmed the footer shows only the Create link button, no project name text; confirmed "Move back to Internal" (fixture creative starts at stage 5) renders on the page header and, on a fresh stage-1 creative, clicking "Move to Client Review" through to "Move back to Internal" and back both worked end to end against the real RPC. Restored `stage-transition.spec.ts`'s all three tests passing (staff advance + share visibility; client has no entry point and the RPC itself rejects a non-staff caller; ShareModal's warning clears once the stage actually moves). Unit suite 48/48. Full e2e suite: 53/54 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `creative-review.png` and `upload-artwork-modal.png` both diffed against actual and regenerated (the former for the real new stage button, the latter the same established dashed-border anti-aliasing flake this session has hit repeatedly, confirmed pixel-for-pixel via the diff image). `npm run build` clean.

## Stage control becomes a state toggle, tools reorganized, comment styling polish, header alignment

Seven related requests on the creative review page and its Comments column.

**Stage control renamed from an action button to a state toggle** — "Move to Client Review"/"Move back to Internal" (one button, labelled by what clicking it would *do*) replaced by `.stagesw`, a two-segment control labelled by what each state *is* — "Internal Review" and "Client Review", both always visible, the current one highlighted (same visual pattern as the topbar's own Preview as Agency/Client `.modeswitch`, ported into this page's light-surface palette rather than that one's dark-topbar one). Clicking the inactive segment calls the same `advance_creative_stage` mutation as before; clicking the already-active one no-ops. Still gated to `isStaff && creative.stage <= 5` exactly as before — stage 6+ (Approved/Scheduled/Published) never had a legal transition and still doesn't.

**Pin and Region moved into the Comments column**, directly above the composer, in a new `.cmt-tools` row — they create a comment (an anchored one), so they now live next to the thing they create rather than in the artwork's own toolbar. The state itself (`toolMode`) stays owned by the page, same as before (AnnotationLayer over the artwork and CaptionHighlighter both still need it) — `CommentsPanel` gained `toolMode`/`onToolModeChange`/`canAnnotate` props rather than owning a duplicate copy.

**Toolbar dividers made explicit and symmetric** — the Creative/Copy separator used to come from `.vsel + .vsel`'s own `border-left` + `padding-left: 13px`, which only padded the *right* side of the line (the left side was whatever the flex `gap` happened to be, 5px) — visibly lopsided once looked for. Replaced with a dedicated `<span className="toolsep">` (1px line, `margin: 0 8px`) that combines with the existing 5px flex gap to give 13px on both sides evenly, and reused identically after the Share icon (removing Pin/Region left Share sitting directly next to Copy with nothing separating it from the stage toggle after it).

**Edit button gets breathing room** — `.tools .btn.primary { margin-left: 8px; }`, scoped to this one button (the only `.btn.primary` directly inside `.tools`) rather than a page-wide margin.

**Comment text now matches the composer's own size** — `.cmt p` and `.reply .rb p` were `11px` against the "Add a comment…" textarea's `12.5px`; both raised to `12.5px` to match. `.cmt-h`'s margin-bottom (name row → body) and `.cmt-f`'s margin-top (body → Reply/Resolve row) both increased slightly (5px→9px, 7px→11px) for more breathing room around the comment text itself.

**Filter pills sized up without touching the text** — `.cfilters .chip` padding `2px 8px` → `4px 10px`, font-size untouched.

**Header alignment**: "Comments" and the post title heading were both already built on the same shared rule (`.stage-h, .cmts-h`), but two things pushed them out of sync. First, the two-segment stage toggle rendered slightly taller than the row's other controls (button padding driving its own height rather than matching the row), adding ~4px the comments header never had — fixed by giving `.stagesw` an explicit `height: 26px` matching `.vsel select`'s own min-height. Second, widening the toolbar (two new separators, both toggle segments always visible instead of one at a time) meant its own contents could overflow and clip at narrower widths, since `.tools` had no `flex-wrap` of its own (only `.stage-h` wraps its two direct children — the title and the whole toolbar — not the toolbar's own items). Added `flex-wrap: wrap; row-gap: 7px;` to `.tools` so it wraps internally instead of clipping; the two headers' own borders now land within a pixel of each other at realistic desktop widths, confirmed directly via `boundingBox()` (43px vs. 42px, down from 46.4px vs. 42px before the fix, and clipped text before that).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. ## The stage pipeline, simplified from 8 stages to 4 — and a real bug fixed for the whole agency's real data

A real bug report started this: a creative ("This is a Test Post") with both copy and artwork already saved was still showing as Concept — the user's own definition of Concept being "no copy and no creative has been written yet." Investigation (not assumed — read every RPC, constraint, and hook that touches `stage`, then confirmed against real production data via `supabase db query`) found this wasn't a display bug: **nothing in the app has ever advanced a creative's stage automatically.** A creative is created at stage 1 and stays there forever unless a staff member manually clicks the Internal Review/Client Review toggle. Stages 2 (Copy) and 3 (Design) were unreachable by any code path at all — `advance_creative_stage` jumped straight from "anything < 5" to 5, and back down to a hardcoded 4, never touching 2 or 3. Stages 7 (Scheduled) and 8 (Published) were equally unreachable — nothing anywhere set them (confirmed by repo-wide search). Only stage 6 (Approved) was ever reached, and only via a guest's shared-review-link approval.

The user asked for the plain-English list of what currently changes a stage (exactly three things: creation defaults to stage 1; the manual toggle; a guest approval — nothing reacts to content existing), then explicitly authorized simplifying the model to match reality: **Concept, Internal Review, Client Review, Approved** — collapsing the unreachable stages rather than just relabelling them, and dropping Scheduled entirely (confirmed dead, not just redundant).

**Concept → Internal Review is now automatic** — a Postgres trigger (`bump_creative_from_concept()`, firing `after insert` on both `creative_versions` and `copy_versions`) bumps a creative off stage 1 the instant either its first copy or its first artwork is saved, whichever comes first. Enforced at the database layer rather than an app-code call some hook could forget, matching this codebase's own established preference (`advance_creative_stage` already lives at this layer for the same reason). This directly encodes the user's own definition of Concept and is what actually fixes "Test Post" going forward, not just this once.

**Two follow-up decisions, confirmed with the user directly before writing any migration**: add a staff-side Approve action (today Approved was only reachable via a client/guest link, despite the app's own docs noting the prototype intended a staff "Approve on Behalf" too), and add a way to revoke one. Both landed as one change: the manual toggle grew a third segment — Internal Review / Client Review / Approved — always visible to staff now, every segment independently clickable. Clicking "Client Review" while Approved *is* the revoke path; no separate button needed.

**Migration** (`supabase/migrations/phase13_simplify_stage_pipeline.sql`, mirrored into `supabase/seed.sql`): remapped existing data first, content-aware for the exact bug this was about (a stage-1 row with a real `creative_versions`/`copy_versions` row already attached remaps to Internal Review, not blindly to the new stage 1) — checked against real data before writing it: 12 real creatives, "This is a Test Post" confirmed among them at stage 1 with 1 artwork version and 2 copy versions. Then narrowed `creatives_stage_range` (1-4) and `creatives_exception_stage` (1-3); replaced `advance_creative_stage` with three permissive directions (`to_internal`/`to_review`/`to_approved`, any from any stage, raising only on a genuine no-op) — the new `to_approved` branch mirrors `submit_shared_approval`'s attribution shape (`approved_at`/`approved_by_name`/`approved_by_email`) but sources the name/email from the staff caller's own `users` row instead of a guest-supplied name; the other two clear those same three columns, so a revoked approval doesn't leave a stale "approved by X" record sitting against a non-approved stage. Also updated `shared_link_allowed_creative_ids` (`stage >= 3`) and, as a drive-by correctness fix while already touching it, made `submit_shared_approval` clear `exception` on approval (it didn't before — harmless today since nothing sets `exception` anywhere in the app, but the newly-narrowed exception/stage constraint would otherwise reject an approval on any creative that ever did have one).

**A blocker surfaced along the way**: applying this required the Supabase CLI to be authenticated — linked to the project already (`supabase/.temp/project-ref`) but no access token, and no direct Postgres connection string in `.env.local`. Asked the user rather than guessing around it; they ran `npx supabase login` themselves, confirmed by `npx supabase migration list` no longer erroring, and `supabase db query -f <migration> --linked` applied it directly (the CLI's own migration-history tracking expects `<timestamp>_name.sql` filenames and silently skips this repo's existing `phaseN_*.sql` convention — confirmed every prior phase file gets skipped the same way, so `db query` rather than `db push` is how this repo's migrations actually apply, not a new choice made here).

**A second real bug found while auditing every remaining `Band` reference, not part of the plan** — `CreativePreviewPopover.tsx`'s `band === "published"` check (gating its "Published X ago" vs. "Not published yet" line) would have silently gone permanently false once `"published"` left the `Band` type, always showing "Not published yet" even for a creative with a real `published_at` set. Simplified to check `creative.published_at` directly — the actual signal, and the band check was always redundant with it anyway (nothing ever sets `published_at` through a stage transition either).

**Verified**: read every constraint, RPC, hook, and component touching `stage`/`Band`/stage-number literals before writing the migration (an Explore agent's inventory, cross-checked by hand — it missed the `CreativePreviewPopover.tsx` case above, caught on a second repo-wide grep pass). Confirmed against real production data via `supabase db query --linked` before and after: pre-migration, "This is a Test Post" sat at stage 1 with 1 creative_version and 2 copy_versions, exactly the reported bug; post-migration, stage 2. Smoke-tested the new trigger directly against the live database (inserted a real creative, confirmed stage 1; inserted a real creative_version for it, confirmed stage 2; cleaned up). Typecheck and lint clean throughout (one pre-existing-shape warning: `stageLabel`'s now-unused `delivery` parameter, kept for call-site compatibility). Rewrote both `lib/stage-labels.test.ts` and `lib/shared-link-eligibility.test.ts` for the 4-stage model; added two new e2e tests (`stage-transition.spec.ts`) covering the staff Approve action with real attribution, the revoke path clearing that attribution, and a client session still being rejected for the new `to_approved` direction specifically, not just the pre-existing two. Unit suite 47/47 (48 before — two delivery-mode-parity tests for stage-labels merged into one now-genuinely-a-loop test, not a lost assertion). Full e2e suite: 55/56 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake); one additional flake hit and confirmed via isolated re-run (`upload-artwork-modal.png`'s AI-model-label text, a timing race against this fixture's own settings load, unrelated to this change — passed clean alone). `creative-review.png` and `project-calendar.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (the 3-way toggle; the 4-stage legend) before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## Rename, archive and unarchive clients and projects

Direct ask: the ability to rename, delete, or archive clients and projects. Neither existed — `useClients`/`useProjects` were read-only, and there's no delete RLS policy on either table at all. Investigation first: both tables already have an `archived_at` column and a unique index scoped to `where archived_at is null` — built for archiving from day one, just never wired to any UI action — and the dashboard already had a working Active/Archived filter reading it, with copy already written ("Archived accounts keep their history…"), again never fed by anything. Confirmed with the user directly that "delete" means archive here, not real hard-deletion — adding an actual delete permission plus a cascade decision (what happens to a client's projects, creatives, comments, shared links underneath it) would be a materially bigger, riskier feature than what was actually wanted, and this schema already had the safe, reversible mechanism sitting unused.

**Reused rather than invented**: the row-actions menu (`.vdots`/`.colpop`/`.cp-b`/`.cpr`) already existed for exactly this shape — `ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`'s saved-view "..." menu (Rename/Delete) — extracted into a new `components/ui/RowActionsMenu.tsx` since this feature needed the same shell at four independent call sites (dashboard client rows, client-workspace project rows, and each entity's own detail-page header) rather than the one the shell was built for. Self-contained open/anchor state with its own outside-click/Escape close, mirroring `ColumnsPopover.tsx`'s own shape rather than the view-menu's parent-owned handler. New `RenameClientModal`/`RenameProjectModal` mirror `NewClientModal`/`NewProjectModal` exactly (single field, blank-check validation, raw unique-constraint error surfaced the same unhandled way those create-modals already leave it). Four new mutation hooks (`use-rename-client`, `use-archive-client`, `use-rename-project`, `use-archive-project`) follow `use-update-brief.ts`'s shape — `.select("id").maybeSingle()`, throw if null, the guard against RLS's silent-no-op-on-blocked-write this codebase uses everywhere a write can be blocked.

**Wording**: nothing is labelled "Delete" for either entity — only "Archive"/"Unarchive". Calling a fully reversible action "Delete" would misrepresent it as destructive, and the dashboard's own existing copy already frames archiving as reversible history-keeping, not removal. No confirmation dialog either, on either action — checked first and found no confirmation-dialog pattern anywhere in this codebase; the one existing precedent for a destructive-looking "..." menu action (deleting a saved calendar view) skips confirmation entirely, by its own explicit comment. Followed the same convention rather than introducing a new UI concept for the first time, especially for something reversible.

**`useProjects` now fetches archived projects too** (confirmed exactly one consumer via repo-wide grep before changing it), matching `useClients`' own "fetch everything, filter client-side" shape — previously archiving a project would have been a one-way door with no UI anywhere to see or undo it. The client workspace page gained its own Active/Archived toggle alongside the existing All/Needs Review/Approved filter chips, and every stat on that page that means "the client's real, active work" (Live Projects count, Campaigns, the onboarding-vs-filtered empty-state heuristic) now reads a new `activeProjects` derived list rather than the raw one, so an archived project never inflates a count that used to only ever see active ones.

**A real bug found and fixed while verifying, not part of the plan**: the row-actions menu's trigger button captured `e.currentTarget.getBoundingClientRect()` *inside* a functional `setState` updater (`setAnchor((prev) => prev ? null : e.currentTarget.getBoundingClientRect())`) — React nulls out a synthetic event's `currentTarget` once the handler that received it returns, but the updater function itself doesn't run until React's later render phase, by which point `e.currentTarget` had already gone `null`, throwing `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'getBoundingClientRect')` on every single click. Caught immediately by the first real Playwright run against the live dev server (a Next.js runtime error overlay, not a silent failure). Fixed by reading the rect into a local variable before calling `setAnchor`, not inside its updater.

Also added, defensively: both the trigger button's and every menu item's `onClick` call `preventDefault()` alongside `stopPropagation()` — every call site sits inside a `<Link className="crow">` row, and `stopPropagation()` alone stops the link's own click handler from firing (which is what normally calls `preventDefault()` to do client-side routing instead of a real navigation) but doesn't stop the *native* browser default action of following the href once that handler never runs. Without the explicit `preventDefault()` here, clicking the menu (or any item in it) would have triggered a real full-page navigation to the row's own link.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server end to end, for both entities, from every surface: renamed a client from its dashboard row menu and confirmed the new name appeared with no reload; archived it and confirmed it disappeared from Active and appeared under Archived; unarchived it and confirmed it was back. Repeated the identical three steps for a project, once from within the client workspace page and again from the project's own detail-page header — confirmed the header's own name updates live on rename, and the toggle correctly flips Archive↔Unarchive. Confirmed the popover closes on an outside click and on Escape, and that using the menu never navigates the row's own `<Link>` (the `preventDefault()` fix above, confirmed by URL staying put). Confirmed a real client-role session renders zero `.vdots` anywhere. New `tests/e2e/manage-clients-projects.spec.ts`, four tests, all passing. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 59/60 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `dashboard.png`, `client-workspace.png` and `project-calendar.png` each diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (the new "..." menu button, the Active/Archived toggle) before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## Row-actions menu polish, client-page header menu removed, two client-workspace columns repurposed

Five follow-up requests on the feature just built.

**Popover made screen-aware** — it was left-aligned flush to the trigger's own left edge unconditionally, so a trigger sitting near the right edge of the screen (the actions column always does, being the rightmost) routinely pushed the 160px-wide popover off the viewport. Now clamped: `Math.max(8, Math.min(anchor.left, window.innerWidth - POPOVER_WIDTH - 8))`, preferring the natural left-aligned position but never letting it overflow either edge.

**Client-page header menu removed** — rename/archive for a client now only lives on the dashboard's own row menu, per direct instruction ("this can be done in the dashboard and client table view"). Removed the `RowActionsMenu` next to `ClientWorkspacePage`'s own `<h1>`, along with the `useArchiveClient`/`renamingClient`/`RenameClientModal` wiring that only existed to support it — none of it had any other caller on that page. The project detail page's own equivalent header menu is untouched; only named "client" specifically.

**Dots icon spread and moved to the row's true edge** — the three dots (`DotsIcon`) were spaced 6 units apart on a 24-unit viewBox (`cy` 6/12/18); widened to 4/12/20. Separately, the trigger button itself sat left-aligned within its own 70px grid cell, leaving dead space between it and the actual right edge of the table — both the dashboard's client rows and the client workspace's project rows now wrap it in `{ display: "flex", justifyContent: "flex-end" }` so it sits flush against the row's real right edge instead.

**"Approval progress" → "Pending Approvals"**, showing a plain number (`waitingOnApproval` from `useProjectCreativeStats`) instead of the progress bar + "N of M approved" label. The bar/label markup and its now-fully-unused `.bar`/`.bar i`/`.barlbl` CSS were removed outright rather than left dead — confirmed via grep no other caller referenced them. `tests/e2e/client-workspace.spec.ts` updated to match (a new `.pendcount` class replaces the old `.barlbl` selector both specs used).

**"Due" → "Latest Approved Post"**, a real date rather than the project's own `due_on`. `ProjectCreativeStats` gained a `latestApprovedAt` field — the max `approved_at` among a project's creatives, computed in the same client-side aggregation loop `use-project-creative-stats.ts` already runs (no new query). The project-row cell reads this instead of `p.due_on`; the "Deadline" sort option in the toolbar's own `<select>` is untouched — it still sorts by `due_on`, real schema data that just isn't shown in this particular column any more.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: approved a real seeded creative with a known `approved_at` and confirmed "Latest Approved Post" showed the correct formatted date, "Pending Approvals" showed the right count, and the client page's `<h1>` had zero `.vdots` next to it. Narrowed the viewport to 900px and clicked a row's own trigger (naturally near the right edge there) — confirmed the popover's bounding box landed at exactly `900 - 160 - 8 = 732px`, not clipped. Screenshotted the dots icon directly to confirm the wider spacing. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 59/60 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `dashboard.png`, `client-workspace.png` and `project-calendar.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly these changes (repositioned button, renamed columns) before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## Status column dropped from the project table, a separator added, dashboard's own columns wired to real data at last

Five more follow-up requests on the same feature, all UI wording/completeness, no architecture changes.

**"Status" column removed from the client workspace's project table** — the tag (`Not started`/`N with the client`/`All approved`/`In production`) duplicated what "Pending Approvals" already says more precisely as a number, and its supporting `pending`/`status` computation (a `const status = ...` ternary block, confirmed via grep to have no other reader) was deleted along with it, not just hidden. `PROJECT_ROW_COLUMNS` dropped a track accordingly.

**"Latest Approved Post" shortened to "Latest Approved"** — direct ask ("remove Post, so just Latest Approved"); the underlying data (`latestApprovedAt`) is unchanged, this is a header-text-only edit.

**A `.toolsep` divider added between the Active/Archived toggle and the All/Needs Review/Approved chips** on the client workspace's filter row — the same `<span className="toolsep" />` element the creative-review toolbar already uses for this exact purpose (see "Toolbar dividers made explicit and symmetric" above), reused rather than a new rule invented for a second instance of the same visual need. Spacing on either side of it matches that existing element's own margin, so nothing needed tuning.

**Dashboard's "Projects" and "Last activity" columns wired to real data** — a real functional gap the user caught, not a wording one: both columns had rendered hardcoded `"—"` since the row itself was first built, never connected to any query. New `hooks/use-client-list-stats.ts` runs the same client-side-aggregation-over-plain-queries shape as `use-project-creative-stats.ts`/`use-agency-creative-stats.ts` (a `clients` id list, a `projects` count filtered to `archived_at is null`, a `creatives` select scoped to those project ids) to produce, per client, an active-project count and the max `creatives.created_at` across them — the latter documented in-line as an approximation, the same trade-off already made and flagged for the client workspace's own "Campaigns" stat: no activity-log table exists anywhere in this schema, so a real, schema-backed proxy stands in rather than a fabricated one. "Approval progress" (the same progress-bar-plus-label pattern removed from the client workspace's project rows in the previous entry, but on the dashboard's client rows this time) is removed outright.

**Dashboard and client-workspace grid columns kept in lock-step, on purpose** — removing "Status" from one table and "Approval progress" from the other, independently, would have left their shared `.crow` CSS grid misaligned differently on each page, landing the "..." menu button at a different x-position depending on which screen it's on. Pre-empted rather than fixed after the fact: `PROJECT_ROW_COLUMNS` (client workspace) and `CLIENT_ROW_COLUMNS` (dashboard) are two new per-page `gridTemplateColumns` constants, applied inline to both the head row and every data row exactly the way `app/(app)/settings/team/page.tsx` already does this for its own table, deliberately ending in the same final two track widths (`92px 70px`) on both so the trigger button lands in an identical slot either way.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: read every header cell's text on both tables and confirmed "Status" is gone from the client workspace's project table and "Approval progress" is gone from the dashboard's client table; confirmed the client workspace header reads "Latest Approved" (not "...Post"); confirmed exactly one `.toolsep` renders between Active/Archived and All on the filter row; confirmed the dashboard's real seeded client shows a real project count ("1", not "—") and a real recent date ("Sep 25", not "—"). Read both pages' `.vdots` trigger bounding boxes directly and confirmed identical `x`/`width` across the two screens. New `dashboard.spec.ts` test asserting the Projects/Last-activity cells hold real, non-placeholder values; existing `client-workspace.spec.ts` updated to drop its now-stale Status-tag assertion. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 60/61 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `dashboard.png` and `client-workspace.png` each diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly these changes before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

**Immediate follow-up, confirmed as stale rather than a real bug**: the user next reported the "..." menu misaligned between the dashboard and client workspace tables, alongside a screenshot still showing the just-removed "Approval progress" column — a giveaway the browser tab simply hadn't reloaded the latest code, not a regression. Verified directly against the live dev server (not assumed): read both pages' `.vdots` bounding boxes at both the suite's default 1280px viewport and a wider 1728px one, and both landed at the identical x-position at each width. This is exactly what the shared "last two tracks the same width" convention (`PROJECT_ROW_COLUMNS`/`CLIENT_ROW_COLUMNS`, see above) guarantees mathematically — the leading `1fr` track absorbs any difference in the columns between it, so the trailing fixed-width tracks (and the button inside the last one) always land at the same offset from the row's right edge regardless of what sits in between. No code change made; confirmed with the user that a refresh resolved it.

## Client workspace's Pending Approvals column replaced with 4 per-stage counts

Direct ask, on the heels of the stage-pipeline simplification (see "The stage pipeline, simplified from 8 stages to 4" above): remove the "Pending Approvals" column from the client workspace's project table and replace it with four separate columns, one count each for Concept, Internal Review, Client Review and Approved — the model's own 4 stages, named exactly as `lib/stage-labels.ts`'s `STAGE_TABLE` already defines them.

**`useProjectCreativeStats` gained a `byStage: [number, number, number, number]` field** — a plain per-project tally indexed by raw `stage` number (1-4), computed in the same client-side aggregation loop that already produces `total`/`done`/`waitingOnApproval`/`feedbackToAction`, no new query. Deliberately counts by raw stage rather than by band: an exception-flagged creative (`changes_requested`/`rejected`, only legal at stages 1-3) still counts under whichever stage 1-3 it's actually sitting at, since the user asked for exactly these 4 named stages and nothing about exceptions. The existing `done`/`waitingOnApproval`/`feedbackToAction` fields are untouched — they still drive the page's own top stats strip (Live Projects/Campaigns/Waiting on Client/Approved) and the Needs Review/Approved filter chips, none of which this request touched.

**`PROJECT_ROW_COLUMNS` grew four narrow tracks in place of the one 150px "Pending Approvals" track** (`74px 96px 90px 84px` for Concept/Internal Review/Client Review/Approved), keeping the same final two tracks (`92px 70px`, Latest Approved + the actions menu) the dashboard's own `CLIENT_ROW_COLUMNS` ends in — same reasoning as before, and confirmed by the diagnostic above that this holds regardless of how many columns sit ahead of them.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: read the table's header row and confirmed it now reads Project/Posts/Concept/Internal Review/Client Review/Approved/Latest Approved with no Pending Approvals column; read the fixture's one seeded project's row and confirmed 0/0/1/0 across the four new columns (its single creative sits at stage 3, Client Review) with "1 total" still correct in Posts. `tests/e2e/client-workspace.spec.ts` updated: the screenshot test's settle-wait now reads the new `.stagecount` class instead of the removed `.pendcount`; the per-row assertion test now checks all four `.stagecount` cells individually (0/0/1/0) instead of the single removed count. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 60/61 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `client-workspace.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

## Calendar header spacing, and the client workspace's stage-count columns pared down and right-aligned

Two more direct follow-ups, one per screen.

**Project calendar view's own header felt tight** — the "N scheduled posts in this view." line sat 3px below the page title (this component's own `<p className="sub">` inheriting the shared `.sub` class's global `margin-top: 3px`) and flush against the Main Table/saved-views tab row directly below it (`.viewbar`'s own `margin-top` is `0`). Fixed by giving this one instance (`ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`) an inline `marginTop: 14, marginBottom: 14` rather than touching the shared `.sub` rule itself — that class is reused verbatim on the dashboard and client workspace pages for their own subtitle lines, and this complaint was specifically about the calendar view. 14px matches the spacing convention `.viewbar`/`.calbar` already use for their own bottom margins, so the whole header block now reads as one consistent rhythm instead of a tight cluster followed by normal spacing.

**Client workspace's project table lost its "Posts" and "Approved" columns** — direct ask, down to Project/Concept/Internal Review/Client Review/Latest Approved. `PROJECT_ROW_COLUMNS` dropped the two corresponding tracks; the underlying `total`/`byStage[3]` values are untouched in the hook (still feeding the page's own top stats strip), just no longer rendered in this table.

**Remaining three status columns' headers and data right-aligned to match "Latest Approved"** — direct ask. Both the header cells and the data cells for Concept/Internal Review/Client Review now share the `.ago` class the "Latest Approved" column already used (right-aligned text, 12px, muted), in place of the header's default left alignment and the data cells' own `fontSize: 13` inline override — one consistent look across all four right-hand columns instead of three left-aligned numbers next to one right-aligned date.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: read the calendar view's subtitle and tab-row bounding boxes and confirmed a 14px gap on both sides (previously ~3px above, 0px below); read the client workspace table's header row and confirmed it now reads Project/Concept/Internal Review/Client Review/Latest Approved with no Posts or Approved column; read the fixture's project row and confirmed 0/0/1 across the three remaining stage columns; read computed `text-align` on both the Concept header cell and its data cell and confirmed both report `right`. `tests/e2e/client-workspace.spec.ts` updated: the now-4th "Approved" `.stagecount` assertion and the now-gone "1 total" text assertion both removed. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 60/61 twice in a row (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake both times; one unrelated `new-project.spec.ts` test flaked once under full-suite concurrency and passed clean both isolated and on the immediate re-run, confirmed unrelated to these changes) — `client-workspace.png` and `project-calendar.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly these changes before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

**Same alignment mismatch found and fixed one screen over**: the dashboard's own client table had the identical inconsistency the client workspace table just had fixed — its "Projects" column (a plain count, `activeProjectCount`) was left-aligned with an inline `fontSize: 13` override while "Last activity" sat right-aligned via `.ago`, directly beside it. Same fix, same reasoning: both the header cell and the data cell now use `.ago` in place of the left-aligned default and the inline style override, so the two numeric-ish columns read consistently instead of one snapping to the left edge of its track and the other to the right. `Status` (a tag/badge, not a plain number) was left alone, matching the earlier decision not to force badge-shaped content into the same right-aligned treatment as a count or date.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: read computed `text-align` on every header and data cell in the row and confirmed `Client`/`Status` report `left` while `Projects`/`Last activity` both report `right`, matching each other exactly. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 60/61 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `dashboard.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this alignment change before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.

**Immediate follow-up: the fix above was incomplete** — leaving `Status` left-aligned while its neighbours on both sides went right-aligned created a worse version of the same problem, just relocated: the gap between `Projects` (right-aligned, hugging the boundary) and `Status`'s badge (left-aligned, starting immediately on the other side of that same boundary) read as too tight, while the gap between the badge (ending early inside its own wide track) and `Status`'s now-far-right-aligned neighbour `Last activity` read as too loose — both symptoms of the one underlying inconsistency, not two separate spacing bugs. Fixed by giving the `Status` cell the same `.ago` treatment as the rest of the row (both header and data cell) — the badge now sits flush against its own column's right edge like every other cell, so the gap either side of it is the ordinary, consistent inter-column gap the rest of the row already has, not an artifact of mismatched alignment. `.tag`'s `display: inline-flex` still respects the parent's `text-align: right` the same way ordinary inline text would, so no separate flex-wrapper was needed. Also renamed the "Last activity" header to "Last Activity", matching the title-case convention every other header in this row already follows.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: read the header row and confirmed it now reads Client/Projects/Status/Last Activity/…; read each data cell's bounding box and confirmed a consistent 14px gap between every column boundary, with the `Status` badge now sitting flush against its own column's right edge rather than adrift after a wide left-aligned track. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 60/61 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake) — `dashboard.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change before regenerating. `npm run build` clean.
