## Continuous-delivery table rebuilt to match Scheduled's, against a real content-planner template

Direct ask: the continuous-delivery project view (e.g. "Q4 Emailers") was a plain CSS-grid `.ptable` — a flat, unsorted six-column list (Creative/Format/Destination/Added/Due/Stage) with no resize, no reorder, no saved views, no week/month grouping — while Scheduled's own view (`ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`) has all of that. Ask: copy Scheduled's formatting and functionality onto Continuous, and replace its default columns with a specific 13-field content-planner template (Live Date, Creative Name, Placement, Funnel, TG, Type, Status, Concept and Ref, Final Creative, V1 Copy, V2 Copy, Notes for Designer, Principles). Built as a genuine copy (`components/project/ContinuousCalendarTable.tsx`, `ContinuousCalendarGrid.tsx`), not a shared generic component — matches this codebase's own established preference (the rename modals, the two `RowActionsMenu` call sites) and was the explicit instruction.

**Field-by-field mapping, schema-backed where a real column already existed, `cx`-jsonb-backed where none did:** Live Date → `due_on` (already real and settable for continuous creatives, just never surfaced anywhere before this — becomes the single frozen date column driving week/month grouping the way `scheduled_at` does for Scheduled; no Week-number/Day-of-week split, since the supplied template has neither). Creative Name → `name` (identical clickable/hover-preview cell to Scheduled's own). Placement → `destination` (already required for continuous, already described in the New Brief form as "Say where this goes" — a direct match for "ad slot/listing position", not a new field). Status → real `stage`/`exception`, byte-identical to Scheduled's own pill/legend/filter. Notes for Designer → real `approach_notes` (byte-identical bulleted, read-only cell to Scheduled's own "Approach Notes"). Concept and Ref → real `concept` + `reference_url` combined in one wide cell (`reference_url` wasn't previously selected by `useCreatives.ts` at all — added). Funnel/TG/Type/Final Creative/V1 Copy/V2 Copy/Principles have no existing schema column — these read and write `creatives.cx`, the same jsonb blob genuine per-project custom columns already use (the schema's own comment on `cx` says plainly: "the set of fields is per-project and open-ended"), just under fixed keys and locally-constructed `CustomColumnRow`-shaped objects rather than real `custom_columns` rows — no migration or seeding needed, and `CxCell.tsx`'s existing `type: "status"` (a `<select>` plus a colored chip) covers Funnel/Type's dropdown-chip requirement exactly as spec'd, reusing `AddColumnForm.tsx`'s own colour palette. V1 Copy/V2 Copy needed multi-line editing, which `CxCell`'s single-line text input doesn't have — rather than extend the shared `CustomColumnType` union (a bigger, separate decision affecting the real "+ Add Column" feature), these two get a small bespoke `<textarea>` cell local to the new component only. `ProjectCalendarGrid.tsx` (the "Calendar" day-grid mode) hardcoded `scheduled_at` internally in two places, not reusable as-is — copied to `ContinuousCalendarGrid.tsx` with `due_on` substituted throughout. `CreativeModal.tsx`'s `onCreated` callback previously only forwarded `scheduledAt` (always null for continuous), silently breaking the "jump the calendar to a just-created brief's month" behavior for continuous — extended to also forward `dueOn`.

**Saved named views needed their own migration, confirmed with the user directly first**: `calendar_views` (phase10) is agency-wide with no column-set dimension at all — reused as-is, a view saved on Scheduled's columns would show up as a nonsensical tab on Continuous's table and vice versa. Asked rather than assumed: user chose a proper fix over skipping the feature for v1. `supabase/migrations/phase14_calendar_views_table_type.sql` adds a `table_type` check-constrained column (default `'scheduled'`, since every existing row predates Continuous having this feature at all) and re-scopes the existing unique name index to `(agency_id, table_type, lower(name))`. `hooks/use-calendar-views.ts` threads a `tableType` parameter through the query (now keyed `["calendar-views", tableType]`) and the create mutation; `ProjectCalendarTable.tsx`'s own call sites updated to pass `"scheduled"` explicitly — its only behavior change from this work.

**A real, pre-existing race condition found and fixed, not shipped as a known limitation**: `hooks/use-update-creative-cx.ts` saved a `cx` field via fetch-then-merge (read the whole blob, merge one key client-side, write the whole blob back) — its own comment already called this "fine for one editor at a time." That was safe when a project had at most one or two custom columns edited one at a time; this table puts 7 `cx`-backed fields on every single row, which a real user will plausibly edit in quick succession. Confirmed the bug empirically before touching anything: editing two fields back-to-back with no pause silently dropped the first edit (the second field's fetch read a `cx` blob that didn't yet include the first field's write); spacing edits ~600ms apart, both persisted correctly. Flagged to the user directly rather than silently shipping either a data-loss risk or a silent scope expansion; **user chose the real fix**. `supabase/migrations/phase15_update_creative_cx_rpc.sql` adds `update_creative_cx(p_creative_id, p_key, p_value_json)` — a single `UPDATE ... SET cx = jsonb_set(cx, array[p_key], p_value_json::jsonb, true)` statement, atomic per Postgres's own MVCC guarantees, no fetch-then-merge round trip and therefore no race window. Deliberately **not** `SECURITY DEFINER` — it runs as the calling role, so the existing `creatives_update` RLS policy (staff-only) still governs it exactly the way it already governs the plain-table-write path; a blocked caller gets zero rows back, the same silent-no-op shape every other mutation hook in this app already guards against, no new permission check needed. `p_value_json` is a `JSON.stringify`'d *string*, not a `jsonb` parameter directly — PostgREST maps a bare JSON `null` in a jsonb-typed RPC argument to a SQL `NULL`, and `jsonb_set(cx, path, NULL)` returns `NULL` for the *whole column*, silently wiping every other key; casting a text `'null'` literal to `jsonb` inside the function sidesteps that ambiguity entirely. This fix lives in the one shared hook, so it also fixes the same latent race for every existing real custom column across the whole app, not just this new table.

**A real, unrelated fixture-teardown gap found and fixed while verifying**: `calendar_views` was missing from `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s agency-id-wide teardown sweep — never surfaced before, since no earlier spec had ever completed a real "Save as New View" flow (existing tests only asserted the Save button's presence, then discarded). The new saved-views-scoping test is the first to actually persist a view, which immediately orphaned two agencies via an FK violation on cleanup (one from this session, one dormant since 2026-09-24 predating this work entirely) — same class of gap as the `shared_links`/`format_directions` teardown fixes noted earlier in this doc. Added to the sweep (before `clients`/`agencies`, alongside `memberships`); both orphaned agencies and everything under them cleaned up directly against the live database via the same deletion order.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. New `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts` helper `createContinuousCreative` (mirrors `createCreativeAtStage`'s shape) seeds real `due_on`/`stage`/`cx` data directly for the new spec. Functionally verified against the real dev server before writing any permanent test: seeded a continuous project with two creatives, confirmed the header reads all 13 fields with "Columns 13/13"; set Funnel and Type via their chip dropdowns and confirmed both persisted (including the exact race-condition repro and fix, above); typed independent V1/V2 Copy text and confirmed both persisted; confirmed the status filter narrows rows the same way Scheduled's does; confirmed Calendar-mode's day grid renders from `due_on`; confirmed a new brief with a future `due_on` jumps the table to that month; confirmed column resize/Discard Changes works; confirmed a saved view created on Continuous does not appear on Scheduled's own tabs and vice versa; confirmed a real client-role session sees every control (chip selects, textareas) disabled. New `tests/e2e/continuous-calendar.spec.ts`, 9 tests, all passing — no screenshot test included, since the only available continuous project is created with a randomized per-run name (`createContinuousProject()`, deliberately not part of the base fixture) that would never produce a stable pixel baseline; visually confirmed the render directly instead before deciding against one. `tests/e2e/new-brief.spec.ts` updated: its continuous-creative test now fills `#nbDue` too, since `ContinuousCalendarTable` — like `ProjectCalendarTable`'s own `scheduled_at` requirement — only shows a creative that has the relevant date set; its four `.ptable, .empty` waits (now-dead selector, checked carefully — each one only ever actually matched via `.empty` in practice, before or after this change) updated to `.tblwrap, .empty` for clarity. Dead `.ptable`/`.prow` CSS rules removed from `app/globals.css` (`.pname` kept — still used by both calendar tables' creative-name cells); the stale comment above them and in `project-calendar.spec.ts` rewritten to reflect that both delivery modes now share the same calendar-table shape. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 70/71 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake; `dashboard.png` also needed one unrelated same-day regeneration for a real date rollover — "Sep 25" → "Sep 26" in the Last Activity column — confirmed via diff before regenerating, not a regression). `npm run build` clean.

## Creative modal's section dividers moved above the title, empty-state notes made smaller and amber

Direct ask, screenshotted on the Checks tab but applying to all three (Brief/Content/Checks share one component, `CreativeModal.tsx`): a section's own "nothing here yet" message (e.g. "No copy saved yet.") sat right after the divider that closed out the section above it, with a large gap and no divider before the *next* title — so the message read as belonging to the wrong section, visually detached from its own title/subtitle and glued to whatever came next.

**Root cause matched the symptom exactly**: `.msection-d`'s own `border-bottom` was the divider, sitting directly under the subtitle — i.e. *between* a section's subtitle and its own content/empty-message, not between sections. Moved the divider from `.msection-d` (bottom border, removed) to `.msection-h` (top border, `:not(:first-child)` so the very first section in a tab doesn't get a redundant line right under the tab bar) — now every section's title, subtitle and empty-state note sit together as one visually grouped unit, with the divider marking the boundary *before* the next section starts, matching the second half of the ask directly.

**A real layout bug surfaced immediately by the `:not(:first-child)` rule**: the Checks tab's three "Check WIIFM"/"Check Creative"/"Check Brand" sections are generated via `.map()`, each one previously wrapped in its own `<div key={kind}>` — a real DOM element that made each `.msection-h` the *first* child of its own wrapper rather than a sibling of the sections before it, so none of the three ever got the new top divider. Fixed by swapping that wrapper to `<Fragment key={kind}>` (no DOM node), restoring true sibling order so the CSS rule applies uniformly across every section in the tab, generated or not.

**Empty-state messages get their own class**: four `<p className="sub">` instances that stood in for "nothing to show in this section" ("No copy saved yet.", "No WIIFM direction yet — save some copy to generate one.", and both formats' "has no caption fields" notes) changed to a new `.msection-empty` (11.5px, `var(--amber)` — matching `.msection-d`'s own size, distinct colour signalling "empty" the way amber already does elsewhere in this app). Deliberately scoped to only these four — `.sub` itself is untouched (used across the whole app for ordinary subtitles) and three other, differently-positioned `.sub` usages in this same file (the inline "Add some copy first…" disabled-reason text next to a Check button, "No findings came back — try again.", "Updating…") were left alone: none of them sit directly after a section divider the way the fixed four did, and "Updating…" specifically is a transient loading state, not an empty one, so amber (which reads as "needs attention") would be the wrong signal for it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server across all three tabs (Brief/Content/Checks, via three screenshots taken in the same modal session): confirmed every section's divider now sits above its own title, including all three generated Check sections after the `Fragment` fix (screenshotted before *and* after that fix — the "before" version visibly showed no divider above "Check WIIFM"/"Check Creative"/"Check Brand", confirming the bug empirically rather than assuming the CSS rule alone was sufficient); confirmed "No copy saved yet." and "No WIIFM direction yet…" render small and amber, directly under their own subtitles with no divider in between. `tests/e2e/creative-review.spec.ts`'s `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly this change (the Content tab's own "Copy" section divider moving above its title) before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 70/71 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

**Immediate follow-up, a real bug**: the divider still showed above the very first heading in every tab ("Latest Copy Version", "Details", "Creative"). Root cause wasn't the `:not(:first-child)` rule itself — it was that `.msection-h` was never actually the DOM-first child to begin with. Each tab's content sits in a bare `<>...</>` Fragment (no DOM node) directly alongside `.mtabs` (the tab-button bar) inside `Modal`'s `.modal-b` — so `.msection-h` was really the *second* child of `.modal-b` (after `.mtabs`), meaning `:not(:first-child)` matched every single heading including the first, in every tab, every time. Fixed by wrapping each tab's content in a real `<div className="mtabbody">` (Brief/Content/Checks all three) so `:first-child` evaluates against that wrapper instead of `.modal-b` — `.msection-h` is genuinely first there, exactly matching the JSX in all three tabs. No CSS change needed once the DOM shape was actually right.

**Second follow-up, also requested**: the "Add some copy first, then check it against…" disabled-reason text (shown next to a "Check WIIFM"/"Check Brand" button while there's nothing yet to check) moved from inline beside the button to its own line underneath, and changed from `.sub` to the same `.msection-empty` (small, amber) the other empty-state notes already use — `LiveCheck`'s button row now holds only the button and any run error; `disabledReason` renders as its own paragraph directly below.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: confirmed no divider renders above the first heading in any of the three tabs; confirmed a divider now correctly appears above every section after it, including the three generated Check sections; confirmed both disabled-reason messages ("...agency's Reference Material...", "...this client's Knowledge...") render small, amber, and on their own line below their respective buttons — caught and diagnosed a real red herring along the way (the messages briefly appeared entirely absent in one throwaway check; root-caused to the knowledge query not having settled yet at screenshot time, not a rendering bug, confirmed by waiting for it and seeing both messages render correctly). `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the divider-position fix before regenerating again. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 70/71 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

## Client Team contacts: New/Edit Client unified into one modal, guest identity picker on shared review

Three related asks, all against the same real gap: nothing in the schema modeled "who at this client can approve or comment." `NewClientModal.tsx`'s "Primary Approver Email" field had always been pure UI decoration — captured in state, never sent to `useCreateClient`, confirmed by reading `handleSubmit` before touching anything. The public shared-review page asked a guest to freely type a name and email into two plain inputs, remembered only in that browser's `localStorage` — no agency-configured list, no way for staff to manage one.

**Replaced the dead field with a real "Client Team" list** — `supabase/migrations/phase16_client_contacts.sql` adds `client_contacts` (`id, agency_id, client_id, name, email, created_at, archived_at`), staff-write / client-and-staff-read RLS matching `custom_columns`'s own reach exactly. `hooks/use-client-contacts.ts` (read) and `hooks/use-set-client-contacts.ts` (write, full-replace — delete every existing row for the client, insert the submitted set; the list is short and edited occasionally, so this is simpler and no less correct than diffing adds/removes/renames client-side) back it.

**New/Edit Client unified into one modal**, direct instruction — the "..." row menu's rename action now opens the exact same form a new client does, not a stripped-down name-only one. `components/clients/ClientModal.tsx` (`mode: "create" | "edit"`, mirroring `CreativeModal.tsx`'s own precedent) replaces both `NewClientModal.tsx` and `RenameClientModal.tsx` outright — Name, Industry, and the new Client Team rows editor (a small bespoke two-field add/remove-row list, styled after `ListEditor.tsx`'s own `.brow`/`.brx`/`.badd` shape, which doesn't fit directly since its rows are single-field). `hooks/use-update-client.ts` (name + industry in one write) replaces `use-rename-client.ts`, which is now gone. The dashboard row menu's item relabelled **"Edit"** (was "Rename"), since it now opens the full editor.

**A guest on the public review page picks their identity from that list instead of typing it** — confirmed with the user directly before building anything: a closed dropdown could lock out a real client contact who hasn't been added yet, so it's a dropdown *plus* a "Someone else" fallback that reveals today's free-text inputs unchanged, not a hard-required list. `get_shared_review` (the one RPC the public page's data fetch already calls) gained one more lookup (`projects.client_id`) and one more `jsonb_agg` to return that client's active contacts in the same round trip — no second RPC, and `app/api/shared-review/route.ts` needed no change at all, since it already just spreads whatever the RPC returns. `GuestComposer.tsx` (shared by both the phone and desktop layouts) renders the picker only when contacts exist; picking a real contact auto-fills name/email and hides the free-text inputs; picking "Someone else" (or an empty contacts list) reveals them exactly as before. Once an identity is set — picked or typed — the composer already switched to its "Commenting as X" pill and stopped rendering the picker/inputs at all; this is pre-existing behavior, now also correctly covering a picked identity, not something new built here.

**A plain, unguarded login link added to the same page** — `<a href="/login">Log in</a>` next to the identity control, present but never required (confirmed the whole flow — picking a contact, posting a comment, approving — works with zero interaction with that link). Deliberately scoped narrowly: a plain entry point back to the real app, not a session-aware rework of this anonymous page — no such link existed anywhere on `/review/[token]` before (grepped exhaustively).

**A real, unrelated fixture-teardown gap found and fixed while verifying**: `client_contacts` was missing from `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s agency-id-wide teardown sweep — same class of gap as `calendar_views` a few rounds earlier in this doc (a table that had never been written to by any earlier spec, so the gap never surfaced until this round's own test first inserted into it). Added to the sweep, before `clients`; two agencies already orphaned by this gap during manual verification cleaned up directly against the live database.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. New `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts` helper `createClientContact` seeds a real Client Team row directly. Functionally verified against the real dev server before writing any permanent test: created a client with two Client Team rows via the new modal, confirmed both persisted on reopen (industry included); confirmed the dashboard row's own "Edit" action opens the same modal pre-filled; opened a real shared-review link for that client and confirmed the identity dropdown listed exactly those two contacts plus "Someone else"; confirmed picking a contact hid the free-text inputs and posting a comment under that name worked end to end (and, tested deliberately in the right order, confirmed "Someone else" still reveals free-text inputs *before* an identity gets set — after, the picker itself is gone, matching existing behavior); confirmed the login link renders with the correct `href` and is never touched by a successful comment/approval. `tests/e2e/new-client.spec.ts` updated for the modal's new field ids (`#cName`/`#cInd`, was `#ncName`/`#ncInd`); `tests/e2e/manage-clients-projects.spec.ts` gained a dedicated New/Edit-modal-parity test; `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts` gained two new tests (identity picker + fallback, login-link presence) and had its two screenshot baselines diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the new login-link line before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 73/74 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

## Shared review: a lighter "Mark as Reviewed" action, and more breathing room around it

Two related follow-ups on the guest review page. First, the gap between the Post button and the section below it (Approve, or now also Mark as Reviewed) read as too tight — the only separator was `.m-decide`'s own `border-top`, a light `var(--line)` easy to miss sitting directly under a button with no margin of its own. Gave `.m-decide` an explicit `margin-top: 10px` on top of its existing padding/border, so the two sections read as visually distinct rather than crowded.

Second, and the more substantial ask: a guest had exactly one way to close out a creative — **Approve** — which carries real workflow weight (advances `stage` to 4, sets `approved_at`/`approved_by_name`/`approved_by_email`) that the guest may not be the one authorized to give, or just isn't ready to. There was no lighter "I looked at this, nothing to add" option. Confirmed the intended weight with the user directly before building anything: a plain acknowledgement, not a hidden approval — posts a comment, same as Approve's own auto-comment, but never touches the creative itself.

**`submit_shared_review_ack`** (`supabase/migrations/phase17_shared_review_ack.sql`) mirrors `submit_shared_approval`'s own validation (token/passcode/name/allowed-creative checks) but only inserts a `comments` row ("Reviewed via shared review link — no changes needed."), skipping the `creatives` update entirely. Deliberately **not** gated on `can_approve` — unlike formal approval, this carries no special permission, so it's available on every link a guest can comment on at all, including ones where Approve itself doesn't render. `hooks/use-shared-actions.ts` gained `useSubmitSharedReviewAck`; `use-review-controller.ts` gained `markReviewed`/`marking`, same shape as `approve`/`approving`. `GuestComposer.tsx`'s `.m-decide` row now always shows a "Mark as Reviewed" button (new `.m-secondary` style — an outline button, visually lighter than `.m-ok`'s solid one, so it doesn't compete with Approve where both are present) alongside Approve when `canApprove` is true, or alone when it isn't.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: confirmed the new spacing reads as a clear section break; confirmed "Mark as Reviewed" renders and works on a link with `can_approve: false` (where Approve itself is absent), posting the expected comment and switching the composer to "Commenting as X" the same way Post/Approve already do; confirmed the creative's own `stage`/`approved_at` are untouched by it (only `submit_shared_approval` ever writes those). New test in `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts` asserts Approve's absence, Mark as Reviewed's presence and effect, and that no "Approved via shared review link" comment appears from it — proving it's genuinely lighter, not a relabelled approval. `shared-review-phone.png`/`shared-review-desktop.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the spacing + new button before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 74/75 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake; `upload-artwork-modal.png` flaked once on its own previously-established AI-model-label timing race, unrelated to this change, confirmed via isolated re-run). `npm run build` clean.

## Approve and Mark as Reviewed made mutually exclusive, and visually matched

Direct follow-up, against a real screenshot: both buttons sat there unconditionally, so a guest could approve a creative in the same breath as leaving unresolved feedback — genuinely contradictory (why approve something you're actively flagging?). Fixed with one rule, driven entirely by the live comment draft, not the thread's prior history: an empty box means there's nothing to mark reviewed *with*, so Approve — a clean, comment-free approval — is what's on offer, and Mark as Reviewed disables; typing feedback means it's no longer a clean approval, so Approve disables and Mark as Reviewed becomes the available closing action instead. Exactly one of the two is ever clickable at a time. `GuestComposer.tsx`'s existing `commentDraft` state (already tracked for the Post button) is the only new input either condition needed — no new state, no schema change.

**"Make the buttons similar to each other"**: `.m-secondary` (Mark as Reviewed) was an outline button next to `.m-ok`'s (Approve) solid one — different visual weight for what are now framed as direct alternatives to each other. Restyled `.m-secondary` to match `.m-ok`'s own solid-fill shape exactly (same padding/radius/font already shared via `.m-decide button`; only the fill colour differs — `var(--action)` blue for Approve, `var(--ink)` dark neutral for Mark as Reviewed), and unified both buttons' `:disabled` look into one shared `.m-decide button:disabled` rule so the greyed-out state reads identically regardless of which one is off.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server with a real `can_approve: true` link (no existing spec had ever created one before — every prior test used the fixture's own `can_approve: false` default): confirmed an empty draft leaves Mark as Reviewed disabled/gray and Approve enabled/blue; confirmed typing feedback flips both — Approve disables/grays, Mark as Reviewed enables/dark-solid; confirmed clearing the draft reverts it. `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s `createSharedLink` gained an optional `{ canApprove }` param (default `false`, preserving every existing caller) to make this reachable in a permanent test at all. New test in `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts` asserts all three states directly via `toBeEnabled()`/`toBeDisabled()`; the earlier "Mark as Reviewed works without approving" test updated to type a comment first, now that the button requires one. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 75/76 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

## Mark as Reviewed replaced with Make Changes — the schema's dormant `exception` field finally gets a real write path

Direct follow-up, against the mutual-exclusivity behavior just shipped above: the user pointed out "Mark as Reviewed" wasn't earning its own weight as a close — it was really standing in for "the creative isn't right yet," which already had a name in the schema. Confirmed with the user directly before touching anything: should "Make Changes" stay a comment-only, cosmetic action like Mark as Reviewed was, or set the real `exception` status (`creatives.exception: 'changes_requested' | 'rejected' | null`) that `bandOf()` (`lib/stage-labels.ts`) has read with "exception always wins over stage" precedence since the phase13 four-stage simplification — with no code path anywhere that had ever actually set it. **User chose the real status.** This means the internal app's Status column, filters and Checks tab all correctly reflect a guest's flagged feedback with zero internal-app code changes — `bandOf()` already had the read side built, this only had to add the write.

`submit_shared_review_ack` is gone; `supabase/migrations/phase18_shared_review_request_changes.sql` replaces it with **`submit_shared_request_changes`** — same token/passcode/name/allowed-creative validation shape as its sibling RPCs, plus `update creatives set exception = 'changes_requested' where id = p_creative_id and stage between 1 and 3` (scoped by the `where`, not gated by an explicit check — an already-approved stage-4 creative just can't take this path, since `creatives_exception_stage`'s own check constraint only allows a non-null exception at stage 1–3; this matches the schema's established convention of a write silently having no effect where it doesn't apply, the same shape RLS-blocked writes already take) before inserting the guest's comment. `get_shared_review` gained `cr.exception` in its creatives column list so the guest page's own data fetch reflects it.

**Two-layered gating, not just the live draft**: Approve now disables on either the *persisted* `active.exception === 'changes_requested'` (survives reload — the point of writing a real status at all) or the *live* `commentDraft.trim()` (typing new feedback right now, before it's even submitted, is the same contradiction). "Make Changes" requires a non-empty draft — there's no "flag changes with no comment" state. `hooks/use-shared-actions.ts`'s `useSubmitSharedRequestChanges` and `use-review-controller.ts`'s `requestChanges`/`requestingChanges` replace the old ack mutation one-for-one.

**"A softer red"**: `.m-changes` (was `.m-secondary`) uses `var(--rose-bg)`/`var(--rose)` — a tinted button, not `.autherr`'s saturated fill — deliberately, since requesting changes is a normal part of review, not an error state. `.m-decide`/`.m-ok`/the shared disabled styling are unchanged from the prior round.

**A real, subtle test bug found and fixed while verifying, not a product bug**: the two new permanent tests initially failed non-deterministically with the DB read showing `exception: null` right after a successful "Make Changes" submission — even though the RPC itself reliably returned `{"status":"ok"}` when inspected directly over the wire. Root-caused empirically (bisecting a debug copy of the test line by line, with response-body and timing instrumentation, rather than guessing): the test's own wait-for-completion check, `expect(page.locator("text=Please fix the CTA colour")).toBeVisible()`, resolved in 2–20ms — a false-positive match against something already on the page (most likely the still-populated draft textarea, since both the phone and desktop `GuestComposer` instances are always simultaneously mounted and neither had cleared its shared `commentDraft` yet at that instant), while the actual RPC round trip measurably took 300–450ms. The test's subsequent `getCreativeStatus()` DB read was racing ahead of the mutation it was meant to be waiting for. Fixed by scoping the wait to the real rendered comment thread instead — `.m-cmt p` (the class the mobile comment list actually renders `c.body` into), which only becomes true once the mutation has genuinely completed and the query has refetched. Confirmed stable across repeated isolated runs before moving on.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Functionally verified against the real dev server before writing any permanent test: a "Make Changes" submission on a fresh `can_approve: false` link posted the comment and set `exception: 'changes_requested'`, visible immediately in the guest thread and confirmed via a direct DB read; Approve stayed correctly absent/disabled throughout, matching each link's own permission and draft state. `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts` gained `getCreativeStatus()` (reads the fixture creative's own `stage`/`exception` directly) for exactly this. The two updated tests in `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts` — "Make Changes sets a real status, not just a comment" and "Approve and Make Changes are mutually exclusive, persistently" (the latter confirming the gate survives a full page reload, i.e. it's reading the persisted field, not local state) — replace the old Mark-as-Reviewed-focused pair, and passed 4/4 repeated isolated runs after the test-race fix above. `shared-review-phone.png`/`shared-review-desktop.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the button-area change (disabled "Make Changes"/"Approve" pair replacing the single "Mark as Reviewed" button) before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 74/76 (the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake, plus `manage-clients-projects.spec.ts`'s Client Team modal test flaking once under full-suite parallel load — unrelated to this round, confirmed passing 2/2 on isolated re-run). `npm run build` clean.

## Guest review's post mockup brought to parity with the internal reference; Approve un-gated; Title Case fixes; a Clients-page divider

Four direct, independent asks in one round.

**The shared review page's own "Instagram post" mockup was missing pieces the internal reference (`app/(app)/creatives/[id]/page.tsx`'s own `.postbox`/`.ig` block) already had**: the format shown under the creative's title was the raw stored key (`ig_feed`, `ig_reel`), not `formatById(...)?.label` — fixed in both `MobileReview.tsx` and `DesktopReview.tsx` (including the desktop creative-list sidebar's own format tag, the same bug in a second spot). The `.ig-h` account header (avatar, name, `•••` menu) and the `.ig-acts` like/comment/share/save icon row existed as dead, never-wired CSS ported from the prototype early in this session — both now actually render in the JSX, reusing those exact classes rather than inventing mobile-specific equivalents, so the guest page's post literally matches the internal one rather than a lookalike. `.ig-cap` gained a `border-top` divider between the media and the caption (there wasn't one — the mobile variant's old bespoke `.m-cap` had this already, the shared desktop/reference `.ig-cap` didn't; unified onto the one shared class this affects the internal reference page too, which was the same gap, not a regression). `MobileReview.tsx`'s bespoke `.m-art`/`.m-cap` are gone entirely, replaced by the same `.ig-h`/`.ig-media`/`.ig-acts`/`.ig-cap` structure `DesktopReview.tsx` and the internal reference already share — one visual language, not three.

**Approve no longer competes with commenting**: direct correction of the mutual-exclusivity behaviour from the round just above — a guest may want to type "love this" and still approve in the same breath, and gating Approve on a live draft (or, until now, a prior "Make Changes") never allowed that. `GuestComposer.tsx`'s `approveDisabled` is now just `approving || alreadyApproved`. This is safe against the schema's own constraint (`creatives_exception_stage`: no non-null `exception` above stage 3) because `submit_shared_approval` already unconditionally set `exception = null` alongside `stage = 4` — confirmed by reading the deployed function before changing anything, not assumed — so approving a creative that had "Make Changes" pending correctly clears that flag server-side, no RPC change needed. The now-inaccurate "Approve stays off until that's addressed" banner text is reworded to "approving it clears that" — still shown as an informational note when changes were requested and the draft is empty, never a blocking claim.

**Clients page**: a `.toolsep` divider (the same 1px vertical rule the Projects/client-workspace page's own filter toolbar already uses between its Active/Archived chips and its secondary filter chips) now sits between the Active/Archived chips and the New Client button, gated behind `confirmedStaff` the same as the button itself so it never appears dangling with nothing after it.

**Title Case**: `lib/project-types.ts`'s `PROJECT_TYPE_OPTS` ("Social media" → "Social Media", "Paid campaign" → "Paid Campaign", "Print and events" → "Print and Events") — the two decorative `.keg` example hints in `NewProjectModal.tsx` that quote these same words updated to match, so the modal doesn't show two different casings for the same term side by side. Confirmed no test or seed data hardcoded the old casing before changing it (this is a free-text column, not an enum — existing rows keep their old casing, invisible today since nothing in the app currently renders a project's raw `type` value anywhere outside this modal's own select). Separately, `.tbl th` (the project/continuous calendar table's own column headers — "Live Date", "Creative Name", "Funnel", etc.) had a `text-transform: uppercase` forcing every already-correctly-cased label into all caps; removed it (and the now-redundant `text-transform: none` override on `.tbl th small`'s sub-labels, which existed only to undo it) — the labels were Title Case in source all along, the CSS was the only thing hiding it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Functionally verified against the real dev server: the guest review post (phone and desktop) now shows "Instagram Feed" under the title, the account header, all four action icons, and the media/caption divider, matching the internal reference; typing feedback on a `can_approve: true` link leaves both Make Changes and Approve enabled together, and Approve stays enabled after a reload even once "Make Changes" has been submitted; the Clients page toolbar shows the new divider; the New Project modal's Type select and its delivery-mode hints read in Title Case; a continuous project's table headers read "Live Date"/"Creative Name"/etc. instead of all caps. `tests/e2e/shared-review-public.spec.ts`'s "Approve and Make Changes are mutually exclusive, persistently" test — which asserted exactly the behaviour just reversed — rewritten to "Approve stays available alongside drafted feedback and a prior Make Changes", asserting the new, opposite guarantee instead of deleted outright, since the same reload-persistence shape (does the check survive a full page reload, not just local state) was still worth keeping. `shared-review-phone.png`/`shared-review-desktop.png`/`dashboard.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly these changes before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 75/76 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

## Make Changes stays clickable like Approve; a full-width meta divider; long table cells clamp-and-hover; Post Copy's version label always shown; two overweight cells fixed

Five direct follow-ups. The first was checked with the user first, since it changes the RPC's own contract, not just a client-side condition — "should the box require text at all, or should the button just look less broken when it doesn't" were genuinely different fixes; **user chose: stay clickable with an empty box, matching Approve exactly.**

**Make Changes no longer requires draft text.** `GuestComposer.tsx`'s disabled condition drops back to just `requestingChanges` (Approve's own shape from the round above). `supabase/migrations/phase19_shared_request_changes_optional_body.sql` makes `submit_shared_request_changes`'s `p_body` optional — an empty one now falls back to a fixed `'Changes requested via shared review link.'` comment, the same shape `submit_shared_approval` already uses for its own no-input "Approved via shared review link." message. `use-review-controller.ts`'s `requestChanges` no longer early-returns on an empty draft either.

**A full-width divider between the creative's own title/format meta and the Instagram mock below it** — there wasn't one; `.m-meta` (mobile) and a new bare `.cmeta` rule (desktop — previously only styled inside `.postbox`, the internal reference page's own wrapper, so the plain guest-page usage had no border at all) both gained a `border-bottom`, replacing an ad-hoc margin with an actual rule spanning the full card width.

**Long table cells clamp to a few lines and reveal the complete text on hover, the same affordance Post Copy already had.** Concept, Approach Notes, and Client Feedback previously rendered unclamped, unlimited-height text wherever it happened to wrap; they now use the exact same `.copyc`/`.cc-t` (`-webkit-line-clamp`, density-aware via the existing `--d-clamp` variable) markup Post Copy/Image on Text already used, so all five text-heavy columns now behave identically. Since Concept/Approach/Feedback have no version history the way copy does, a new small `FullTextPopover.tsx` reuses the same `.copypop` hover-card shell `CopyVersionHistoryPopover` already established, but renders the plain complete text instead of a "V{n} · Latest" version list — a dedicated small piece rather than forcing the version-history component into a shape it isn't (no version numbers exist for these fields).

**Post Copy's own cell now shows which version is latest even without hovering** — a new `.cc-v` label ("V2", small/muted/uppercase, styled after the popover's own row headers) sits above the clamped text in the base cell state; previously that was only visible inside the hover popover's per-row list.

**Two cells were bigger/bolder than the rest of the row's content, not by design**: `.pname` (the Asset Name/Link cell, shared by both calendar tables) was 13.5px/500-weight against every other cell's `--d-fs` (12px) regular weight — dropped to match. The Week column's value was wrapped in a stray `<b>` (Date and Day, right next to it, were not) — removed; it now reads the same weight as its neighbours.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Functionally verified against the real dev server: clicking Make Changes with nothing typed sets `exception: 'changes_requested'` and posts the fixed fallback message, confirmed via a direct DB read, and the button stays enabled immediately after; the divider spans the full card width on both phone and desktop; Concept/Approach/Client Feedback all clamp to three lines with real long seeded data and a hover reveals the complete text in the popover; Post Copy's cell shows "V2" (plus "+1 earlier") in its base, non-hover state; Asset Name/Link and Week now render at the same size/weight as their row's other cells. Two real test/behaviour mismatches caught by the full suite and fixed, not glossed over: `project-calendar.spec.ts`'s frozen-column test asserted `td.sk1 b` (the now-removed bold wrapper) — updated to `td.sk1`; and `shared-review-public.spec.ts`'s two Make-Changes tests asserted the button `toBeDisabled()` on an empty box, exactly the behaviour item one reverses — rewritten to assert `toBeEnabled()`, plus a new dedicated test for the empty-box-and-fallback-message path. `shared-review-phone.png`/`shared-review-desktop.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the divider + no-longer-greyed button before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 76/77 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

## Make Changes pulled from the guest UI for now; a plain Close button in its place

Direct instruction, reversing the round just above: "Make Changes" is gone from `GuestComposer.tsx` — Approve is the only real action left, alongside a new, unconditional **Close** button (`window.close()`).

Deliberately a UI-only removal, not a rip-out — "for now" reads as reversible, so `submit_shared_request_changes` (the RPC), its migration, and `useSubmitSharedRequestChanges`/`requestChanges`/`requestingChanges` in `use-shared-actions.ts`/`use-review-controller.ts` are untouched and still callable; only `GuestComposer.tsx`'s own consumption of them (the button, its handler, the `changesRequested` banner) is gone. Bringing the button back later is a small, contained diff, not a re-build. `hooks/use-shared-review.ts`'s comment on the `exception` field updated to say so plainly, rather than describing a UI path that no longer exists.

**Close is a best-effort action, disclosed as such in code, not oversold in the UI**: `window.close()` only actually closes a tab a script opened itself — a browser refuses it (usually silently) on a tab the guest navigated to directly, which is how a shared link is normally opened. There's no reliable way to detect that refusal from the page, so the button makes no claim beyond attempting it; no fallback copy invented for the failure case, since there's nothing accurate to say about it either way. Styled `.m-close` — outlined, not filled, so it doesn't compete visually with Approve the way a second solid button would.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally verified against the real dev server: "Make Changes" is gone from both a `can_approve: true` and a `can_approve: false` link; Close renders on both; Approve still works end to end (`stage` reaches 4, confirmed via a direct DB read, button relabels "Approved"). The three permanent tests built around Make Changes in the round above replaced with one consolidated test covering exactly the new shape (button absent, Close present on both link types, Approve's real effect). `shared-review-phone.png`/`shared-review-desktop.png` diffed against actual and confirmed as exactly the button swap before regenerating. Unit suite 47/47. Full e2e suite: 74/75 (only the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake). `npm run build` clean.

Scoped to `.postbox` rather than the shared `.bf-b` (the collapsible section body wrapper `CollapsibleSection.tsx` also uses for Brief and Checks) specifically because that class is shared across all three tabs — widening it would have changed Brief/Checks spacing too, which wasn't asked for and wasn't touched.
