## Comment intelligence, Phase 1: classify every comment on arrival, a repeat-issue warning at brief time

`docs/frank-data-intelligence.pdf`, shared directly by the user, sketches ten data-intelligence features built on records Frank already keeps but never reads back. All ten is a multi-month effort; the doc's own "what to build first" list is explicit that everything else depends on one foundation existing first, so this round builds exactly that foundation and the two things it most directly unlocks — confirmed with the user via a planning pass before any code: **classify every comment into a small fixed taxonomy on arrival** (including internal staff comments, not just client/guest ones), **a category-level repeat-issue count**, and **a brief-time warning drawn from it**. Issue-cost-in-days, approver patterns, approval risk, revision archaeology, knowledge-gap prompts, client health and cross-agency pooling are the doc's own later phases, deliberately not touched here.

**The taxonomy**: the doc's own eight categories, plus one addition of ours, disclosed rather than assumed — `no_issue`, for a comment that isn't a correction at all (plain praise, the fixed "Approved via shared review link." message). `supabase/migrations/phase20_comment_issue_category.sql` adds `comments.issue_category`/`issue_category_note` (a short one-sentence rationale from the model, so a bare category doesn't read as a black box) plus a check constraint restricting it to those nine values, and updates the three comment-inserting RPCs (`submit_shared_comment`, `submit_shared_approval`, `submit_shared_request_changes`) to `returning id` and include it in their response — needed so the client has something to hand the classifier.

**Classification is asynchronous and best-effort, never something a comment's own posting waits on** — confirmed directly with the user before building, since a synchronous version would add real LLM latency to the Post button on the anonymous guest page especially. `lib/ai/build-classify-comment-prompt.ts` (`buildClassifyCommentPrompt`/`parseClassification`) mirrors `build-check-prompt.ts`'s own shape exactly — a pure, unit-tested build/parse pair, one `CATEGORY:`/`NOTE:` block, malformed replies dropped rather than guessed at. It also folds in the comment's own `anchor` (already a rich, free signal this schema has carried since the annotation system was built: a highlight anchor hands the classifier the exact quoted wording, a pin/region anchor says plainly that this is about the artwork, not any text). `app/api/ai/classify-comment/route.ts` is the new route every comment-creation path fires at (never awaited): `hooks/use-create-comment.ts` (now selects the new row's `id`) and all three mutations in `hooks/use-shared-actions.ts`, via a tiny shared `lib/ai/classify-comment-client.ts` helper. It uses the service-role client (`lib/supabase/service-role.ts`, the same one `/api/shared-review` already uses for exactly this reason) rather than a session-bound one, since a guest-authored comment has no session at all — and, matching that file's own documented discipline, trusts nothing about the caller beyond "this `commentId` resolves to a real, recent (≤15 min), not-yet-classified row," so the worst a bad-faith call can do is ask for a real comment to be reclassified. Every AI call goes through the existing `runAiTask` (the agency's monthly cap, model choice, usage logging) exactly like the three existing AI routes — attributed to the creative's own `created_by`, since a guest-authored comment has no real user and `ai_usage_events.user_id` is `not null` (a deliberate, disclosed simplification, not an oversight).

**A real, easy-to-miss auth bug found and fixed while verifying, not assumed away**: the first live test showed the classify request being silently redirected to `/login` — `lib/supabase/middleware.ts` gates every route behind a session by default, and the new route wasn't on its public-path allowlist (`/login`, `/review`, `/api/shared-review`). Added `/api/ai/classify-comment` alongside `/api/shared-review`, the exact same reasoning already documented there (a guest page with no session needs to reach it too, and the route validates its own input rather than trusting the caller). Caught by watching the actual network response in a throwaway script, not by reasoning about the middleware in the abstract — it returned the login page's HTML with a 200, which would have read as "success" from a less careful check.

**The repeat-issue count is category-level for this phase** ("3 Craft and Layout issues on Instagram Story in the last 90 days"), not the doc's own finer example ("text too close to the interface edge" recognised as the same issue every time it recurs) — confirmed with the user directly, since the finer version needs a second clustering pass (embeddings or an LLM comparison step) this phase doesn't build. `hooks/use-repeat-issues.ts`'s `useRepeatIssueCount(clientId, format, windowDays=90)` follows the exact plain-select-plus-client-side-aggregate shape `use-client-list-stats.ts`/`use-agency-creative-stats.ts` already established (no new RPC/aggregate function — that's an explicit, repeated convention in this codebase, not an oversight) — a two-step fetch (this client's creative ids for the format, then a count of matching comments among them, since `creatives` has no `client_id` of its own) — and only reports a category once it has 2+ occurrences; a single comment isn't a repeat issue.

**The brief-time warning** sits in `CreativeModal.tsx`'s Brief tab, right after the Content Type/Format row — the exact place the doc itself calls "the highest-value placement, because it costs nothing to act on." Reuses `.note` (`app/(app)/settings/knowledge/page.tsx`'s own info-banner class) rather than inventing a new style, renders nothing while loading or when there's nothing to say, and only appears in create mode — an existing creative's format is already fixed, so there's nothing left to warn about changing.

**A real, unrelated fixture-teardown gap found and fixed while verifying**: `ai_usage_events` was missing from `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s agency-id-wide sweep — same class of gap as `calendar_views`/`client_contacts`/`shared_links` earlier in this doc, never surfaced before because no earlier spec had ever triggered a real AI call. Added before `agencies`/`users`, same reasoning as the others; two agencies already orphaned by it during manual verification cleaned up directly against the live database in dependency order.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. New unit tests for `buildClassifyCommentPrompt`/`parseClassification` (9 cases: every category listed, anchor context for highlight vs. pin/region vs. none, well-formed/case-insensitive/unknown-category/missing-field parsing) — same style as `build-check-prompt.test.ts`. Functionally verified against the real dev server before writing any permanent test: a claim-shaped guest comment ("This cream removes wrinkles in 3 days.") landed as `claim_or_compliance` with a sensible note within seconds, confirmed via a direct DB read, while the Post button itself completed in well under 100ms; a plain "Looks great, love it!" landed as `no_issue`; an internal authenticated-app comment about a wrong price landed as `factual_error`; seeding two `craft_and_layout` comments across two Instagram Story creatives for one client and opening New Brief showed "This client has raised 2 Craft and Layout issues on Instagram Story in the last 90 days." exactly, and showed nothing at all when switching the format to Instagram Feed. Permanent e2e coverage: `shared-review-public.spec.ts` gained a test confirming the classify request fires with the right `commentId` and that posting never waits on it (the real AI response isn't asserted in a permanent test — not deterministic enough for CI, verified manually instead, as above); new `tests/e2e/brief-time-warning.spec.ts` (2 tests) seeds already-classified comments directly and confirms the warning's exact text, its format-scoping, and that a single occurrence never counts as a repeat issue. `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts` gained `getCommentByBody`. Unit suite 56/56. Full e2e suite: 76/78 (only the two pre-existing, already-documented environmental flakes above — no new failures). `npm run build` clean, new `/api/ai/classify-comment` route present in the build output.

**Deliberately not built this phase, in the doc's own order**: issue cost in days/rounds (blocked on a real decision — there's no `stage_history` audit table today, so "time in Client Review" specifically isn't derivable, only full `created_at → approved_at` lifecycle — a call for that phase, not this one), approver patterns and approval risk at share time, revision archaeology feeding the drafter, knowledge-gap prompts, client health timing signals, cross-agency pattern pooling, and any client-facing "X% preference, not fault" report.

## Opening motion across the interface — a deliberate addition, not a port

Frank's modals, menus, popovers, collapsible sections and tab panels all appeared instantly. The prototype does the same: it switches every one of them with a bare `display` toggle (`.scrim.on{display:flex}`, `.colpop.on`, `.evpop.on`, `.brief.open .bf-b`), and its only real motion is the `.toast` slide-in and the `.review` grid transition. So everything here is new behaviour the prototype doesn't have, recorded here per the parity rules rather than slipped in. Two scope decisions confirmed with the user first: **opening only, CSS only** (closing stays instant, no animation library), and **subtle** (120–180ms, a few pixels of movement), since this is a tool people open the same menu in dozens of times a day.

**Why opening-only costs almost nothing:** every one of these surfaces mounts through a React conditional (`{open && <Modal/>}`, `{hover && <Popover/>}`, `{open && <div className="bf-b">}`), and a CSS `@keyframes` animation plays by itself every time an element mounts. So the whole change lives in `app/globals.css`, with no component changes. Animating the close would have meant a "closing" state or a library at every one of those mount points, which the user chose not to take on.

**What's in `globals.css`:** four tokens in `:root` (`--ease-out`/`--ease-std`, reusing the prototype's own two curves from `.toast` and `.review` rather than new ones, plus `--dur-fast: .12s` and `--dur: .18s`) and three keyframes (`fade-in`, `rise-in`, `pop-in`), each defining only its starting state so an element's normal styles are always the end state. Applied to: `.scrim` (fade) and `.modal` (pop); `.colpop`, which covers every click menu (Columns, the row "…" menu, the view-tab menu, the Save dropdown; rise); `.evpop`/`.copypop` hover previews, **opacity only**, since a preview that moves under the pointer feels jumpy and `project-calendar.spec.ts` moves the mouse onto `.evpop`'s bounding box the moment it appears; `.brief.open .bf-b` collapsible bodies (rise); `.mtabbody` (fade, which plays on every CreativeModal tab switch because each tab is its own conditional block); `.note`/`.autherr` banners and inline errors (rise); and the review page's `.phone`/`.browser` frames (fade). Those two stay mounted and toggle `display`, and a CSS animation restarts whenever an element goes from `display: none` to shown, so the Phone/Desktop switch animates too.

**Reduced motion was only half-covered before this:** the existing `prefers-reduced-motion` rule turned off `transition` but not `animation`. It now turns off both. Because every keyframe only sets a starting state, disabling animation just shows everything in its final position, with nothing left stuck invisible.

**Not included:** the prototype's `.toast` slide-in (the app has no toasts to animate) and the `.review` grid transition (already left out on purpose above, since the feed panel it animates doesn't exist). `.daypop` also has CSS, but no component uses it.

**Verified**: functionally checked against the real dev server with real timing, not Playwright's screenshot default. Opening the New Client modal, the row "…" menu, a collapsible Brief section and the review page's Desktop toggle each showed the expected animation running from opacity 0 at the moment of opening, then fully at rest with no animation left by ~400ms. The modal's resting screenshot sits exactly where it did before. With `reducedMotion: "reduce"` emulated, the modal reported `animation-name: none` and opacity 1 the instant it opened. `project-calendar.spec.ts` run on its own first (13/13, including both hover-popover tests), then the full suite: 76/78, the pre-existing `settings-team.png` flake plus `dashboard.png`'s unrelated date rollover (Sep 27 → Sep 28 in Last Activity, diffed and regenerated). No screenshot baseline changed because of the motion, as expected, since Playwright's `toHaveScreenshot` jumps finite animations to their end state. Unit suite 56/56. `npm run build` clean.

## Hover previews, popovers and menus always stay fully on-screen

A direct report against a real screenshot: hovering a creative in the calendar grid's bottom row opened a preview card that ran off the bottom of the screen, cut off mid-image. The user asked for this to be fixed everywhere, not just on that page.

**Root cause:** every anchored pop-up positioned itself with its own hand-written maths, and most of them *guessed* their own height. `CreativePreviewPopover` assumed 340px, but the real card is taller than that once its caption loads, which happens *after* it opens. So the "does it fit?" check passed on the guess while the real card overflowed. The same pattern was repeated nine times with slightly different rules: `CreativePreviewPopover`, `CopyVersionHistoryPopover`, `FullTextPopover`, `ColumnsPopover`, `RowActionsMenu`, and the inline view-tab menu and Save dropdown in both `ProjectCalendarTable` and `ContinuousCalendarTable`. Several of them only clamped horizontally, so a row menu near the bottom of a long list could also run off-screen.

**One shared mechanism instead of nine guesses:**
- `lib/viewport-fit.ts`'s `computeFit` is pure and unit-tested. Given the pop-up's *measured* size, the trigger's box and the viewport, it returns left/top: `"below"` a trigger (flipping above when there's more room there, start- or end-aligned), or `"beside"` it (right of the trigger, flipping left). When the pop-up is taller than any room available, it takes the roomier side and returns a `maxHeight` so the content scrolls inside rather than spilling off. It always keeps an 8px margin from every edge.
- `hooks/use-viewport-fit.ts` runs that in a layout effect, before paint, so nothing visibly jumps. It measures `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, which ignore transforms, so the new opening animation's few pixels of movement can't skew the measurement. It re-measures through a `ResizeObserver`, which is what fixes the reported case, since the creative preview grows as its caption and comments arrive. It also re-measures on window resize. It writes position straight onto the element rather than through React state, avoiding a second render just to move a box.

All nine call sites now just attach a ref and call the hook; each one's hardcoded size guesses are gone.

**Checked but deliberately not changed:** `.hl-composer`, the caption-highlight comment box, sits in the normal page flow, so it can't overflow. `.annot-composer`, the comment box that opens when you drop a pin or draw a region on the artwork, is positioned *inside the artwork* (percentages plus its own edge-flip) rather than against the screen, and moves with the image as you scroll. It's a click-opened box rather than a hover, and making it screen-aware is a different mechanism. Flagged to the user, not silently folded in.

**A real, unrelated teardown race found and fixed while verifying:** the full suite logged one fixture's cleanup failing at `agencies`. A direct DB query showed everything had been swept except a single `ai_usage_events` row, logged *after* the sweep. This came from the comment-classification work above: a comment posted near the end of a test fires a background classify call, and if the AI reply lands mid-teardown, its usage row appears after `ai_usage_events` was already cleared and blocks the agency delete on the FK. It only happens in tests; in the real app nothing deletes an agency seconds after a comment. `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s `agencies` step now re-sweeps `ai_usage_events` and retries until the agency is gone. After that, any even-later usage insert just fails its own FK harmlessly. The one orphaned agency was cleaned up directly, and a clean re-run confirmed zero teardown failures.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Ten new unit tests for `computeFit` (below/beside placement, end-alignment, flipping above/left, both horizontal edges, the reported slide-up-near-the-bottom case, and the too-tall-for-anywhere case that pins and scrolls). Functionally verified against the real dev server at 1280×800: reproduced the exact reported case, a creative in the calendar grid's bottom row with a long caption, and after its content loaded the card measured fully on-screen (bottom edge 792px, the 8px margin) with the whole caption visible. The Columns popover on a 420px-tall window placed itself in the roomier space and scrolled internally instead of overflowing, and the dashboard's row "…" menu at the right edge clamped inside the screen. Unit suite 66/66. Full e2e suite: 76/78, only the two pre-existing environmental flakes (`settings-team.png`, `settings-knowledge.png`'s dev-tooling overlay), with zero teardown failures on the re-run. `npm run build` clean.

## Typeface swapped from Inter to Urbanist — a deliberate divergence

Direct ask: replace the prototype's Inter with Urbanist everywhere. The prototype stays on Inter (it's read-only, and this is exactly the kind of intentional divergence this file exists to record rather than silently reproduce). `app/layout.tsx` now loads `Urbanist` from `next/font/google` instead of `Inter`, keeping the same shape (`variable: "--font-urbanist"`, `subsets: ["latin"]`, applied via `className` on `<html>`); `app/globals.css`'s `body` rule points `font-family` at the new variable.

**`font-feature-settings: "cv05", "ss01"` dropped, `letter-spacing: -0.006em` and `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` kept.** The prototype-parity skill flags all three as tied specifically to Inter's own metrics. `cv05`/`ss01` are Inter-specific OpenType stylistic-set numbers (alternate glyph shapes Inter itself defines at those slots) — meaningless once the body font is a different family, so left in would either do nothing or, worse, silently pick up whatever unrelated feature Urbanist happens to register under the same numbers. `tabular-nums` is a generic feature most text fonts including Urbanist support, kept for the same reason the prototype wanted it (aligned figures in tables). `letter-spacing: -0.006em` is a plain tracking value, not Inter-specific in the way the other two are, so it was left as-is rather than guessed at for Urbanist's different metrics — worth a second look if the tighter tracking reads wrong now that a human has seen it rendered, but not changed speculatively here.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean (`app/layout.tsx`, `app/globals.css`). Functionally confirmed against the real dev server: `getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily` reports `Urbanist` first, and the dashboard, a client workspace, and a creative's review page all render visibly in the new typeface with no overflow, wrapping, or truncation regressions. Full e2e suite surfaced exactly the expected fallout and nothing else: 12 of 78 tests failed, every one a `toHaveScreenshot` diff (0 functional/logic failures) — every diff image was opened before touching any baseline, and each one showed the identical signature (only glyph pixels flagged, every box, border, and button in its original position; the one visibly different case, `client-workspace-darwin.png`, was a column header re-wrapping from two lines to one, still fully contained, not clipped or overlapping). Baselines regenerated with `--update-snapshots`; full suite re-run afterward, clean 78/78. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean.

## Every font-size and letter-spacing value bumped +1px, app-wide

Direct follow-up to the Urbanist swap above: reported as reading slightly smaller than Inter did at the same declared sizes. Two scope questions confirmed with the user before touching anything, since this codebase hand-ports every element's own explicit `font-size`/`letter-spacing` (no shared type scale, no `rem`) — so "increase sizes by 1pt" could have meant a 2-line tweak to the `body` rule alone, or a mechanical pass over every declaration in `globals.css`: user chose the latter, global. Same question for letter-spacing, since 19 of the file's 20 declarations are `px` (ranging `-0.8px`–`1px`, in fine 0.1–0.3px steps) and a flat +1px is large against values already that fine (`-0.3px` → `0.7px`, `1px` → `2px`): user chose the literal flat +1px reading over a smaller proportional nudge.

**Mechanical, not hand-retyped** (the prototype-parity skill's "extract, don't retype" rule, applied to a generated bump instead of a copied value): a small one-off script read every `font-size:`/`--d-fs:`/`letter-spacing:` declaration in `app/globals.css` matching a literal `px` number and added 1, preserving decimal precision (`11.5px` → `12.5px`, not rounded). 189 `font-size` declarations, the 2 `--d-fs` density tokens (compact `12px`→`13px`, comfortable `13px`→`14px`), 15 `px`-based `letter-spacing` declarations, and 4 unitless `letter-spacing: 0` resets (→ `1px`) were all bumped this way. `letter-spacing: 0` was included deliberately — the user's own scope choice ("every declaration") didn't carve out zero-resets as a special case, and `0` is exactly as real a declared value as `0.3px`.

**One value needed a manual decision the script couldn't make**: the `body` rule's own `letter-spacing: -0.006em` — the file's only `em`-based tracking value, left alone during the Urbanist swap above. A flat "+1" has no sensible meaning in `em` next to 19 `px` siblings, so it was converted: `-0.006em` at the pre-bump `14px` body size ≈ `-0.084px`, then the same flat `+1px` every other declaration got, landing on `0.916px` — written directly as `px`, not re-expressed in `em`, so it reads consistently with the rest of the file rather than as a unit outlier.

**Four inline `fontSize` style props outside `globals.css`** — `components/review/GuestComposer.tsx`, `components/clients/ClientModal.tsx` (×1 each, both `11.5`→`12.5`), and `app/(app)/settings/team/page.tsx` (×2, `13.5`→`14.5` and `13`→`14`) — found by grepping for `fontSize` across `.tsx`, bumped by hand to match, since leaving them out would have meant three specific spots on two screens quietly staying smaller than everything around them.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean on every touched file. Functionally confirmed against the real dev server: `getComputedStyle(document.body).fontSize` reports `15px`; the dashboard and a creative's review page both screenshotted visibly larger, more readable text with no overflow, clipping, or wrapping regressions. Full e2e suite surfaced the expected fallout and nothing else on the first clean pass: the same 12 of 78 tests that flagged for the Urbanist swap above flagged again, all `toHaveScreenshot` diffs, 0 functional failures — diffs reviewed (login, dashboard, client workspace, project calendar, upload-artwork modal) before touching any baseline, all showing text reflow only, nothing clipped or overlapping (the widest table view, client workspace's project row, rewrapped a header from one line to two, still fully contained). Baselines regenerated with `--update-snapshots`.

**Full-suite re-verification was blocked by the environment, not the change, and that's disclosed rather than papered over**: three consecutive full-suite attempts after the baseline regen each failed a different, effectively random subset of tests (7, then 8, then 5 more on a single-worker retry) — always navigation timeouts (`page.goto`/`waitForURL` exceeding 30s) or, on the last attempt, `Tearing down "frank" exceeded the test timeout` and individual tests running 2–13 minutes instead of ~10 seconds. None of the failures were screenshot diffs, and none clustered around anything CSS-related. `top` showed severe memory pressure at the time (17G used, 67MB unused, ~9GB in the compressor) from many consecutive full Playwright runs earlier in the same session, and re-running the first 7 failures in isolation, and then the next 8 single-threaded, passed 31/31 and 43/48 respectively (the remaining 5 single-worker failures were the same timeout/teardown-timeout signature, including a 13-minute single test — a machine-load artifact, not a 46th distinct pattern). Confirmed via direct `ps`/`top`/`df` checks, not assumed. Two `E2E Test Agency` rows orphaned by the timed-out teardowns were found and swept by hand, same tables/order as `fixtures.ts`'s own sweep; a further ~31 stray `e2e-*` auth users were found in `public.users` with older timestamps and naming patterns (`pdfcheck`, `draftsrc`, `headersave`, etc.) not matching this session's work or the current fixture — left alone as a separate, pre-existing cleanup debt rather than mass-deleted without more context. Given this, a full clean 78/78 run under normal system load is still owed and not claimed here — what's actually verified is: the change itself (direct visual check), zero screenshot regressions beyond the expected/reviewed twelve, zero functional-test failures across every attempt, and that every failure seen was reproducibly navigation/teardown-timeout-shaped rather than assertion-shaped. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean.

## Letter-spacing bump walked back from +1px to +0.2px — direct feedback after seeing it rendered

Direct follow-up to the font-size/letter-spacing bump above: the +1px letter-spacing read as too much once the user actually saw it (font-size at +1px was confirmed fine and left untouched). Asked whether there was an in-between rather than assuming a full revert to the original was wanted; the "+0.2px small uniform nudge" option sketched (but not chosen) during the original scope question was still on the table, so applied that instead of re-litigating from scratch.

Every current letter-spacing value was already exactly `original + 1px` (mechanically derived, recorded in the entry above), so reaching `original + 0.2px` was a uniform `current − 0.8px` across all 20 declarations — verified arithmetically against the recorded original values before running it, not just assumed consistent. The body rule's comment (previously describing the em-to-px conversion in terms of the old +1px figure) was corrected to match; the derived value itself is `0.116px` (`-0.084px` at the pre-bump 14px size, `+0.2px`).

**Verified**: typecheck clean. `getComputedStyle(document.body).letterSpacing` confirmed as `0.116px` against the real dev server; a diff image that first looked like new text wrapping (login page) turned out to be diff-overlay ghosting from the two screenshots' slightly different vertical offset, not real wrapping — confirmed by reading the plain "actual" screenshot on its own, single line, fully contained. The 12 screenshot-bearing spec files most likely to move were run at reduced concurrency (`--workers=2`, following the environmental flakiness noted above) first without `--update-snapshots` to review every diff: all 12 failed as expected, all `toHaveScreenshot`-only, 0 functional failures; login and settings-team screenshots reviewed directly, clean. Baselines regenerated, same 12 files re-run clean (33/33 including all their non-visual tests). A subsequent full-suite pass at `--workers=2` surfaced 4 more failures, all in the same navigation-timeout/teardown-timeout shape as before (`top` still showing ~48MB free, ~8.5GB compressed) — none were screenshot diffs, consistent with environmental load rather than a regression, and the same conclusion as above applies: not re-chased with more full runs. One more pair of orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows from this round's timed-out teardown found and swept the same way. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean.

## Letter-spacing nudged again, +0.2px → +0.4px

Second direct follow-up: +0.2px still read as slightly tight once seen rendered. Same mechanism as the first walk-back — every current value was exactly `original + 0.2px`, so a uniform `current + 0.2px` across all 20 declarations reached `original + 0.4px` (body's derived value: `0.316px`, i.e. `-0.084px` + `0.4px`), verified arithmetically against the recorded originals before running. The body rule's comment updated to match.

One regeneration pass missed `project-calendar-darwin.png` — that test's own login step hit the same environmental navigation-timeout flakiness documented above before ever reaching its screenshot assertion, so `--update-snapshots` had nothing to compare against and silently skipped it rather than failing. Caught by counting "is re-generated" log lines against the 12 expected files rather than assuming a 0-exit-code update run meant every target file was actually touched; the one file re-run on its own to regenerate it.

A separate, unrelated one-off flake surfaced and was run down rather than assumed away: `upload-artwork-modal.png` diffed by ~4600px (0.01 ratio) on the very next run against its own just-regenerated baseline, entirely localized to the "Draft with Claude Opus 5" button's width — nothing else on the page differed. Re-ran that single test twice more with zero code changes in between: passed clean both times against the same, untouched baseline. Confirmed as a pre-existing rendering-timing flake on that specific AI-labeled button (same class as the project's already-documented `settings-team.png`/`settings-knowledge.png` flakes), not something the letter-spacing change caused.

**Verified**: typecheck clean. Screenshot-bearing specs re-run clean end to end: 33/33. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows after this round.

## Creative review: Brief/Content/Checks accordions replaced with a single collapsible side nav, Feed Preview folded in

Direct ask, with a reference screenshot (monday.com's collapsible "Workspace" panel — an icon-rail-adjacent list of links that can expand/collapse, main content showing only the selected item). Investigated first rather than assumed: there are two separate Brief/Content/Checks implementations in this codebase — `CreativeModal`'s own tab bar (`.mtabs`/`.mtab`, real exclusive `activeTab` state, untouched by this change) and, on the creative review page itself, three independent `CollapsibleSection` accordions (`BriefPanel`, the inline Content block, `ChecksPanel`) that could all be open at once. Only the page's version is what the reference screenshot's "single content window" behavior meant, confirmed by re-reading the actual screenshots against the two candidates before writing any code.

**New**: `components/creative-review/ReviewNav.tsx` — a single `activeSection` selection (`"brief" | "content" | "checks" | "feed"`, default `"content"`, matching the one section that used to default open) replaces the three independent accordions. Its collapse toggle (chevron path lifted from the prototype's `#fpHide`/`.feedrail` buttons — a different feature there, the Instagram grid panel, reused here only for the glyph) hides the link list down to a bare 42px strip, `.review.navcollapsed` shrinking that grid column the same way `.review`'s own first column already did for the (also-unbuilt) feed-preview reservation.

**Feed Preview folded in, per direct instruction**: it was previously a permanent 300px placeholder column (`.feed-rail`), always visible, nothing behind it. It's now the fourth `ReviewNav` link, rendered with the same `.awaiting` empty-state treatment `Content` already uses for its own "No artwork yet" state, in the same width `.canvas` context as the other three sections — a placement change, not a feature build; still nothing behind it, same as before.

**`BriefPanel`/`ChecksPanel` stopped owning their own accordion header** — the `ReviewNav` selection makes a per-panel expand/collapse button redundant. Rather than inventing a new box style for their content, both now use the exact `className="brief open"` / `<div className="bf-b" style={{ display: "block" }}>` shape `app/(app)/settings/brand/page.tsx` already established for "this box, always open, no header" (grepped for other `.brief`/`.bf-b` usage before writing anything, specifically to avoid a parallel one-off class). `components/ui/CollapsibleSection.tsx` had exactly three call sites, all three replaced by this change, leaving it with zero remaining usages — deleted outright rather than left as dead code, confirmed via grep after the edits, not before. Its `.bf-h` CSS (the header button, `.bf-h b`/`.bf-h .bn`/`.bf-h svg`/`.brief.open .bf-h svg`) was equally dead once `.brief.open`'s only remaining callers (settings/brand, and this change's `BriefPanel`/`ChecksPanel`) never render a `.bf-h` button — removed for the same reason. `.brief`/`.bf-b` themselves stay, since settings/brand still uses them.

**`.review`'s first grid column** went from a fixed `300px` (sized for the old placeholder box) to `220px` expanded / `42px` collapsed — a plain link list needs less width than a padded dashed-border placeholder did. The stale code comment above `.review` (explaining `.feed-rail` as permanently-reserved empty space) was rewritten to describe what's actually there now, rather than left to describe a column that no longer exists.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean on every touched/added file. Functionally confirmed against the real dev server: all four sections show correctly and exclusively (selecting one hides the previous one entirely, unlike the old accordions), the active item highlights (`--action-soft`/`--action`, the same treatment `.stagesw`'s selected pill already uses elsewhere on this same page), the collapse toggle shrinks the column to a bare strip and back, and the last-selected section persists across a collapse/expand cycle. Screenshotted all four states plus the collapsed state directly. The two screenshot baselines that render this page (`creative-review.png`, `upload-artwork-modal.png`) diffed as expected — reviewed before touching either, both showing only the intended layout change — and were regenerated; re-run clean. The four other specs that reach this page without ever touching Brief/Checks/Feed Preview specifically (`comment-resolve`, `comment-visibility`, `share-eligibility`, `stage-transition` — grepped first to confirm none of them reference those three sections, meaning all of them depend only on the unchanged default) re-ran clean, 11/11, zero functional regressions. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Creative review, follow-up: vertical separators, a real single-post template, and a real Feed Preview grid

Direct follow-up, three parts, with a reference mockup (four Instagram phone screens). One claim checked and found already true rather than acted on blind: "the middle header should move with the main content when closing the section" — measured `.stage-h`'s bounding box before/after toggling `ReviewNav`'s collapse (`x: 336 → 158`, width growing to fill the freed column) — it already does, since `.stage` is a plain CSS grid item and resizes with its track automatically. No code change; flagged back to the user in case a different scenario was meant.

**Vertical separators, ≥20px either side**: `.review`'s grid gained two literal 1px separator tracks (`.review-sep`, a real sibling element between `ReviewNav`/`.stage` and between `.stage`/`CommentsPanel`, not a border-on-neighbour trick), and `gap` went from `16px` to `20px` — since `gap` applies to every adjacent track pair including the new separator ones, this gives exactly 20px on both sides of each line for free, verified by measuring all three gaps directly (`20, 20, 20`) rather than eyeballing it.

**Content's single-post template, matched to the 3rd mockup phone**: the existing `.postbox`/`.ig` markup already matched most of it (avatar+username header, square media, caption below) from earlier work — the one real gap was a 4th "bookmark/save" action icon the mockup doesn't show (it has three: heart, comment, share). Removed. Its CSS (`.ig-acts .save`) stayed, since `DesktopReview.tsx`/`MobileReview.tsx` (the guest-facing shared-review pages) still use the identical icon set including that 4th one — grepped before assuming it was dead. The mockup's "123 likes" line was **not** added: confirmed with the user first, since Frank has no real like data for content that isn't published yet, and faking a number here would be the exact thing `CreativePreviewPopover`'s own comment already calls out avoiding ("drops the fabricated like/view counts... no schema backs those") — omitted entirely rather than shown as a dishonest 0 or decorative filler.

**Feed Preview is a real grid now**, matching the 2nd mockup phone: `components/creative-review/FeedPreviewGrid.tsx`, backed by the existing `useCreatives(projectId)` hook (no new query) — every other creative in the *same project* specifically, confirmed with the user over the two other candidates (whole-client, or just this creative's own versions) since a real Instagram grid needs more than one post's worth of data and this page is scoped to one project's work, not a client's entire history. Each tile reuses `PlaceholderArt` (exported from `CreativePreviewPopover.tsx`, previously module-private) rather than fetching a real thumbnail per tile — the same reasoning that component's own comment already gives ("this popover never fetches creative_versions, so there's no real image to show"), extended to a grid of many tiles rather than reinvented. CSS overrides make its 306×230 rectangular card fill a square tile instead (the inner svg's own `preserveAspectRatio="slice"` already crops to fill any box, so this is sizing, not a second crop).

**Feed Preview → Content linking**: clicking the currently-open creative's own tile just switches `activeSection` to `"content"` (no navigation, nothing to navigate to); clicking any other tile calls `router.push`'` to that creative's own `/creatives/[id]` route, since this page is scoped to one creative by URL and there's no other way to display a different one. Its default `activeSection` (`"content"`) means landing there is automatic on the route change, not a second step.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Functionally confirmed end to end against the real dev server with two extra fixture creatives seeded into the same project: the grid rendered exactly 3 tiles, clicking the current creative's own tile switched to Content on the same URL, clicking a different tile navigated to that creative's own page. Separator spacing measured precisely (20/20/20px). The four specs that reach this page without touching Feed Preview/the icon row specifically re-ran clean, 13/13 — including the two screenshot baselines from the prior round, unaffected here since the fixture creative's own screenshot is the empty "No artwork yet" state, which never renders `.ig-acts` at all. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Brief/Checks panels: more padding, scoped to avoid a third caller

Direct ask: more padding around the edges and between each field in Brief/Checks. `.brief`/`.bf-b`/`.bsec` are shared by more than those two — `app/(app)/settings/brand/page.tsx` uses the exact same classes for its own box, and wasn't part of the ask. Checked with a grep before touching anything, then scoped the change instead of editing the shared classes directly: `BriefPanel.tsx`/`ChecksPanel.tsx`'s outer wrapper gained a third class, `reviewpanel`, and the actual padding increase lives on `.reviewpanel .bf-b`/`.reviewpanel .bsec` — `.bf-b`'s own `padding: 2px 0` (barely any edge room at all) and `.bsec`'s `padding: var(--d-list-y) 12px` (8–12px depending on density mode) stay exactly as they were for every other caller.

- `.reviewpanel .bsec`: `var(--d-list-y) 12px` → `calc(var(--d-list-y) + 6px) 12px` — `+6px` on top of whichever density value is active, rather than a fixed value that would silently stop respecting the compact/comfortable toggle.

**Verified**: computed styles measured directly — `.reviewpanel .bf-b` → `12px 14px`, `.reviewpanel .bsec` → `14px 12px` (compact density, `8+6`). Then, to confirm the scoping actually held, navigated to `/settings/brand` in the same run and measured its own `.bf-b` — `2px 0px`, unchanged. Screenshotted the Brief panel directly. ## Fixed 9-post grid height, centered picker, Title Case modal titles, and real artwork instead of only the gradient

Four direct asks in one round.

**Both grids (`.feedgrid`, `.pkgrid`) now reserve a fixed height for exactly 9 posts (3 rows)** regardless of how many actually exist — computed from the card's own box model rather than guessed: `390px` card − `1px` border each side − 2 column-gaps of `2px`, ÷3 columns = `128px` tile width; ×5/4 for the 4:5 ratio = `160px` tile height; ×3 rows + 2 row-gaps of `2px` = `484px`. Fewer than 9 posts leaves blank space below (the grid doesn't stretch existing tiles to fill it); more than 9 scrolls, same `overflow-y: auto` behaviour the picker already had, just no longer an arbitrary `260px` guess.

**ShareModal's `.pkcard` centered** with `margin-inline: auto` — it was left-aligned in the modal's wider content column since nothing positioned it.

**Modal titles matched to Title Case**: grepped every caller of the shared `Modal` component first rather than guessing which ones needed it. Of seven, five were already correct (`New Client`/`Edit Client`, `New Project`, `Rename Project`, `New Brief`, `Rename View`) and one is a real filename (`KnowledgeFilePreviewModal`, deliberately left alone — forcing "photo.jpg" into Title Case would read as broken, not fixed). Two needed a fix: `Share for review` → `Share For Review`, `Save as New View` → `Save As New View`.

**Real artwork on the grid tiles, not just the decorative gradient**: new `components/creative-review/FeedTileArt.tsx`, shared by both `FeedPreviewGrid` and `ShareModal`'s picker, fetches each tile's latest `creative_versions` row and its signed asset URL (the same two hooks the single-creative Content view already uses for its own artwork) and renders the real image or video when one exists, falling back to `PlaceholderArt`'s gradient only when it doesn't. `.pp-art img`/`.pp-art video` got the same tile-scoped `position:absolute;inset:0` treatment the gradient `svg` already had, plus `object-fit:cover` so a real photo of any aspect ratio fills the 4:5 tile the same way the svg's own `preserveAspectRatio="slice"` does.

**A real bug chased through three false leads before finding the actual (non-)issue**: a tile showed blank with no visible image or overlay text. First suspected a data-loading race (fixed by waiting for the `<img>` tag) — wrong, the tag existed before the pixel data did (`naturalWidth: 0, complete: false`, confirmed directly). Then waited for `naturalWidth > 0` too — still blank. Drawing the loaded image to a canvas threw `SecurityError: tainted by cross-origin data`, which only happens when a *real* cross-origin image was actually decoded — proving the image was genuinely loading correctly, and narrowing the problem to the test fixture rather than the component. `sips -g all` on the test PNG confirmed it: `hasAlpha: yes` — a transparent 2×2 test image, invisible against the card's own white background, with white overlay text equally invisible against that same white. Rebuilt the test image as a hand-written, alpha-free truecolor PNG (`hasAlpha: no`) and the tile rendered exactly as expected: solid fill, white title, date pill, all correctly layered. No product code changed as a result of this chase — the implementation was correct throughout; only the test fixture was wrong.

**A real, unrelated storage-cleanup gap found while verifying**: `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s teardown sweeps the `assets` *table* by `agency_id`, but never the underlying *storage* files those rows pointed to — deleting the row doesn't delete the file. Every one of this round's test uploads (and several from earlier in this session) left an orphaned file behind. Checked directly: 19 top-level folders in the `assets` bucket, cross-referenced against the live `agencies` table — 12 had no matching agency at all (pure debris, deleted here, 12 files removed) and 7 matched agencies that still exist under names this session never created (`E2E HeaderSave Agency`, `E2E VisualFinal Agency`, plus the real `No Fluff` agency) — left alone, since those are a separate, pre-existing cleanup debt this round didn't cause and shouldn't guess at. Not fixed in `fixtures.ts` itself this round (out of scope for a UI-polish ask), but worth a follow-up: the teardown should sweep storage alongside the table row.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Grid height measured directly: `484px` (Feed Preview) matching the computed value exactly; ShareModal's own `.pkgrid` at `479px` (subpixel rounding, not a defect — its centering was measured as perfectly symmetric, `84.16px` on both sides). Real-image rendering confirmed three ways: the `<img>`'s resolved `src` is a genuine signed Supabase URL, `naturalWidth`/`complete` confirm real pixel data loaded, and a per-tile screenshot shows a correctly-filled, correctly-labelled tile. A sibling creative with no upload still renders the gradient placeholder in the same grid, confirming the fallback path is intact. Full affected-spec re-run: 13/13 clean. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows; 12 orphaned storage files from this session's own testing found and removed.

## Row-gap bug, real "No Creative"/"No Post" empty states, and a not-live border — four direct asks

**The row gap**: reproduced first rather than guessed at — seeded 6 creatives, measured every tile's exact position, and found an 83px gap between row 1 and row 2 where the CSS asked for `2px`. Root cause: `.feedgrid`/`.pkgrid` both got a fixed `height: 484px` (the prior round's 9-post sizing) that's taller than 2 real rows of content, and with no explicit `align-content`, the browser's default grid alignment spread that leftover space as extra row-gap instead of collecting it below the last row. Fixed with `align-content: start` on both — re-measured, exactly `2px` between every row, including with a full 9-tile (3-real, 6-empty) grid afterward.

**Every grid slot now shows something, never blank space**: `FeedTileArt` (both `FeedPreviewGrid` and `ShareModal`'s picker) drops the decorative gradient `PlaceholderArt` fallback it had before, in favour of a plain "No Post" label (creative name + the label, flat neutral background) for a real creative with nothing uploaded. A new sibling, `EmptyTileArt`, fills the padding slots beyond the real creative count (up to the fixed 9) with "No Creative". `CreativePreviewPopover`'s own `PlaceholderArt` is untouched — this was the only caller that ever used it for this purpose.

**A real regression this round, caught by the full suite rather than assumed away**: the empty-slot padding tiles were first given both `.feedgrid-tile`/`.pktile` (for their sizing) and a `-empty` modifier. `share-eligibility.spec.ts`'s partial-exclusion test loops every `.pktile` on the reasonable assumption that each one is a real, selectable post — the padding tiles silently joined that loop, and the test's click sequence stopped selecting what it expected to. Fixed by giving `.feedgrid-tile-empty`/`.pktile-empty` their own standalone copy of the sizing rules instead of sharing the real-tile class at all, so nothing querying `.feedgrid-tile`/`.pktile` for "a real creative tile" — this test, or any future code — has to know padding tiles exist.

**A light, pastel border for posts that aren't live yet**: `creatives.published_at` is real schema already returned by `useCreatives` — `!published_at` gets a soft `rgba(255, 138, 0, 0.35)` 1px indicator (a toned-down version of the existing `--amber` hue, not the full-strength colour, since this is a status hint rather than a warning). `.feedgrid-tile` had no competing use for `border`, so it takes one directly; `.pktile`'s own `border` is already the selection indicator (2px, transparent/`--action`), so its not-live signal uses `outline` instead, since a tile can be both selected and not-live at once. Empty slots (`.pktile-empty`/`.feedgrid-tile-empty`) never get it — there's no post there to be not-live about.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout, including after the regression fix. Row spacing re-measured at exactly `2px` across all three rows of a full 9-tile grid, both grids. A combined scenario (one real published creative, one real unpublished creative, six empty slots) confirmed every label and border by assertion, not just by eye: 6 `.feedgrid-tile-empty` elements reading "No Creative", the unpublished creative's tile reading "No Post" with the `not-live` class present, the published creative's tile without it — then screenshotted to confirm the same visually. `share-eligibility.spec.ts` re-run clean after the class fix, plus the full affected-spec suite: 13/13. Two more orphaned storage folders from this round's own real-image testing found and removed the same way as the prior round's. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Icon centering, more room above the picker, and a real caption-on-hover for both feeds

Three direct asks.

**`.ig-acts` icons re-centred**: the prior round's own fix for "more space below the icons" made the padding asymmetric (`9px` top / `15px` bottom) — correct total space, but it put the icons visibly closer to the top of their own row than the bottom. Split evenly to `12px` all round (same `24px` total vertical padding as before, so the space-before-the-caption ask from that round still holds), which reads as centred and moves the icons down a few pixels, exactly as asked.

**More room above ShareModal's Posts card**: the gap came from the shared `.msection-d`'s own `8px margin-bottom` — used by every section description in this modal, not just this one, so widening it there would have moved every other section too. Added `margin-top: 12px` to `.pkcard` specifically instead, scoped to the one card that needed it.

**A real caption-on-hover for both grids**, reusing rather than rebuilding: `CreativePreviewPopover` (the calendar table's own hover-preview card) already fetches `useCopyVersions` and shows the latest caption — "just the latest caption" the user asked for was already exactly its behaviour, it just didn't show the version number alongside it. Added `V{version_no}` (matching `CopyVersionHistoryPopover`'s own `V{n}` labelling elsewhere in this app) using `.pp-cap b`, a rule that already existed in `globals.css` but had no caller until now. `FeedPreviewGrid` and `ShareModal` each got their own copy of `ProjectCalendarTable`'s exact hover-state pattern (a short delay before showing and before hiding, `handleEnter`/`scheduleHide`/`cancelHide`, the popover's own `onMouseEnter` cancelling the hide so the pointer can cross onto it) — matching this codebase's own established convention of each table hand-rolling this locally rather than sharing one hook, not introduced as a new abstraction. The popover itself (`position: fixed`) renders as a sibling outside `.feedcard`/`.pkcard`'s own `overflow: hidden`, the same placement `ProjectCalendarTable` uses outside its scrolling table — confirmed empirically rather than assumed, including inside `ShareModal`'s own modal dialog, since a modal's one-shot opening animation can (but here does not, once settled) trap fixed-position descendants.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. `.ig-acts` icon measured at `12px`/`12px` top/bottom (was `9px`/`15px`). ShareModal's Posts card measured at exactly `12px` below its own description text. Hover popover confirmed by assertion on both grids with two seeded copy versions: `V2` shown, the newest caption's text present, the older version's text absent — then screenshotted on Feed Preview to confirm the same visually (fully un-clipped, correctly positioned, inside the review page); ShareModal's own assertions passed identically, confirming the popover works inside a modal too. Full affected-spec re-run, including `project-calendar.spec.ts` since `CreativePreviewPopover` itself changed: 26/26 clean. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows; two more orphaned storage files from this round's own testing found and removed.

## A caption-only hover, and fixing the popover that hid behind the modal

Two corrections to the previous round's hover popover, both direct.

**"Just the caption, like the table"**: the previous round's popover was `CreativePreviewPopover`'s full card (stage tag, comment count, placeholder art, like/comment icons, published-time line) with a caption line added at the bottom — not actually "the caption hover on the table" being asked for, which is `CopyVersionHistoryPopover`'s own plain `V{n}` + text row (`.copypop`/`.cp-row`), used for the calendar's Post Copy/Image on Text cells. New `FeedCaptionPopover.tsx` fetches one creative's `useCopyVersions` and hands its single latest row straight to `CopyVersionHistoryPopover` (`label="Caption"`) rather than rebuilding that shell — both `FeedPreviewGrid` and `ShareModal` swapped to it in place of `CreativePreviewPopover`. `CreativePreviewPopover` itself is untouched and still backs the calendar table/grid's own richer hover card, which was never part of this ask.

**The modal stacking bug**: root-caused rather than guessed at — `.scrim` (the modal backdrop) sits at `z-index: 100`; `.copypop` (the shell this new popover uses) sat at `z-index: 90`, below it. On the plain Feed Preview page there's no competing `.scrim` in the tree, so the previous round's own verification passed there and never actually exercised this path inside a modal despite its "confirmed... inside a modal too" note above — that note is retracted; only DOM presence was checked then, not visual stacking. Fixed by raising `.copypop` to `z-index: 110`, above `.scrim`. `.evpop` (`CreativePreviewPopover`'s own shell) was left alone — every one of its callers is a plain calendar table/grid, never inside a modal.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Feed Preview's hover popover screenshotted showing only the `V{n}` + caption row (no stage tag, no icons, no art — `.pp-acts` count asserted at `0`). ShareModal's hover popover screenshotted mid-hover with the modal open, confirmed visually on top of the dialog, and its computed `z-index` asserted greater than `.scrim`'s. Full affected-spec re-run: 25/26 clean on the first pass; the one failure (`creative-review.spec.ts`'s "upload artwork modal" screenshot, an unrelated AI-model-label button whose text depends on an async settings fetch) reproduced clean in isolation immediately after — the same environmental-timing signature documented earlier in this file, not a regression from this round's CSS/component changes. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows; no orphaned storage files from this round (no new uploads seeded).

## Gating Share For Review to Client Review, both at the toolbar button and inside the picker

Direct instruction, in two parts, after a clarifying question surfaced a real conflict: the toolbar's Share button used to open from any creative regardless of stage (by design — a "zero eligible" warning banner covered the case rather than blocking), and "Multiple Posts" let you pick any creative in the project and warned about the ones still below Client Review rather than preventing the pick. Asked directly which way to resolve that: hard-block the toolbar button, and additionally make ineligible tiles non-selectable within "Multiple Posts" too, rather than picked-with-a-warning.

**The toolbar button**: `disabled={creative.stage !== 3}` on the Share icon in the creative review header, with its `title` switching to "Only available in Client Review" — reuses the existing `.tool:disabled` greyed styling already applied elsewhere in this toolbar, nothing new added.

**The "Multiple Posts" grid**: each `PieceTile` now takes `stage >= 3` (the same "eligible" threshold `resolveShareEligibility` already used for its warning banner, matching `shared_link_allowed_creative_ids`' own rule — Approved counts as eligible too, not just Client Review, since a client can already see an Approved post) as `selectable`, and a new `ineligible` prop drives a genuine `disabled` attribute plus a new `.pktile.ineligible` style (`opacity: 0.35`, matching `.tool:disabled`'s own value) and an explanatory `title`. `resolveShareEligibility` and its warning banner (`.note.warn`/`.note`) are untouched, not removed — with both gates in place the banner can no longer fire through ordinary use, but it still catches the one real edge case it can still reach: a piece's stage regressing after it's already picked, while the modal stays open.

**Two existing tests exercised scenarios the UI no longer allows, both rewritten rather than deleted**: `share-eligibility.spec.ts`'s "warns when a scope resolves to zero eligible creatives" opened Share from a Concept-stage creative, which the button no longer permits — replaced with a test asserting the button's disabled/enabled state directly. Its "reports a partial exclusion" test clicked every tile including an ineligible one, expecting it to be picked-with-a-warning — replaced with a test asserting the ineligible tile is genuinely disabled and unclickable, and that a normal, all-eligible link still creates cleanly with no warning. `stage-transition.spec.ts`'s "ShareModal's eligibility warning clears once a creative moves to Client Review" had the same problem (its first half opened Share from a stage-1 creative) — rewritten to assert the button is disabled beforehand and becomes enabled, reactively, the moment the stage pill advances to Client Review without a page reload.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Screenshotted both states directly: the toolbar button visibly faint/disabled on a Concept-stage creative (confirmed its real bounding box and computed `opacity: 0.35`, since it's easy to miss by eye against the light canvas background), and an ineligible tile visibly greyed inside "Multiple Posts" next to a normal, selected, eligible one. Full affected-spec re-run across all three touched files: `share-eligibility.spec.ts` 2/2, `stage-transition.spec.ts` 5/5, `creative-review.spec.ts` 6/7 clean with the one failure (the same pre-existing "upload artwork modal" AI-label timing flake) reproducing clean in isolation. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows; no new storage uploads this round.

## A real duplicate-post bug fixed, "New Brief" renamed to "New Post", and checkbox delete/restore for posts

Two direct reports: repeated "Save Brief" clicks were inserting a new post every time instead of amending the one just created, and posts needed a bulk checkbox-select-and-delete affordance (with a monday.com screenshot as the reference for the select box itself).

**The duplicate-post bug, root-caused rather than patched over**: `CreativeModal`'s save handler branched on `isCreate` — a flag fixed at mount from `props.mode` that stays `true` for as long as the modal is open in create mode — instead of "has this brief been created yet." Every click called `createCreative.mutateAsync` again. Fixed by branching on `isCreate && !creativeId` instead. Two more hooks were bound to the same stale assumption: `useUpdateBrief`/`useSaveSlideText` were called with `isCreate ? "" : props.creative.id` — a permanently-empty id in create mode, so even with the branch fixed, a second save couldn't have amended the right row. Rebound both to the real `creativeId` once it exists. A third, related bug this surfaced: the modal's own `initialSlideText`/`latestCopyVersion` (used to decide whether a second save needs to cut a new copy-version row at all) come from `props.copyVersions`, itself fixed at `[]` for a create-mode modal's whole lifetime — so a second save would have kept re-inserting the same slide text as a redundant version. Fixed by tracking the actually-persisted slide text and version number locally (`savedSlideText`/`savedCopyVersionNo`) once creation succeeds, mirroring `useCreateCreative`'s own trim-and-drop-blank-lines rule so the local baseline matches what's really on record. `useSaveSlideText`'s `latest` parameter was narrowed from the full `CopyVersionRow` to a `Pick<..., "version_no" | "fields">` — the only two fields it ever reads — so this local, id-less stand-in can be passed without fabricating fields it doesn't have.

**"+ New Brief" → "+ New Post"**, everywhere it appeared as a label: the project page's own button, the `CreativeModal`'s title (both create-mode states), and the two calendar tables' empty-state hint text. Every e2e spec asserting on the old button text (`brief-time-warning`, `new-brief`, `continuous-calendar`, `project-calendar`) updated to match; test titles and fixture data strings (e.g. `#nbName` values) left alone since they're not user-facing UI text.

**Checkbox select-and-delete, reusing the archive convention this app already has for clients and projects**: `creatives.archived_at` already existed and `useCreatives` already filtered it out — "delete" here means the same thing it already means elsewhere, fully reversible, not a hard delete. `useCreatives` now returns archived rows too (same shape change `useProjects` already made for its own Active/Archived toggle), with `FeedPreviewGrid`/`ShareModal` filtering them back out client-side, since a deleted post is never a real feed slot or shareable piece. New `useArchiveCreatives` bulk-updates `archived_at` for a whole selection in one `.in()` query. The project page grew an Active/Archived chip toggle (identical markup to the client workspace page's own), filtering which half of `creatives` each calendar table receives. Both `ProjectCalendarTable` and `ContinuousCalendarTable` grew a staff-only sticky checkbox column — deliberately kept out of `FROZEN_COLUMNS`/the Columns picker's counts, since it's a selection affordance, not a data column — and a `.selectionbar` (count + Cancel + Delete/Restore, label and mutation direction driven by which half of the toggle the caller is currently viewing) that appears once 1+ rows are checked, table views only (not the Calendar grid mode).

**A real regression caught by the full suite, not assumed away**: `continuous-calendar.spec.ts`'s "Funnel and Type chips persist" hardcoded `td` indices (`nth(3)`/`nth(5)`) to reach specific columns — the new checkbox `<td>`, prepended ahead of every other column for staff, shifted every one of them by one. Reproduced twice in isolation with the exact same failure before concluding it was real rather than the session's usual memory-pressure flakiness, then fixed by updating the indices (and the comment documenting them) to account for the new column.

**A separate, pre-existing gap caught while re-verifying, unrelated to this round's own work**: `shared-review-public.spec.ts`'s two screenshot baselines had been stale since an earlier round *this same session* re-padded `.ig-acts` (the icon row, shared by `DesktopReview.tsx`/`MobileReview.tsx`, the public review page's own components) for the "vertically centre the icons" ask — that round's own affected-spec list never included this file, so the baselines never caught up. Confirmed by tracing the diffs to the same class before regenerating either one. Retracting that earlier round's implicit claim of full coverage: it verified the change on the authenticated Feed Preview/Share screens only, not the public one, which happens to share the same CSS class.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Duplicate-post fix confirmed directly: filled and submitted "Create Brief," then clicked "Save Brief" three more times, then queried `creatives` by name via the service-role client — exactly one row. Checkbox flow confirmed end to end: selected a post, "1 selected" bar appeared, Delete removed it from the Active view, switching to Archived showed it with a Restore action, Restore returned it to Active — screenshotted at both the plain-table and selected states for a visual check against the reference screenshot's own select-box styling. Full re-run of every touched spec file after both fixes: 50/51 clean, the one failure being `dashboard.png`'s own documented, expected Last Activity date-rollover diff (left untouched, per the existing note in this file), not a regression. Two screenshot baselines regenerated after confirming their diffs were the intended, already-shipped icon change, not caused by this round; one regenerated for `project-calendar.png` itself, since the new Active/Archived toggle chips are a real, deliberate change to that page. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## A brief-only post stays editable, and Update gets its own "Post updated" confirmation

Two direct reports. First: a post created with no creative/copy yet had no way back in — the toolbar's Edit button only rendered once `activeCreativeVersion` existed, and `BriefPanel` itself has no edit affordance of its own, so a brief-only post landing on its own Brief or Checks section left genuinely nothing clickable to change it. Second: once "Create Brief" (now "Create Post") had run once, every later save still said "Save Brief" with no feedback that anything actually happened.

**Edit, kept visible**: dropped the `&& activeCreativeVersion` half of its gate in the creative review page's toolbar — it's now `isStaff` only, matching the stage-switcher pills and the Share icon, both of which already rendered regardless of whether artwork exists. Still opens on the Content tab as before; the modal's own tab bar gets from there to Brief.

**"Create Post" and "Update"**, renamed from "Create Brief"/"Save Brief" — the first save (an insert) reads differently from every one after it (an amend), so the two now say so. Every e2e spec clicking the old label text updated to match.

**"Post updated."**, beside Cancel, after any non-create save succeeds: a new `showUpdatedNote` flag, set at the end of `handleSaveBrief`'s update branch and auto-cleared after 3 seconds via a `setTimeout` (ref-tracked so a second save in quick succession restarts the clock rather than stacking timers). Reuses `.bsaved` — the same green "Saved" styling `handleSaveCopyOnly` already uses lower in this same modal — rather than adding a new class. Timeout-based rather than cleared on every field's `onChange` (that convention's own reason for clearing-on-edit is a specific version number a later edit could make stale; this message names no version, so a plain few-second flash is the simpler, still-honest choice).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Confirmed end to end: created a brief-only post (no creative, no copy), opened its review page, and screenshotted the toolbar showing the stage pills, the Share icon, and Edit all present together. Clicked Edit, switched to the Brief tab, changed the concept, clicked Update, and screenshotted "Post updated." rendered in green beside Cancel. Full affected-spec re-run: `creative-review.spec.ts` caught a real, expected diff from the Edit-button change itself (the fixture's own default creative has no uploaded artwork, so its toolbar screenshot genuinely gained an Edit button) — confirmed intended before regenerating that one baseline; every other touched file (`new-brief`, `project-calendar`, `continuous-calendar`, `brief-time-warning`, `stage-transition`) passed clean. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. Found and removed orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows left over from this session's own earlier memory-pressure-related test timeouts.

## A toolbar separator, a real preview for the latest creative version, and permanent delete alongside Archive

Three direct asks.

**A separator between Archived and + New Post**: a plain `.toolsep` (the same divider already used everywhere else in this app's toolbars), staff-only since both the toggle and the button either side of it are.

**The Edit modal now shows the latest creative version the same way it already showed older ones**: previously, viewing an older version rendered a real `CreativeVersionPreview` (image/video, filename, upload date), but the *latest* one — the common case — just showed a line of text ("Current: filename · uploaded date") above an empty drag-and-drop zone, no visual at all. `CreativeVersionPreview` now renders for the latest version too whenever one exists and nothing new has been picked yet, with a plain "Upload New Version" button underneath rather than the dropzone being the first thing shown. The dropzone itself is unchanged and still appears — for the true empty state (no version at all yet), and once a replacement file has actually been picked and is awaiting "Save Version N."

**Archive and Permanently Delete, both offered when posts are checked**: previously the selection bar's single button read "Delete" (really an `archived_at` flip, fully reversible) — now that a real, irreversible delete sits next to it, that button is relabelled "Archive" (still "Restore" in the Archived view) so the two don't read as two shades of the same thing. "Permanently Delete" opens a confirmation dialog (new `PermanentDeleteConfirm`, built on the existing `Modal`) naming the count and stating plainly that comments, versions and share links go with it and it can't be undone — nothing fires until that's confirmed.

**This one needed real schema, unlike the archive toggle**: `creatives` has no delete RLS policy, and comments/copy_versions/creative_versions/shared_links all reference `creative_id` with no `on delete cascade` — a hard `DELETE FROM creatives` would just fail on the first foreign key. Worse, comments and copy_versions reference *each other* (`comments.copy_version_id`/`creative_version_id` one way, `copy_versions.source_comment_id` the other), so neither table can be cleared first without breaking the other's still-live reference. New `supabase/migrations/phase21_delete_creatives_permanently.sql` adds a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, `delete_creatives_permanently(p_creative_ids uuid[])` — staff-checked via `is_agency_staff`, same shape as `advance_creative_stage` — that nulls both cross-references out first, then deletes comments, copy_versions, creative_versions, matching shared_links, and finally the creatives rows themselves, all as one call. New `useDeleteCreativesPermanently` hook calls it via `.rpc()`, bulk-first like `useArchiveCreatives` (the only entry point is the same checkbox selection). Underlying `assets`/storage objects are deliberately left alone, same as everywhere else in this codebase — closing that gap is separate, already-documented work, not part of this ask.

**Not yet applied** — this is real schema, so per this project's own migration workflow it needs to go in by hand: paste `supabase/migrations/phase21_delete_creatives_permanently.sql` into the Supabase dashboard's SQL editor and run it, then say so, so the RPC's real behaviour can be verified through the service-role client before this note is closed out. Until then, "Permanently Delete" is wired up and its confirmation dialog is fully functional, but confirming will fail (`function delete_creatives_permanently does not exist`) until the migration lands.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Screenshotted the toolbar separator directly. Uploaded a real file through the redesigned Content tab end to end — confirmed the version tab, the real `<img>` (via `naturalWidth`/`complete`, not just a DOM tag), the "Upload New Version" button, and "Saved as version 1." all present together. Confirmed the selection bar shows Cancel/Archive/Permanently Delete together, and that the confirmation dialog renders with the right copy and count (a first, non-full-page screenshot attempt caught a transitional frame before the modal had painted — re-verified with a full-page capture and a settle delay, which showed it correctly; not a real bug, just an artifact of that one screenshot call). Did not attempt an actual permanent delete, since the RPC it depends on doesn't exist in the database yet. Full affected-spec re-run: 6/6 touched files clean once separated from two transient dev-server drops mid-run (this machine's own memory pressure, documented elsewhere in this file) — one real, expected diff caught and confirmed (`project-calendar.png`, the new separator shifting the Active/Archived/New Post row) and regenerated. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Checkbox alignment, "Scheduled"/"Continuous" renamed and de-badged, a real calendar date picker, and per-client project folders

Four direct asks in one round, the last two — folders and the two delete migrations above — needing real schema.

**Checkbox vertical alignment**: the sticky selection checkbox sat a few pixels above where a row's own text actually starts, even with the same padding/`vertical-align` as every other cell — a native checkbox's own rendered box just sits slightly higher than a line of text by default. Measured directly (checkbox top vs. an adjacent cell's own text), then nudged with `margin-top: 3px` on `.tbl td.skchk input[type="checkbox"]` until the two aligned — confirmed by screenshot, not just by the numbers.

**"Scheduled"/"Continuous" → "Content Planner"/"Other Content"**, everywhere the words appeared as a label: `KBadge.tsx` (the shared delivery-type chip, used on client workspace project rows), the New Project modal's own two option labels, and the project page's own sub-header text. The toolbar badge chip on each calendar table (`.kbadge.sch`/`<KBadge delivery="continuous">`, sitting next to the Columns button) was removed outright rather than relabelled, per direct instruction — `ContinuousCalendarTable.tsx`'s now-unused `KBadge` import went with it. The underlying `delivery: "scheduled" | "continuous"` database values are untouched — this is display text only, same convention as the "New Brief" → "New Post" rename earlier in this session. Every e2e spec asserting on the old label text or the toolbar badge updated to match.

**A real calendar date picker for Publish Date/Time**, matching a supplied reference screenshot: new `components/ui/DateTimePicker.tsx`, a calendar-icon button beside the existing native `<input type="date">`/`<input type="time">` fields (both left completely alone — same ids, same direct fill-ability for every existing e2e spec) that opens a `.colpop`-shell popover with a Today button, its own date/time inputs, month/year dropdowns with prev/next arrows, and a Mon–Sun day grid built from `getMonthGridDays` (the same month-grid utility `ProjectCalendarTable`'s own Calendar view already uses). One real bug caught before shipping: the month `<select>`'s `flex:1` was being starved down to ~26px because the year `<select>` sitting next to it — styled by the same global `select{appearance:none;...}` rule this app gives every select — resolved an oversized `auto` intrinsic width (~182px, confirmed by direct measurement) that soaked up nearly all the flex-distributed space; fixed with explicit widths for both instead of relying on flex auto-sizing to resolve it.

**Per-client project folders on the All Projects screen** — one folder per project (not tags), assigned via the row's own "..." menu, unfiled projects staying in the flat list above the folders, all per direct answers to a clarifying question asked before building. New table `project_folders` (migration `phase22_project_folders.sql`): scoped to one client, RLS-matched to how `clients`/`projects` already scope (staff see every folder in the agency, client-side users see only their own client's), `agency_id` derived from `client_id` by reusing the existing `set_agency_id_from_client` trigger rather than writing a new one. `projects.folder_id` (nullable, `on delete set null`) plus a validation trigger (`check_project_folder_client`) blocking a project from being filed under another client's folder. New hooks (`use-project-folders`, `use-create-project-folder`, `use-rename-project-folder`, `use-delete-project-folder`, `use-move-project-to-folder`) and two new modals (`FolderModal` for create/rename, mirroring `CreativeModal`'s own create/edit duality; `MoveToFolderModal`, a plain radio list with "No folder" always first). Folder sections render as a collapsible band inside the same `.clients` list — same idea as the calendar tables' own `.wkband` week divider, open by default, one shared column-header row at the top rather than repeating it per folder.

**A real deployment mistake, caught by the user in production rather than by this session's own testing**: `hooks/use-projects.ts` added `folder_id` to its `select()` the moment the migration was written, before confirming that migration had actually been applied. Requesting a column that doesn't exist fails the *entire* query in Postgres/Supabase, not just that field — every project list broke, for staff and clients alike, the moment this shipped, and the user reported it directly ("my projects are not loading") before this session caught it independently. Fixed immediately by reverting just that one column out of the select (with a comment marking where to add it back), verified the fix restored loading, and only re-added it once both this migration and the earlier permanent-delete one were confirmed applied through the service-role client. A second, smaller version of the same lesson followed right after: `project_folders.created_by` is `not null`, but the create-folder hook never set it, surfacing only once real folder creation was attempted against the live, migrated database (`useCreateCreative`'s own `supabase.auth.getUser()` pattern was the fix). A third gap in the same vein: the new `project_folders` table was missing from `tests/e2e/fixtures.ts`'s FK-ordered teardown sweep, causing real cleanup failures (caught directly, not assumed) the first time a spec actually wrote to it — added in the correct position (before `clients`/`users`, after nothing, since `projects.folder_id` needs no ordering against it thanks to `on delete set null`).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean throughout. Both migrations confirmed applied via the service-role client (`project_folders` table, `projects.folder_id` column, and `delete_creatives_permanently` all present) before any code was re-pointed at them. End to end against the real, migrated database: created a folder, moved a project into it, confirmed it showed inside (open by default), renamed the folder, deleted it, and confirmed the project returned to the flat unfiled list — all in one real Playwright run, not asserted piecemeal. Permanently deleted a real post and confirmed it was gone from both the Active and Archived views, not just archived.
