Full regression re-run across every touched file (`client-workspace`, `new-project`, `creative-review`, `project-calendar`, `continuous-calendar`, `new-brief`, `brief-time-warning`, `stage-transition`, `share-eligibility`): 43/43 clean, after regenerating two screenshot baselines whose diffs were confirmed as this round's own intended changes (`client-workspace.png` — the Active/Archived toggle, Add Folder button, and "Content Planner" label; `project-calendar.png` — the removed "Scheduled" toolbar badge) and re-verifying the one pre-existing async-label flake (`upload-artwork-modal.png`) clean in isolation. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. Found and removed one orphaned `E2E Test Agency` row left over from the teardown gap above, using the now-fixed sweep order by hand.

## Trimming the All Projects header and toolbar to what's actually used

Three direct asks, all scoped to the client workspace page.

**The All/Needs Review/Approved pills, removed**: this was the `filter` state (`pendingFor`-based: any open approval or feedback vs. none), a separate axis from the Active/Archived toggle next to it. Removed the state, the type, and the three buttons; `pendingFor` itself stays — it still backs the "Most Pending" sort option, which wasn't part of this ask.

**Header stats replaced with the same three stage buckets the table already shows per project**: "Campaigns" (an approximation — distinct project `type` values standing in for a concept this schema never had a real column for) and "Waiting on Client"/"Approved" (narrower bands that could read differently from the table's own per-project numbers below them) are gone. In their place: Concepts, Internal Review, Client Review — each just `byStage[0]`/`[1]`/`[2]` summed across every active project, the exact same real, schema-backed counts the table's own Concept/Internal Review/Client Review columns already display per row, just totalled. Live Projects is unchanged. The client-preview-mode-specific wording ("Waiting on You" vs "Waiting on Client") went with the stat it belonged to — the new cards read the same for staff and clients, matching the table columns they mirror.

**Header title font sizes**: "Concept"/"Internal Review"/"Client Review"/"Latest Approved" all render at 13px via the shared `.ago` class, but "Project" — the one header cell without that class — was inheriting `.crow.head`'s own 12px instead, a pixel smaller than its neighbours. Given an inline `font-size: 13px` to match, without pulling in `.ago`'s other properties (right-align, muted colour) that wouldn't suit the leftmost column.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Confirmed directly: the three pills are gone from the DOM, the new stat cards show the same numbers as the table's own stage columns for the fixture project (0/0/1, matching exactly), and computed `font-size` on all five header titles reads `13px` uniformly (previously 12px on "Project" alone). Full regression re-run across every file this page's changes could touch (`client-workspace`, `new-project`, `manage-clients-projects`): 12/12 clean, including one regenerated baseline (`client-workspace.png`) after confirming its diff was exactly this round's own toolbar/header changes. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Creating a folder inline from Move to Folder

Direct instruction: if the folder a project needs doesn't exist yet, `MoveToFolderModal` shouldn't send staff away to Add Folder and back — it should offer to create one right there. A "+ New Folder" link (`.badd`, the same style `ListEditor`'s own "+ Add" already uses) below the radio list swaps in a plain name field with Cancel/Create; creating calls the same `useCreateProjectFolder` the main Add Folder button uses, and the new folder is auto-selected in the radio list the moment it exists — one less step than creating it, closing this modal, reopening it, and picking it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Confirmed end to end: opened Move to Folder on a project with no folders yet, created one inline, watched it appear in the radio list already selected, then moved the project into it and confirmed the new folder's own section showed the project — all without leaving the modal until the move itself. Affected regression re-run (`client-workspace`, `manage-clients-projects`): 8/8 clean. Unit suite 66/66. `npm run build` clean. No orphaned `E2E Test Agency` rows.

## Four staff roles and per-client access for Users (schema and permissions only)

Direct instruction: agency staff are Primary Owner, Owner, Admin or User; the Primary Owner decides who gets in and at what role; and each User can be limited to specific clients. This round is the database half only — no invite email, accept-invite page or Team screen yet (those are the next two pieces, not started).

**Roles**: `agency_role` gains `primary_owner` and `owner` (`phase23a`, its own paste — Postgres won't let a new enum value be used in the same transaction that adds it). Owner is Admin plus more, per direct instruction, so `is_agency_admin()` itself now covers all three top roles rather than every Admin-gated policy being switched to a new helper. The "plus more" today is one thing: only an Owner or the Primary Owner can add, change or remove an Owner. Nobody can grant Primary Owner through a normal insert or update — only `transfer_primary_ownership()`, callable by the current Primary Owner alone, which demotes them to Owner and promotes the target in one go. A unique index allows at most one Primary Owner per agency, a trigger refuses any update that would leave zero, and the Primary Owner's own membership row can't be edited or deleted by anyone else. `finance` stays in the enum unused.

**Per-client access**: Primary Owner, Owner and Admin still see every client. A User sees only the clients listed for them in the new `staff_client_access` table, which only the top three roles can read or change. Every client-scoped table's policies were rewritten around two helpers — `visible_client_ids()` for reads client-side members are also allowed, `staff_visible_client_ids()` for everything that was staff-only before. `delete_creatives_permanently()` and `advance_creative_stage()`, which bypass RLS, check the same thing themselves. Existing staff are all Admins, so nobody lost access.

**Three follow-up migrations, and why**: the first cut (`phase23b`) failed to apply (a function referenced a table created later in the file) and, once applied, had real bugs only a real-session test exposed — it gave client-side members staff-only writes (projects, creatives, folders, shared links) and private comments, because the one helper it used also returns a client member's own client; it hid unlinked assets from staff, which broke creative uploads; it left the Primary Owner's membership deletable by an Owner; and its `clients_select` looked the new row up under the statement's own starting snapshot, so New Client failed even for an Admin. `phase23c` and `phase23d` fix each of those. No client-side logins existed in the live database while `phase23b` alone was applied, so nothing was exposed.

**Known gaps**:
- Every existing agency has no Primary Owner yet — the role didn't exist before, and `transfer_primary_ownership()` needs one to already exist. Each needs a one-off assignment through the service-role client; not guessed at here.
- Asset rows (file metadata) remain visible agency-wide to any staff member, a restricted User included. They carry no client of their own, and the storage bucket was already agency-wide for staff (`phase8_storage.sql`), so narrowing only the metadata would add nothing real.
- Creating a new client is limited to Primary Owner/Owner/Admin — previously any staff member. A judgement call: there's nothing to scope a brand-new client against, and it reads as an admin action.
- `supabase/seed.sql` doesn't include any of phase23 (it already lagged behind phases 20–22).

**Verified**: a throwaway script against the real database, with a real signed-in session per role (Primary Owner, Owner, Admin, a User granted one client, a User granted none, a client-side member) and data seeded as the app would — 63/63 checks. Covered: each role sees exactly the right clients, projects, creatives and comments (private included only for staff with access); a User can work in their granted client but can't read, edit, create in, move stages in or permanently delete from an ungranted one, can't read or grant themselves access, and can't create a client; granting and revoking access takes effect immediately; client-side members can still comment but can't create or edit anything or see shared links; Admin can add Users but not Owners, can't promote to Owner, can't touch an Owner or the Primary Owner; nobody can grant Primary Owner directly; the Primary Owner can't step down except by transfer, only they can transfer, and the index blocks a second one even for the service role; staff can upload an asset and read it back; an Admin can create a client and read it back. No test rows left behind. Full Playwright suite 76/78 after all four migrations: `settings-knowledge.png` (known flake) and `upload artwork modal`, which touches the asset path changed here, so it was re-run alone and then three more times, passing every time. The one earlier real failure, New Client (2 specs), is what `phase23d` fixes; both pass now. `dashboard.png` regenerated after confirming its diff was only the Last Activity date. Unit suite clean.

## Inviting team members by email, and member management narrowed to Owners

Direct instruction: the Primary Owner decides who gets in and at what role, the way Claude's own invite flow works — an account is created, an email goes out, the person clicks the link and sets a password. Also directly decided: only an Owner or the Primary Owner can invite or manage members, not an Admin.

**Who can do what** (`phase24_invites.sql`): adding, changing or removing a membership, and granting or revoking a User's client access, now need Owner or Primary Owner. Admin keeps everything else it had (agency settings, every client). The Team page only shows "Invite Member" to Owners and the Primary Owner; the route and the database both refuse anyone else independently.

**The invite**: the prototype's own Invite Team Member modal (Email Address, Role, "Invite link expires in 48 hours", Send Invite), with the role list replaced by this schema's real invitable roles — Owner, Admin, User — each with a one-line hint. Choosing User adds a Client Access checklist (the prototype's `.cpr`/`.cbx` rows) — an addition, since the prototype has no per-client access at all. Sending creates the account with no password if the email is new, adds the membership as Invited through the inviter's own session (so the database's rules, not the route, decide who may invite whom), and emails a single-use link. Inviting someone who's still pending sends a fresh link and cancels the old one — the only way to re-send until the Team management screen exists.

**The link**: our own token, not Supabase's built-in invite link — Supabase's can't last longer than 24 hours, the prototype says 48, and this keeps the email itself in the codebase (`lib/email/invite-email.ts`). Only a sha256 hash is stored (`invites.token_hash`); the raw token exists only in the email. Email goes through Resend's API directly (`lib/email/send-email.ts`, no new package). Addresses on the reserved `.invalid` domain are never handed to Resend, so the e2e suite can invite freely without generating bounces.

**Accepting** (`/invite/[token]`, public): a new account enters a name and a password (8+ characters) and is signed straight in. Someone who already uses Frank just accepts and signs in as usual. Expired, unknown and already-used links each get their own message rather than a form.

**Failures are loud**: if the email doesn't send, the modal says the invite was created but the email didn't go, with the reason — never "Invite sent".

**Also fixed**: `useMyAgency` and `useMyMembership` now ignore memberships that haven't been accepted — an existing Frank user invited to a second agency would otherwise have been dropped into it before accepting.

**Known gaps**:
- Email isn't configured yet — `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` aren't in `.env.local`. Until they are, every invite to a real address is created but reports that its email didn't send.
- No Team management yet: changing someone's role, editing a User's clients, revoking a pending invite, removing a member, transferring Primary Owner. Next phase.
- No "forgot password" — someone who loses theirs has no way back in yet. Worth doing alongside Team management.
- The Team list shows a pending person's name as their email's first half until they accept and choose one.
- The prototype's permissions matrix (per-role toggles, "Save Permissions") and seat count aren't built — this schema has fixed roles, not configurable permissions.
- No Primary Owner exists at any agency yet. Raj (raj@nofluff.in) will be invited through this flow directly as No Fluff's Primary Owner once email is configured.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, unit suite (66/66) and `npm run build` clean. A new permanent spec, `team-invite.spec.ts` (5 tests), drives the real UI: an Owner invites a User with one client through the modal, and the database shows a pending membership, the one client grant, and an invite expiring in 48 hours; inviting the same person again leaves exactly one live link, a different one; an Admin sees no Invite Member button and the route answers 403; a new person accepts with a name and password, lands signed in on the dashboard, and the same link then says it's been used; someone with an existing account accepts without a password; expired and made-up links show their own message with no form. Separately, with real signed-in sessions against the database: an Admin can no longer add or remove members or grant client access but still edits clients; an Owner can do all three; nobody can create an invite row in someone else's name. Looked at the real screens (Team page, modal as User and as Owner, the email-failed state, the accept form at desktop and phone width, the expired page) against the prototype's own modal; fixed the email showing twice on the accept form and a left-aligned "Go to sign in". Full Playwright suite 81/83: `settings-knowledge.png` (known flake) and `upload-artwork-modal.png`, whose only difference is the "Draft with Claude…" button label, which loads after the modal opens — nothing to do with this change, and it passes on its own. No test accounts or rows left behind.

## Deactivating, removing and revoking team members

Direct instruction: an Owner or the Primary Owner can turn off or remove someone's access. Also directly decided: removing someone takes them out of the agency but not off their past work, and a person whose access is off is told so plainly when they try to get in.

**On the Team page**, Owners and the Primary Owner get the same "⋯" row menu clients and projects use. An active member: Deactivate, or Remove from Team (with a confirm). A deactivated member: Reactivate, or Remove. Someone who hasn't accepted yet: Revoke Invite, which kills their link. There's no menu on the Primary Owner's row, on your own row, or for Admins at all — the database refuses those anyway (phase23c/24), the menu just doesn't offer them. Deactivated members stay listed with a rose "Deactivated" tag; removed ones drop off the list. The list is now ordered by seniority (Primary Owner, Owner, Admin, User) rather than the role type's internal order, which had the Primary Owner near the bottom.

**Remove is a flag, not a delete** (`phase25_member_removal.sql`, new `memberships.removed_permanently_at`). The membership row is what ties a person to an agency, so deleting it would have made their name disappear from every comment and approval they'd written. Found by the new e2e spec, not by design: deactivating someone blanked their row on the Team page, because `users_select` only showed people with an active membership — the same rule was quietly hiding former members' names on their comments. That policy now shows anyone who has ever been in the caller's agency. Revoking a never-accepted invite still deletes the row outright; there's no work to attribute.

**Signing in without access**: a deactivated or removed person who signs in — or who was already signed in and loads a page — is sent to `/access-removed` ("Your access to {agency} on Frank has been turned off. Contact an Owner…"), which signs them out. The check is in the authenticated layout, so it runs on every full page load. Inviting someone who was deactivated is refused with a pointer to Reactivate instead, rather than creating a second membership next to the old one.

**Known gaps**:
- A deactivated person already inside the app isn't bounced until their next full page load; moving between screens inside the app doesn't re-run the check (RLS still shows them nothing in the meantime).
- The login account itself is never deleted, by decision. A revoked invitee's never-used account lingers too; inviting the same address later reuses it.
- A permanently removed person can be invited back, which creates a fresh membership; their old, flagged one stays as history.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New permanent spec `team-manage.spec.ts` (6 tests), all through the real UI: deactivate, then that member signs in and lands on the access page naming the agency, is genuinely signed out, and after Reactivate signs in to the dashboard; a member already signed in is bounced on their next reload once deactivated; Remove asks first (Cancel keeps them), then drops the row, flags the membership, clears their client grants, and their name still reads back through a remaining member's real session; Revoke Invite removes a pending member and their link then says it isn't valid; no menu on the Primary Owner or yourself, and none for an Admin; re-inviting a deactivated person returns the Reactivate message. `team-invite.spec.ts` (5) still passes. Looked at the Team list with every status, both menus, the confirm and the access page. Full Playwright suite 86/89: `settings-team.png` changed as intended (the new menu column shifts Email/Role/Status left) and was regenerated after looking at the diff; `settings-knowledge.png` and `upload-artwork-modal.png` are the same flakes as the last two runs. Unit suite 66/66, `npm run build` clean. No test accounts left behind.

## Team page: a Clients column, editing a member's role and clients, and a By Client view

Four direct asks on the Team page.

**The "..." menu in the same place as Clients and Projects**: the last column is now the same 70px, right-aligned slot the dashboard and client workspace use. Measured, not eyeballed: the button's left edge is at x=1214 (19px wide) on all three pages at 1280px.

**A Clients column after Email**: a User lists the clients they've been given, alphabetically ("No clients" if none); the Primary Owner, Owners and Admins show "All clients", since that's what they see. Client grants are only readable by Admin and above (RLS), so the column — and the By Client view — are only offered to them; a User looking at the Team page sees the plain list without it rather than a column of wrong "No clients".

**Edit**: a new first item in the row menu (active and invited members) opens Edit {name} — Role, and for a User the same Client Access checklist the invite modal uses (now one shared `ClientChecklist`). Save stays disabled until something changes. Changing a User to Admin or Owner clears their client grants, since those roles see every client and the rows would only go stale. Role editing wasn't in the ask; it's in the same modal because Edit with only half of what defines a member's access read as incomplete, and the database already enforced who may set which role (Owner tier, never Primary Owner).

**By Person / By Client**: the dashboard's own Active/Archived chip pair, with Invite Member moved beside it the way New Client sits on the dashboard. By Client is read-only: each active client with the Users who can see it (pending ones marked "(invited)") and a count, under a line explaining that the Primary Owner, Owners and Admins see every client and so aren't repeated on every row. The prototype has no equivalent of either view.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Two new tests in `team-manage.spec.ts`: an Owner opens Edit, sees the current client ticked and Save disabled, swaps it for another client, and the row, the database and the By Client view all follow (and By Client shows no menus); then changes the role to Admin, the checklist disappears, the row reads Admin / All clients and the grants are gone. An Admin sees the Clients column and By Client with the right names but no Invite Member and no menus. All 13 Team tests pass. Looked at By Person, the Edit modal and By Client with a realistic mix of roles and grants. `settings-team.png` regenerated after looking at it — the new header row and Clients column. Full Playwright suite 89/91: `settings-knowledge.png` (known flake) and the project calendar's hover-preview timing test, on a page this didn't touch, which passed 3/3 on its own. Unit suite 66/66, `npm run build` clean.

## The sort dropdown on All Projects, removed

Direct instruction: the Deadline / Name A–Z / Most Pending dropdown on a client's All Projects toolbar isn't needed. Removed along with its state. The list keeps the order it always defaulted to — by deadline, no deadline last, the prototype's own default — so nothing moves for anyone. `pendingFor`, kept last round only because it backed "Most Pending", is gone with it. The toolbar separator now only shows for staff, since it only ever divided the chips from staff-only buttons once the dropdown was gone. The `select.sort` style stays — both calendar tables still use it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Looked at the toolbar (Active / Archived, separator, Add Folder, New Project). `client-workspace.png` regenerated after confirming its diff was only the toolbar shifting into the dropdown's space. `client-workspace`, `manage-clients-projects`, `new-project`: 12/12. `npm run build` clean.

## A project's Type can be changed after it's created

Direct instruction: the project edit modal should let you change the project type. The row menu's "Rename" is now "Edit", opening Edit Project (`EditProjectModal`, formerly `RenameProjectModal`; `useUpdateProject`, formerly `useRenameProject`) with the name and the same Type select New Project uses. Its options follow the project's delivery kind — Social Media / Paid Campaign / Launch / Other for a Content Planner project, Amazon / Web / Email / Print and Events / Other for Other Content.

Delivery kind itself (Content Planner vs Other Content) isn't offered: the database refuses to change it once a project has any posts (`projects_delivery_immutable`, "duplicate the project instead"), and offering a choice that fails for nearly every real project would be worse than not offering it. A project with no type, or one outside today's list, keeps it as a selectable option, so saving the name alone never rewrites the type.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. `manage-clients-projects.spec.ts`'s project test now edits rather than renames: the fixture's typeless project opens on "—" with exactly the Content Planner options, saves a new name and "Paid Campaign", the row shows it, and reopening shows it selected with "—" gone. Checked by hand that an Other Content project gets its own list. `manage-clients-projects` + `client-workspace`: 8/8. `npm run build` clean.

## Calendar specs no longer break when the month rolls over

Not a product change. Every calendar spec reached its March 2027 test data by clicking Next exactly six times from whatever month it opened on — written in September, so on 1 October all 21 started landing on April and failing ("0 scheduled posts in this view"). They now call a shared `goToMonth(page, "March 2027")` fixture helper that clicks until the month header actually reads that month. The plain `project-calendar.png` screenshot opens on the current month too, so its month label and the empty state's sentence naming the month are masked rather than re-baselined every month.

**Verified**: both calendar specs 22/22 on their own (the hover-preview timing test, which also failed in a loaded full run, passed 3/3 alone). `project-calendar.png` regenerated after confirming its diff was only the month name; `dashboard.png` regenerated after confirming its diff was only the Last Activity date.

## A way to sign out

There wasn't one anywhere in the app — the prototype's avatar (`#me`) only jumped to Settings. Clicking the avatar at the foot of the rail now opens a small account menu beside it (the same `.colpop`/`.cpr` shell the row menus use, placed with `useViewportFit`'s "beside" side): your name, your email, and Sign out. Signing out ends the Supabase session, clears every cached query so nothing from that session is shown to whoever signs in next, and lands on `/login`. The avatar is now a real `<button>` labelled "Your account", with the browser's button chrome reset in `globals.css`.

Also: Next.js's own development indicator (the "N" button) defaults to the bottom-left corner, directly on top of the avatar, which made it unclickable in `next dev`. Moved to bottom-right in `next.config.ts` (`devIndicators.position`). Production never shows that indicator.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New `sign-out.spec.ts`: the menu names the signed-in person and their email, closes on Escape, and Sign out lands on `/login` — and going back to `/dashboard` afterwards is sent to sign in again, so the session really ended. Looked at the open menu: beside the avatar, fully on screen.

**Screenshots no longer include Next.js's dev overlay**: moving the dev indicator changed every baseline that had captured it, which exposed why `settings-knowledge.png` had been a "known flake" for so long — its baseline had captured the indicator's intermittent "1 Issue" badge, so it only matched on runs where that badge happened to be showing. `tests/e2e/screenshot.css` (applied to every `toHaveScreenshot` via `playwright.config.ts`'s `stylePath`) now hides `nextjs-portal`, which only exists under `next dev`. Ten baselines regenerated after a pixel check confirmed every difference sat in the two bottom corners where the indicator had been (and, for `settings-knowledge`, its wider badge). All 33 screenshot specs then passed twice in a row, `settings-knowledge` included.

## Profiles: first and last name in title case, designation, bio and photo

Direct instruction: someone accepting an invite gives a first and last name, a designation and optionally a short bio and photo; names and designation must never be stored in lowercase. Also decided directly: first name, last name and designation are required, photo and bio optional; the photo shows on the rail avatar and account menu, the Team page and next to comments; and everyone gets a Your Profile page to change these later.

**Schema** (`phase26_user_profiles.sql`, `phase26b_title_case_apostrophes.sql`): `users` gains `first_name`, `last_name`, `designation` and `bio` (300 characters, enforced by a check constraint). `users.name` stays the one display name every screen reads, rebuilt from first + last by a trigger. Existing names were split on their first space ("Raj Rana" → Raj / Rana). Photos are ordinary `assets` rows behind the existing `users.avatar_asset_id`, stored at `{agency}/avatars/{user}/…` so the existing bucket policies apply; `assets_select` gained one branch so a client-side member can load a staff member's photo beside their comments.

**Title case is enforced by the database**, not just the form: `users_normalise_profile` runs on every insert and update, so the service-role invite route and the Your Profile page's own session can't bypass it. The rule: an all-lowercase word gets a capital at its start and after any non-letter ("raj" → "Raj", "o'neil" → "O'Neil", "smith-jones" → "Smith-Jones"); a word that already has capitals keeps them and only has its first letter raised ("CEO", "UX", "McDonald"); small joining words stay lowercase mid-phrase ("Head of Design"). Flattening ALL-CAPS words was considered and rejected — it would turn "UX Designer" into "Ux Designer". The form applies the same rule (`lib/title-case.ts`) when a field loses focus, so nobody is surprised after saving. 26b exists because the first version used Postgres's `initcap()`, which doesn't capitalise after an apostrophe — caught by writing "o'neil" through the real trigger, which came back "O'neil".

**The invite page** asks for photo, first name, last name, designation, bio and password. The photo can only be uploaded once they're signed in (storage needs a staff session), so it goes up right after the account is created; if that one step fails they're told so, signed in anyway, and pointed to Your Profile — never shown a false success.

**Your Profile** (`/profile`, from the account menu): the same fields plus Change/Remove photo. The account menu now shows the person's designation, and their photo replaces the initials on the rail.

**Known gaps**:
- A typed-in-lowercase acronym can't be told from a word: "ux designer" becomes "Ux Designer". Type it as "UX" and it's kept.
- Old photos are unlinked, not deleted, when replaced or removed — the file and its `assets` row stay.
- Pending invitees who were invited before this change have their email's first half as their "first name" until they accept and fill the form in properly.
- Storage files uploaded by e2e specs aren't swept by the fixture teardown (it deletes rows only); the two photo specs remove their own file.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; `lib/title-case.test.ts` (6 cases). Against the real database through the trigger: lowercase, ALL-CAPS, mixed-case, hyphenated and multi-space input each stored as intended, and a 301-character bio rejected. `team-invite.spec.ts`'s accept test now types every field in lowercase, sees each title-case on blur, attaches a photo, and checks the stored row (names, designation, bio) plus the photo's asset row, storage path and the file itself. New `profile.spec.ts`: Your Profile from the account menu, an empty first name refused, lowercase input saved title-cased (row and menu both); a photo uploaded there appears on the rail, the Team page and on a comment — including for a client-side member, checked as a genuinely loaded image, not just an `<img>` — and Remove brings the initials back. Looked at the invite form at desktop and phone width (fixed a name field spilling out of the card and the photo circle squashing) and the Your Profile page.

## Profile photos are cropped to the head automatically

Direct instruction: when a photo is uploaded, find the face and keep just the head. Also decided directly: do it with Google's MediaPipe face detector (a new dependency, `@mediapipe/tasks-vision`), chosen over Chrome's built-in FaceDetector (no Safari or Firefox) and over Claude vision (a per-upload AI cost against the agency cap).

**How it works**: picking a photo opens "Position your photo" — the picture under a circular window, already centred on the detected face. The square kept is 1.7x the detector's face box (roughly brow-to-chin), lifted slightly for hair: the head, not head-and-shoulders. Drag to move, slide to zoom, Use photo. Only that square is uploaded, resized to 512 px and re-encoded as JPEG (~50-60 KB), however large the original — so originals up to 25 MB are now accepted, since phone photos routinely exceed the old 5 MB limit. With several faces, the biggest is the subject. With none found, or if the detector can't load, it starts from the largest centred square and says so; nothing is blocked. Same cropper on the invite page and Your Profile, since both go through `PhotoPicker`.

**Loading**: MediaPipe is imported only when someone opens the cropper, never bundled into ordinary pages. Its WebAssembly runtime (11.7 MB uncompressed, two variants for SIMD and older browsers, ~3-4 MB over the wire) is copied from `node_modules` into `public/mediapipe/wasm/` by a `postinstall` script (`scripts/copy-mediapipe-wasm.mjs`) and git-ignored, so it's served same-origin at exactly the version `package-lock.json` pins, without 23 MB of binaries in the repo; Vercel's own install step produces it. The 230 KB face model (`blaze_face_short_range.tflite`, Google's official float16 build) is committed. Correction to the estimate given before building: the runtime is 11.7 MB on disk, not "about 3 MB" — that's only the compressed transfer size.

**Console noise**: MediaPipe's runtime logs routine start-up lines ("INFO: Created TensorFlow Lite XNNPACK delegate for CPU.") through `console.error`, which Next's dev overlay counts as an Issue. Only lines in MediaPipe's two log formats are filtered, only while it runs (`quietMediaPipe` in `lib/face-crop.ts`).

**Known gaps**:
- Detection quality is MediaPipe's short-range model: tuned for faces within a couple of metres of the camera, which suits headshots; a tiny face in a group or landscape shot may not be found (falls back to the centred square).
- HEIC photos (iPhone's default) aren't accepted — browsers outside Safari can't decode them. People see "Use a JPG, PNG or WebP image".

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; `lib/face-crop-math.test.ts` (7 cases: no face, centred face, faces at each edge, a face filling the frame, clamping, zoom keeping its centre, picking the biggest of several). By hand in a real browser with MediaPipe's own sample portrait (waist-up, face a small part of the frame): face found in about 2 s including the first download, the crop opened on it, and the stored file was a 512x512 JPEG of just the head — looked at both. No console errors and no dev Issue badge once filtered. Permanent specs: the photo steps in `team-invite.spec.ts` and `profile.spec.ts` now go through the cropper with a faceless image (the fallback message shows, zoom and drag both move the picture, and what's stored is a JPEG — for a client the loaded photo measures 512 px, the crop, not the 600x400 original); both specs passed twice in a row. Caught along the way: a picture smaller than 32 px had a zoom minimum larger than itself, so zoom did nothing — now capped at the picture's own size. `npm run build` clean; MediaPipe lands in its own lazily-loaded chunk.

**Fix after going live — the detector couldn't load signed out**: checking the live deploy showed every `/mediapipe/*` file answering 307 → `/login`. The auth gate's matcher only skips image extensions, so `.wasm`/`.tflite`/`.js` under `public/` were treated as protected routes. Signed-in people (Your Profile) were unaffected, but an invitee on the accept page has no session yet, so their cropper silently fell back to a centred square — invisible to the specs, which use a faceless image on purpose and so can't tell "no face" from "detector never loaded". `/mediapipe` is now in `PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES` (static files only, nothing private). **Verified**: a new `team-invite.spec.ts` test fetches all five files with no session and expects 200 with real content (the same check against the pre-fix live site gets 307s); and by hand, a signed-out invitee uploading MediaPipe's sample portrait now gets "Centred on your face".

## Table headers styled like the Knowledge section headers

Direct instruction: the column header rows on Clients (dashboard), a client's Projects list, and both Team views use the same look as the Knowledge page's section headers (`.panel-h`) — white background, 14.5px semibold, ink — instead of the prototype's grey 12px muted band. Done once on `.crow.head`, which is used by exactly those four header rows and nowhere else; right-aligned stat columns (`.ago`) inherit it, and the Projects "Project" header lost the inline 13px it had been given to match its neighbours. A deliberate difference from the prototype.

At the larger size "Internal Review" and "Client Review" now wrap to two lines on the Projects list, as "Latest Approved" already did — left that way so every column, including the row menu, stays exactly where it was.

**Verified**: computed style on every header cell across the four tables reads 14.5px / 600; looked at each against the Knowledge page. `dashboard.png`, `client-workspace.png` and `settings-team.png` regenerated after a pixel check confirmed every change sat in the header row and the row it pushes down; those specs plus `team-manage` and `manage-clients-projects` pass.

**And 20px lower**: also by direct instruction, the same tables sit 20px further below the toolbar row above them — `.clients` (used only by these four tables) gained `margin-top: 32px`. 32, not 20: it collapses with the 12px bottom margin of whatever sits above (`.secthead`, or the By Client note), so 32 is what actually adds 20. Measured on all four, the gap went from 12px to 32px; the first attempt at a plain 20px only moved them 8px, caught by measuring rather than eyeballing. The three baselines above were regenerated again after confirming the change began at the table and nothing above it moved.

## All Projects: the count moves into the Active/Archived chips

Direct instruction: the number of projects shows in the chips — "Active (3)", "Archived (1)" — and no longer beside the "All Projects" title. Each chip counts its whole tab; the old title count followed the search box, which a tab count shouldn't, so a search can show fewer rows than the chip says. Folder sections keep their own counts.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. `manage-clients-projects.spec.ts`'s project test now checks the chips read "Active (1)" / "Archived (0)", then "Active (0)" / "Archived (1)" once the project is archived. `client-workspace.png` regenerated after confirming the only change was the title-and-chips row; `client-workspace` + `manage-clients-projects` pass.

## The rail follows where you are inside a client

Direct instruction, replacing the prototype's `syncRail()` (Calendar, Analytics and Visibility whenever a client is open):

- a client's projects list — Clients, Knowledge, Settings
- a project's calendar table — Clients, **Projects**, Knowledge, Settings
- a post's review page — Clients, Projects, **Calendar**, Knowledge, Settings

Projects goes back to that client's project list; Calendar opens the post's own project table. Knowledge now also shows on the table and review pages (before, the rail only knew the client from a `/clients/…` URL, so it disappeared once you opened a project): the client and project come from `useProject`/`useCreative`, the same cached queries those pages already load. Settings stays staff-only. The Projects icon is a folder drawn to match the rail's other icons — the prototype has no folder glyph anywhere. `AnalyticsIcon` and `VisibilityIcon` were removed as unused.

**Known gap**: `/calendar`, `/analytics` and `/visibility` (placeholder pages) still exist and render if visited by URL, but nothing links to them any more.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New `nav-rail.spec.ts`: the exact rail — each link's label and destination, in order — on all three pages for staff, then Calendar → the project table → Projects → the client's list by clicking through; and a client-side member's rail on a review page (the same, without Settings). Passed twice in a row. Looked at the rail on a review page. `creative-review`, `upload-artwork-modal`, `client-workspace` and `project-calendar` baselines regenerated after a pixel check confirmed every difference was inside the 84px rail. In a slow full run (7.3 min) six other specs timed out; each passed on its own.

**Flaky under load (not new, not this change)**: `project-calendar.spec.ts`'s "hover preview holds open while the pointer moves onto it" fails intermittently inside a full parallel run — it depends on pointer-move timing against the popover's hide delay — and passed 5/5 run on its own. Worth giving it a wider timing margin if it keeps showing up.

**Correction**: the first version of this change overwrote the existing `nav-rail.spec.ts` without reading it, dropping two checks still worth having — the rail outside a client (now: just Clients and Settings, and no Calendar/Analytics/Visibility link anywhere) and a client-side member on their own projects list (Knowledge, no Settings). Both restored in the new form; the spec is now 4 tests, passed twice.

## Moving a project to another client; client images and descriptions; Projects status; Clients counts

Five direct asks.

**Move to client** (row menu on a client's Projects list): pick another active client; the project lands at its top level, out of any folder. The folder it left stays, even if empty, for the user to delete. Per direct decision, its Share for Review links are revoked in the same step — they were sent to the previous client's contacts. All of it is one database call, `move_project_to_client()` (`phase27_client_profile_and_project_move.sql`), a SECURITY INVOKER function so `projects_update`'s RLS decides who may move what — checked against the old client and the new one — and the function adds only what RLS can't: same agency, an active target, an atomic revoke, and a readable message when the target already has a project of that name. Someone who can't see the target client gets "client not found", since RLS hides it from them entirely.

**Empty folders stay visible**: building this surfaced that the Projects page hid its whole table — folders included — whenever no project rows matched, so moving a client's last project out made its now-empty folder invisible and undeletable. On the Active tab with no search, the table now shows whenever the client has a folder.

**Client profile image** (New/Edit Client modal): per direct decision, square images only — within 1%, so a 1080×1081 export still counts and the sliver is trimmed evenly — resized to 512px, PNG kept as PNG for transparency, anything else JPEG. A non-square image is refused the moment it's picked ("Use a square image — this one is 600×400"), not at Save. Stored like profile photos (`{agency}/client-logos/{client}/…`, `clients.logo_asset_id`, which already existed) and shown in the Clients list in place of the initials tile.

**Client description**: new `clients.description` (500 characters), a Description box in the same modal, shown under the client's name at the top of its Projects page in place of the generic "Pick a project to open its calendar." when set.

**Projects Status column**: Active/Archived, the same tag and 128px column the Clients list uses, between Client Review and Latest Approved. **Clients counts**: moved into the chips — "Active (n)" / "Archived (n)" — and off the title, as on Projects.

**Known gaps**:
- Replacing or removing a client image unlinks the old file rather than deleting it (same as profile photos).
- Unexplained, not reproduced: twice, in the first run after a change, saving the Edit Client modal with a new image was refused with "new row violates row-level security policy". It showed as an error and the modal stayed open (nothing claimed success); 14 later attempts, 8 of them in parallel, all succeeded, and no failing request was captured. Recorded rather than guessed at.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. The move function through real sessions against the real database, 14 checks: a client-side member, a User without access to the target, an archived target, a name clash (readable message, project unmoved) and moving to its own client are all refused; an Admin's move lands at the new client's top level in the same agency, revokes the share link (the public review RPC then returns not_found), and leaves the old folder. New `client-profile.spec.ts`: chip counts on the Clients list; a 600×400 image refused, a 400×400 one saved (stored as a 512px PNG at the right path, shown in the list), the description saved and shown on the client page, and Remove clearing it; a project moved through the dialog — confirmation shown, gone from the old client, the emptied folder still listed, folder and share link checked in the database, present under the new client. Looked at the modal, the list and the client page. `dashboard.png` and `client-workspace.png` regenerated after confirming the only changes were the chips row and the new Status column. The two longest photo specs got a 60s timeout after timing out in a slow parallel run (6.9 min); full suite otherwise 96 passed, and the four photo/team specs then passed together, 18/18.


**Client images now go through the cropper too** (follow-up): per direct instruction, the client image works like a profile photo — any image opens the same drag-and-zoom cropper (`PhotoCropModal` with face detection off, and a rounded-square guide matching the client tile rather than a circle), and only the chosen 512px square is kept. That replaces the earlier square-only rule, which the cropper makes unnecessary; PNG stays PNG so a logo keeps its transparency. Two fixes this needed, since it's the first dialog opened on top of another: the cropper renders beside the Edit Client modal rather than inside it (a fixed overlay inside an animating, transformed modal is positioned within it), and `Modal`'s Escape now closes only the topmost dialog — before, Escape in the cropper would also have closed Edit Client and lost its edits. **Verified**: `client-profile.spec.ts` now picks a 600×400 image, sees the cropper's status, presses Escape and checks Edit Client is still open with its typed description intact, then uses the crop and checks a 512px PNG is stored; the image, profile and invite specs passed together twice (10/10). Looked at the cropper over Edit Client. (A stray Prettier run reformatted `ClientModal.tsx` wholesale — the repo has no Prettier config — and was reverted before anything was committed.)

## Branding: the prototype's page, colours that actually apply, and an agency logo

Direct instruction: make Settings > Branding look like the prototype's (Colour Presets, Interface Colours, Status Colours) and let the agency upload its logo with the same cropper as the other images. Also decided directly: include "Save current as preset"; the theme and logo reach clients everywhere, review links included; leave out the prototype's Display Density switch.

**The page** follows the prototype's `renderSet("brand")` markup and its CSS, ported as-is (`.sw`, `.presets`, `.pre`, `.prex`, `.unsaved`, `.swgrid`, `.dots2`, `.logo-up`, `.logo-box`): a Logos panel (Agency Logo only — the client logo lives in Edit Client and your avatar on Your Profile, the prototype's other two rows), Colour Presets (the five built-ins, your own saved ones with ✕, and Save current as preset with its swatch-preview dialog), Interface Colours (7) and Status Colours (4), each a colour picker plus a hex box that only accepts a 6-digit hex, then Save Branding and Reset to Default. An Agency panel with the name field sits on top, kept from the old page. Users see it read-only; Admin and above can change it (the existing RLS).

**Colours now actually apply.** Found while building this: the old page saved two colours that nothing ever read — saving had no visible effect anywhere. `lib/theme.ts` ports the prototype's THEME, PRESETS and `applyTheme()` (the base tokens, the tints derived from them, and `body.lightrail` when the rail is light), and `AgencyTheme` in the app shell applies the saved theme for staff and signed-in client members alike. On the Branding page, edits preview live across the whole app through a draft (`store/theme-draft-store.ts`) with "Unsaved changes" shown; leaving without saving puts the saved theme back. Review links get it too: `/api/shared-review` looks up the link's agency with the service-role client only after `get_shared_review` has validated the token, and returns just the theme and a signed logo URL. The stored theme is read through `normaliseTheme()` — the original column default used `highlight`, not the prototype's `hl`, and had no `private`.

**Agency logo**: same cropper as client images (no face detection, rounded-square guide), saved straight away like Your Profile, stored at `{agency}/agency-logo/…` on `agency_settings.logo_asset_id` (which already existed). It replaces the "F" mark in the rail and on review pages.

**Schema** (`phase28_branding_presets_and_agency_logo.sql`): `agency_settings.custom_presets` (jsonb), and an `assets_select` branch so client-side members can load the agency logo. The fixture teardown now sweeps `agency_settings` (before assets, which its logo references) — no spec had ever saved branding before, so it never needed to.

**Known gaps**:
- Display Density (Compact / Comfortable) left out, by decision.
- Invite emails still don't carry the logo — they'd need a public, non-expiring image URL, not a signed one.
- A first-time visitor can see Frank's default colours for a moment before the agency theme loads (it's applied in the browser once settings arrive).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; `lib/theme.test.ts` (6: the old column default reads as a full theme, malformed values fall back, presets start from the default and are recognised, a hand edit matches none, key order doesn't count as a change). New `branding.spec.ts` (4), passed twice in a row: picking Forest previews it (the computed `--action`), says "Unsaved changes", and leaving without saving restores the default; saving persists the full theme (including `hl` and `private`) and it then applies on the dashboard, for a signed-in client member, and on a review link; a malformed hex is rejected, a hand-typed one previews, a preset can't take a built-in's name, a saved one persists and is recognised, Reset and delete persist too; the logo goes through the cropper, lands at the right path, shows on the rail, loads for a client member (512px) and on a review link, and Remove brings the "F" back; a User sees the page read-only. Caught while testing: an agency with no saved theme wasn't restored after an unsaved preview (now falls back to the default), and comparing saved presets with `JSON.stringify` called them "unsaved" forever because jsonb reorders keys (now compared key-order-insensitively). Looked at the page against the prototype screenshot, and at the light rail. Full suite 101/102 — the one failure the known load-flaky calendar hover test.

## A post can have several formats; Platforms removed; copy-only posts can close

Direct instruction: formats should be multi-choice, the Platforms field (and the table's Platform column) removed — Post Type does that job — and a post needs neither a file nor copy to close. Decided directly: a post with several formats gets every one of their copy fields combined, and formats can be mixed from any content type.

**Formats**: `creatives.formats` (phase29) holds every format in the order chosen; the first is the main one. `creatives.format` stays and a trigger keeps it equal to the first, so anything reading the single format — the shared-review RPC's own data, comment classification, the repeat-issue warning, the Feed Preview's shape — keeps working against the main format. Existing posts were backfilled with their one format. In New/Edit Post, the prototype's Content Type + Format selects are replaced by one Format field that opens a list of every format, grouped by content type, with tick boxes and a search (the table's Display Columns `.colpop`/`.cpr`/`.cbx`); the first ticked is tagged "Main", and the last one can't be unticked. The copy fields are every chosen format's, each once (`copyFieldsFor` in `lib/formats.ts`) — in the Content tab, the Checks panel and the WIIFM note — and Draft writes for all of them, given each format's own direction. Every place a format is named lists them all ("Meta Feed Ad + Instagram Feed"): the table's Post Type, the post page, the hover preview, the Brief panel and the review link (which looks the formats up after the token is validated, as it does branding).

**Still the main format only**: the quick-add Row picks one format (more can be added in Edit Post); comment classification and the brief-time repeat-issue warning look at the main format.

**Platforms removed**: the New/Edit Post picker added in the previous change, the Content Planner's Platform column (and its chip colours), and the draft row's "Set later" cell. `creatives.platforms` is left in the database, no longer read or written. Saved views that listed the column simply skip it.

**Save and Close**: it refused to close a post with no artwork ("Choose a file to upload"), so a copy-only post couldn't be finished. That requirement is gone — copy only, creative only, or both. Also found while checking it: in a brand-new post, copy saved in the same window never counted as saved, so Save and Close stayed disabled anyway; the window now reads the new post's versions once it exists (which also stops a second artwork upload there from reusing version 1).

**Also fixed**: saving an edited post never refreshed the project table's list (`useUpdateBrief` only refreshed the single post), so an edited name, format or date only showed in the table after a reload. It now refreshes the list too.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint and build clean. phase29 checked through the service-role client: all 17 existing posts backfilled with their one format; a scratch post (removed after) confirmed the trigger — an insert with only `format` gets `formats = [format]`, setting `formats` makes `format` the first, an older write changing only `format` swaps the main one and keeps the rest, an unrelated update leaves both alone. Unit tests for the combined label, combined copy fields and the fallback (86/86). The new `new-brief.spec.ts` test: Meta Feed Ad added to the default Instagram Feed, the last format can't be unticked, Escape closes only the list, the stored `format`/`formats`, all six combined copy fields shown once each, copy saved with no artwork then Save and Close closes, and the table's Post Type reads "Meta Feed Ad + Instagram Feed" — this is what found the create-mode Save and Close bug. `brief-time-warning.spec.ts` now picks formats through the list, which showed the "Main" tag leaking into each row's accessible name (now its label only, with "Main format" as a description). Looked at the closed field, the open list and the table. Two baselines regenerated after looking at their diffs: `upload-artwork-modal.png` (the Copy description's new wording) and `project-calendar.png` (Columns 13/13, which shifts the status filter). Full suite 101/105 with those four failures — all fixed, and the five affected specs then passed 24/24.

## New Post → Row: adding a post straight into the table

Direct instruction, matching the prototype's #briefWrap/#briefMore and startDraft(): the project page's "+ New Post" is now a split button — the main part still opens the full window; its caret ("How to add it") offers **Window** or **Row**. Row puts a draft row at the top of the table and the prototype's floating `.editbar` ("New post in {project}" · Cancel · Save) bottom-right. Decided directly: both table kinds.

**Content Planner**: Date and Time, Post Type (every format, grouped by category), Lead, Asset Name (the post's name) and Concept are editable; Status shows "1. Concept" (the copy columns are set from the post once it exists). **Other Content**: Live Date, Creative Name, Placement (the destination, which these posts require) and Concept and Ref; its format starts from the window's default and can be changed in the post. In both, the date starts at today — an Other Content post with no due date is never listed in its table, so one added by row would otherwise vanish on save.

Save goes through the same `useCreateCreative` call as the window, with the same checks in the same order (a name; a publish date for Content Planner; a destination for Other Content), shown in the bar. Afterwards the table jumps to the post's month, the same way the window's create does. The row's cells follow whatever columns are shown, in their current order; if a column the post needs is hidden (Asset Name, or Placement for Other Content), the bar says which to show and Save stays off. Opened from the Calendar grid, it switches to the Month table first (the prototype refused instead). An Other Content project with no posts, which shows an empty state rather than a table, still gets the table while a row is open.

Built from shared pieces: `components/project/draft-post.tsx` (the draft state and save, the bar, and one input per field kind) and `components/ui/SplitButton.tsx` (the prototype's `.split`, with the row menus' `.colpop` shell). `.editbar`, `.eb-t`, `tr.draft` and `.ecell` ported from the prototype as-is.

**Also**: Next's dev-only indicator now sat on top of the bar's Save button, after being moved there to clear the account avatar; it's switched off entirely (`devIndicators: false` — Next still surfaces compile and runtime errors). Four calendar specs found the saved-view "Save as New View" button by the bare `.split` class, which New Post now also uses; they now pick it out by its text.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New `quick-add-row.spec.ts`: on a Content Planner project, Row → Cancel adds nothing; an empty name is refused in the bar; a filled row (date, time, post type, concept) saves, the table moves to March 2027 and lists it, and the stored format, concept, stage 1 and exact date and time are checked; on an empty Other Content project, a missing destination is refused, then a saved row appears with its destination and due date stored. Looked at the open menu and a draft row. `project-calendar.png` regenerated after a pixel check confirmed the only change was the header strip with the wider button. Full suite 105/105.

## A copy-only post shows as a post

Direct instruction: a post with copy but no artwork should still show, and be commentable, as a post on the Review page, with the existing "No artwork yet" message where the image would be. Before, the post page's Content section showed only the empty state until artwork was uploaded, so the copy couldn't be read or commented on in place. Now the post preview (the `.ig` shell) shows whenever there's copy or artwork: with no artwork, its image area holds the same icon and "No artwork yet" message, sized to the main format's shape, with Upload Artwork for staff and "The agency hasn't uploaded the artwork for this post yet." for clients. The line under the caption reads "Copy saved {date}" in place of "Uploaded {date}". The review link shows the same message in place of its old "No preview available" (still used when artwork exists but can't be previewed). A post with neither copy nor artwork keeps the empty state. Pins and regions still need artwork to sit on; caption highlights and general comments work without it.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New `copy-only-post.spec.ts`, passed twice in a row: with no copy, the empty state and no post; after a copy version, the post with "No artwork yet" and Upload Artwork for staff, the caption and "Copy saved", and a staff comment posts; a signed-in client member sees the post and the client message with no button; a review link shows the message and the caption. Looked at the staff, client and review-link screenshots. Full suite 103/106: the known load-flaky calendar hover test (passed on rerun) and the two review-link baselines, `shared-review-phone.png` and `shared-review-desktop.png`, regenerated after their diffs showed only the image area changing from the black "No preview available" box to the "No artwork yet" panel (taller on desktop, at Instagram Feed's 4:5). Those specs then passed 7/7.

## Copy version tabs, deleting versions, and quieter windows

Direct instruction: the Edit window's copy gets the same version tabs as the artwork, and every version can be deleted. **Copy tabs**: V1, V2… above the copy fields; the latest is where new copy is written and saved, an older one shows read only ("An earlier version, read only. New copy builds on the latest"). Saving a version moves to its tab. **Deleting**: hovering a version tab (or focusing it) shows a small rounded-square X flush in its top-right corner, red on its own hover, as asked in place of a "⋯" menu; it opens a confirmation. Decided directly: any agency staff who can upload for the post's client can delete, and only a version with no comments — the dialog says how many and offers only Close; `delete_post_version` (phase30) enforces both server-side. The other versions keep their numbers (deleting V2 of three leaves V1 and V3; the next is V4); deleting the latest frees its number for the next one, which is safe since nothing pointed at it. Deleting the latest copy puts the draft back on the one before it. A deleted artwork's file stays in storage (the same known storage-sweep gap as permanently deleted posts).

**Windows**: per direct instruction, a window with a Cancel button no longer also has the corner X (14 of them). Share for Review (closed with Done) and the knowledge file preview (no buttons) keep it. The three confirmations — delete a version, remove a member, permanently delete posts — now share `ConfirmDialog`: the message is addressed to whoever is signed in by first name ("Raj, Version 1 of the artwork will be gone…"), and the space above its buttons is the same 28px as below the title's separator. Pressing Escape on a version tab's confirmation closes just that.

**Dashboard**: per direct instruction, "{n} active clients." under Clients is replaced by "{First name}, {greeting}" — one of 20 (`lib/greetings.ts`), chosen from the sign-in time so it stays put for that sign-in and changes with the next. The count is still in the Active Clients card.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; `lib/greetings.test.ts` (greetings start lower case with no "!" or "&", one sign-in keeps its greeting while sign-ins vary, the name prefix and its capitalised fallback), 89/89. `dashboard.spec.ts`: the greeting is "E2E, " plus one of the list and survives a reload; `dashboard.png` regenerated after its diff showed only the line under Clients changing, now masked since it varies. `team-manage.spec.ts` checks the removal confirmation leads with the viewer's first name; the team and delete specs pass 8/8. Looked at the tabs, the X and its hover close up, a version confirmation (now on top of the Edit window, which testing showed it had been hidden behind), New Client without its X, and the greeting. phase30 confirmed present. New `version-delete.spec.ts` (3), passed twice: copy tabs open on the latest (editable) and an older one reads only; V1 with a comment gets "E2E, this version has 1 comment…" with only Close; V2 is deleted, the window stays open, the draft returns to V1's caption, and the next save is V2 again; an artwork version uploaded through the window is deleted from its tab, leaving V2 selected; in the database, a client session is refused, a version with a (private) comment is refused, and once that comment is soft-deleted the version deletes and the comment's link is cleared. The test caught a wrong claim of mine — that numbers are never reused — now corrected above. Full suite 108/110: the known load-flaky calendar hover test, and a client-window test that then passed 3/3.

## Carousels: slide count, per-slide text, slide sets and arrows

Direct instruction: a carousel format gets a number of slides, Text on Image opens one field per slide (one field by default otherwise), and the review screens get left and right arrows. Decided directly: a version is the whole set of slides; 2 to 20 slides, with Meta Carousel Ad held to Meta's limit of 10; LinkedIn Carousel (Document) works like the others, with images rather than a PDF.

**Schema (phase31)**: `creatives.slide_count` (2–20) and `creative_version_slides` (version, position, asset), with `agency_id` from the parent version by trigger and RLS mirroring `creative_versions` — read by whoever can read the version, written by staff who can upload for that client. `creative_versions.asset_id` holds the first slide, so thumbnails, the feed and the table show slide 1 unchanged.

**Brief tab**: when any chosen format is a carousel, a **Slides** select (2 up to the tightest limit of the chosen carousel formats) appears under Format, and Text on Image shows exactly that many Slide fields with no add/remove; otherwise it opens with one field and can grow. Text keeps its positions (slide 1 blank, slide 2 written saves as `["", "…"]`); only trailing blanks are dropped, and cutting the count drops the extra slides' text.

**Content tab**: a carousel's artwork is one slot per slide, in the format's shape (`CarouselSlots`). Several files dropped or picked together fill the slots in order; a slot can be replaced, removed or dragged onto another to swap; Save Version N saves the whole set, uploading only new files and carrying the rest over; Discard Changes goes back to the latest version. Older version tabs show their slides read only. A slot left empty keeps its place.

**Post page and review link**: arrows, an "n / N" count and dots over the image (`CarouselNav`) — not in the prototype, which has no carousel viewer. A disabled arrow is hidden. Each post starts on slide 1. On the post page, pins and boxes record their slide (`anchor.slide`; older ones read as slide 1), only that slide's show, numbering continues across slides, and picking a pinned comment moves to its slide. The review link gets each post's slides from its latest version, signed by `/api/shared-review` after the token is validated (as with formats and branding).

**Not done**: the feed preview and the table's hover preview show slide 1 only; LinkedIn's document carousel takes images, not a PDF; guests on the review link comment on the post as a whole, as before (no pins there).

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean (the post page's manual memo became a plain calculation, which the React compiler flagged once it depended on the current slide). phase31 confirmed through the service-role client. New `carousel.spec.ts` (4), passed twice in a row: one Text on Image field and no Slides for Instagram Feed; Instagram Carousel offers 2–20, 3 gives three fixed fields, adding Meta Carousel Ad cuts it to 2–10, and the stored count, formats and `["", "Second slide words"]`; three files fill three slots and save as V1 with slide 1 as the version's asset; replacing slide 2 saves V2 with slides 1 and 3 the same assets and 2 new; on the post page "1 / 3" with no previous arrow, a pin on slide 2 shown only there, no next arrow on 3, and picking the pinned comment returns to slide 2; the review link moves from "1 / 3" to "2 / 3". Looked at the Brief tab, the slots before and after saving, the post page on slide 2 and the phone review link. `creative_version_slides` added to the test teardown before `creative_versions`. `lib/slide-text.test.ts` covers the field rules and the limits (92/92 unit tests). Full suite 113/114: the one failure, an invite test's wait under load, passed 5 times on its own.

## Emptying a carousel, and clients seeing every slide

Direct instruction: removing every slide of a carousel and saving has to be possible — Save Version was disabled with no slides, the save refused an empty set, and the database required every version to have a file. Since a version is the whole set, an empty set now saves as the next version with no images (phase32 makes `creative_versions.asset_id` optional): the post page and review link show "No artwork yet", and the earlier versions, with their comments, stay in the tabs. Deleting the old versions instead was not done — it would lose history, and commented versions can't be deleted.

**Found while checking**: a signed-in client member could only read a carousel's first slide — `assets_select` let a client read a file only as a version's `asset_id` (slide 1). Confirmed with a real client session (slides 2 and 3 came back with no file), which would have broken the post page for them. phase32 adds a branch for carousel slides; the post page also now skips any slide whose file can't be read rather than failing. The review link was unaffected (signed server-side). Also: `get_shared_review`'s asset lookup skips a version with no file and falls back to the previous one, so `/api/shared-review` now clears it when the latest version is empty — otherwise an emptied carousel showed V1 on the review link.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Two new tests in `carousel.spec.ts`: removing all three slides saves V2 with no file and no slides, V1 is still a tab, the post page shows "No artwork yet" with no arrows, and the review link shows the same; a real client session reads every slide's file, and the client's post page moves to slide 2 and shows its image. The spec's 6 tests passed twice in a row. Full suite 116/116.

## Carousel slides that are empty, and images that didn't fit

Reported on live: a 2-slide carousel showed no arrows on the post page. Its V3 held one image, saved as slide 2 with slide 1 empty — most likely from picking two images from slot 2: the first went there, and the second, with no slot after it, was silently dropped. Two fixes. **Upload**: the first image goes where it was dropped or picked, the rest fill the empty slots after it and then any before it, and any that still don't fit are named ("This carousel has 2 slides, so 1 file wasn't added. Raise the slide count on the Brief tab to add more."). **Viewing**: the arrows step through one place per slide (`lib/slide-frames.ts`) — the post's slide count, or more if a version has more — so an empty slide still counts and says "Slide 1 has no image yet." On the post page and the review link (which now also gets each post's slide count from `/api/shared-review`). Per direct instruction the image slides across when an arrow is pressed: the next slide slides in from that side over 0.24s (none with reduced motion). Every slide's file is signed and loaded up front, so the next one shows at once rather than after a blank moment while its link is fetched.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. Two new tests in `carousel.spec.ts`: a 2-slide carousel with only slide 2 shows "1 / 2", "Slide 1 has no image yet." and slide 2's image, on the post page and the review link; two images picked from slide 2 fill both slides, and three onto two slides fill both and name the one left out. The spec's 8 tests passed twice in a row. The slide-in measured with real timing: the image is there at once and moves from about 109px to 2px over 217ms. Full suite 117/118: the one failure, a client seeing a commenter's photo under load, passed 3 times on its own (that read goes through the unchanged avatar branch of the policy).

## Warning before a format change removes the artwork

Direct instruction: changing a post's format in a way its uploaded artwork no longer fits warns that all uploaded creatives will be removed. Decided directly: confirming deletes every artwork version for good — the versions, their slides, the files, and the comments placed on them with their replies (copy is untouched) — and the warning applies to each of: carousel to single or back, image to video or back, a different shape, and fewer carousel slides than were uploaded (`lib/format-change.ts`, comparing the main format; carousel-ness counts any). It shows on Update in the Brief tab only when the post has artwork; Cancel leaves the post as saved. A change that still fits — more slides, adding a second format, Story to Reel (both 9:16, Story can be either) — saves without asking.

**Image or video**: the prototype's catalog doesn't say which formats are video, so this is a reading of it: video is Instagram Reel, LinkedIn Video, YouTube Video, YouTube Short and YouTube Pre-roll; "either" (never conflicts) is the four story formats, Performance Max and Other Creative; text only is Google Search ads, SMS and Push; the rest are images. Shapes are compared only when both are fixed ratios ("varies", "multi" and "—" aren't).

**How it deletes**: `delete_creative_artwork` (phase33, SECURITY DEFINER, the upload permission) removes the versions (slides cascade), the comments on them and their replies (clearing any copy version's `source_comment_id`), and the file rows nothing else uses, and returns those files' paths. `/api/creatives/clear-artwork` runs it as the signed-in person and then removes the files from storage with the service role, since no signed-in role can delete storage objects; the paths come from the function, never the browser. `ConfirmDialog` gained a `details` list for the reasons.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean; `lib/format-change.test.ts` (carousel to Reel gives all three reasons; changes that fit give none; shape, single-to-carousel and fewer slides each alone), 96/96 unit tests. Two new tests in `carousel.spec.ts`: on a 3-slide carousel with a pin and a reply, raising it to 4 slides saves with no warning; Carousel to Reel shows the warning addressed to the viewer with its three reasons; Cancel leaves the format and the version; confirming saves the Reel format, and the version, both comments, the file rows and the storage files are gone, with the Content tab back to an empty upload; a client session is refused by the function. Looked at the warning. Full suite 120/120.

## Reels: 50MB uploads, and comments at a moment in the video

**Upload size**: the app said videos could be 500MB, but the storage bucket stopped at 25MB — a 30MB file was rejected, so most Reels couldn't be uploaded. Decided directly: 50MB, the most the Free plan allows per file (phase34 sets the bucket; the app's check and hint say 50MB; images stay at 25MB). Checked through the service role: 30MB and 49MB store, 51MB is refused. The spec's 500MB needs the Pro plan and resumable uploads — not done.

**Commenting at a moment** (direct instruction): a video gets its own player (`VideoPlayer`) — play/pause, a timeline and the time — not in the prototype, which has no video review. Pausing (or clicking the timeline) sets the moment; the comment box then shows an "At 0:12" chip, on by default, which ties the comment to that moment and version (`anchor: { type: "time", t }`); clicking it posts a general comment instead. A pin or box placed on the paused frame records the moment too (`anchor.t`), and shows only when paused within a quarter-second of it. Every comment made at a moment is a marker on the timeline — blue for pins and boxes, orange for time only; picking a marker goes to that moment, pauses, shows its pin or box and highlights the comment, and picking the comment in the list does the same. Comments show their moment ("0:12", "Pin 1 · 0:12"). The video is capped at 62% of the window's height, sized to the frame so pins stay aligned, so a 9:16 Reel and its timeline fit on screen together.

**Review link** (decided directly: guests too): the same player and markers. Guests can pause and post with the "At 0:12" chip — `submit_shared_comment` (phase34) takes the moment as an optional seventh argument (the six-argument call still works) and ties the comment to the latest version — and each comment's moment is a button that goes to it. Guests still don't place pins or boxes. `/api/shared-review` adds each comment's anchor (the RPC returns text only). A reload after posting re-signs the files; the player keeps the first link it was given, so the video doesn't jump back to 0:00.

**Verified**: typecheck and lint clean. New `video-review.spec.ts` (2), on a real 3-second MP4 recorded by the test browser, passed twice in a row: on the post page, stopping half way gives "At 0:01", the comment and a pin store the moment (within 0.05s) and the version, both are markers, the pin is gone from the frame at the start, its marker returns to its moment with the pin shown and its comment highlighted, and picking the other comment in the list goes to its moment; on the review link, a guest's comment at a moment stores a time anchor and a version, shows "0:01", the video stays put after posting, and its badge goes back to the moment. Looked at the post page with a pin and both comments, and the phone review link. Full suite 121/122: the one failure the `upload-artwork-modal.png` baseline, whose diff showed only the upload hint changing to "up to 50MB" — regenerated, and that spec then passed.

## Empty carousels keep their arrows, and carousel slides can be videos

Reported on live: a 2-slide carousel with nothing uploaded showed no arrows. A carousel now always has one place per slide (`lib/slide-frames.ts`), uploaded or not — on the post page and the review link each empty slide says "Slide N has no artwork yet" ("artwork", since a slide can be a video), and the arrows move between them; an emptied carousel does the same. Found while testing: moving to a slide before the post's artwork had loaded was undone when it arrived (the slide reset on the first version appearing) — it now resets only when switching versions.

**Video slides** (direct instruction: some carousel slides are videos): each video slide is its own player from 0:00, its timeline shows only that slide's comments, and pins, boxes and moments made on it record the slide; picking such a comment from another slide goes to that slide and moment, on both pages; the dots sit above the video's controls. Guests' comments on a video slide record it too — `submit_shared_comment` takes the slide as an optional eighth argument (phase35). Found while testing: the review link draws both its phone and desktop layouts, and the hidden one's video, at 0:00, kept overwriting the shown one's paused moment (it had only worked by luck); now only the shown layout reports it.

**Verified**: typecheck, lint, build and unit tests (96/96) clean. phase35 checked through an anon call before it ran (the slide argument didn't exist) and by the guest test after. New tests: a 2-slide carousel with nothing uploaded moves between "Slide 1/2 has no artwork yet" on the post page and the review link; a carousel of an image and a video — the video slide has its own player, the dots sit above it, a moment comment records slide 2, picking it from slide 1 goes to slide 2 and its moment, and a guest's comment there records slide 2. The emptied-carousel test now expects its three empty slides. Full suite 123/124: the one failure, the Edit Client window slow to close under load, passed twice on its own; the video and carousel specs passed twice.

## Videos compressed in the browser before upload

Direct instruction: uploaded videos were very heavy — the latest Reel was 28.2MB (15 seconds of 1080 × 1920 H.264, about 15 Mbps). Decided directly: compress in the browser before uploading, to 720p (the short side) at about 1.1 Mbps video and 96 kbps AAC audio — roughly 4–5MB for 30 seconds — and keep only the compressed copy (the agency keeps its own master). `lib/compress-video.ts` uses the browser's own encoder through **Mediabunny** (new dependency, MPL-2.0, approved directly; no runtime dependencies of its own; loaded only when a video is uploaded). Both upload paths (a single file and carousel slides) compress first, with "Compressing video 42%…" on the Save button; the result is an MP4 with its index at the front so it starts playing before it has all downloaded. Only ever smaller: a video already 720p or below keeps its size, and if the copy isn't smaller the original is kept.
